# Pacifica commitments ledger

Generated by `commitments/ledger.py report` from `data/ledger.toml`: 365 commitments, 135 parties. Each entry quotes the storyline; `ledger.py check` confirms every quote and its label. Status is the storyline's own label at the quote (CANON = unlabelled base text).

## Maturity schedule

Starts, scheduled renewals and reviews, term ends, endings and lapses at a death.

## Who can refuse or end what

- **Kingdom of Bohemia within the federal monarchy (from 1919):** Joachimsthal uranium concession (renewals pass through the Bohemian Diet)
- **State of California as a member of the Confederation (member government and legislature, from 1856):** Articles of the Confederation of Pacifica (ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change); Article XIV (emergency Protector) (member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal); Protectorship of Elias Thorne (renewal requires the member legislature)
- **The signing First Nations of the Caledonian compacts of 1932 (one id for each nation to be assigned):** The First Nations compacts of Caledonia and the Fraser River salmon clause (a nation may refuse to lease or sign, and the standstill rule then holds on its ground; only the nations of the river can reopen the Fraser clause)
- **China (Qing, then the Republic):** Chinese Customs Inspectorate under Pacifican leadership (China's retained appointment power)
- **The Crown of Pacifica:** Polytechnic charter (chartered estate) (the ruler chooses the chief from three graduate-council nominees; removal only with Senate consent); Inland command at Sacramento (Truce life office) (after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent); Japanese naval party at Pearl Harbor (Truce life office) (after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent); Polytechnic headship at Carquinez (Truce life office) (after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent); Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi) (from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations); 1932 renewal of the Concord without term (the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations)
- **The Confederacy:** Confederate free-womb amendment (the constitutional route requires state consent)
- **Spain:** Spanish law of sale (Philippines, Marianas, Carolines, Palau) (the sale requires a law of the Cortes)
- **Gulf Republic:** Joint guarantee of Cuban independence (intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors)
- **Kingdom and crown of Hawaii:** Puʻuloa Convention (the legislature ratified it narrowly; a lease, not a cession); Articles of the Confederation of Pacifica (ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change); Article XIV (emergency Protector) (member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal (withheld in 1860)); Protectorship of Elias Thorne (renewal requires the member legislature); Confederal ordinances and the Territorial Council (Hawaii's Supreme Court declares the ordinances void; the judgment is never enforced or reversed); Act of Settlement of 1907 (the union is put to a plebiscite of Hawaiian subjects by the restored legislature and the ʻAha Aliʻi); Ka Lōkahi, the council of the crowns (one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes); Puʻuloa tenancy at the Mōʻī's pleasure (held at the Mōʻī's pleasure; renewed at each accession)
- **Japan:** Compact with Tokyo (ratifies each ten-year renewal); Provisional application of the Pacific Concord (Japan's side follows Japanese law and can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent); Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi) (Japan's Emperor ratifies on the Privy Council's conditional advice; its side can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent); Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi) (from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan holds no date at which it may refuse); 1932 renewal of the Concord without term (the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan may raise the alternating executive at any review and cannot bring it about by refusing); The gold cover behind Japan's reserves in San Francisco (neither ratio can be lowered without Japan's consent)
- **Republic of Kamchatka and the Koryak coast:** Kamchatka Company charter (the charter is given with the consent of the Republic of Kamchatka (proposed))
- **Korea:** Kamchatka Company charter (Korean workers' paid contracts require Seoul's consent (proposed arrangements))
- **London bondholders and lenders:** Convention of Querétaro (the bondholders' consent is sought (Hartnell carries the convention to London)); Assumed Mexican debt to the London bondholders (the bondholders' consent to the convention is sought)
- **The compact nations of the Realm (the homelands as a class):** The compact lands under the Constitution of the Realm (a compact changes only with the consent of the nation affected and the Realm)
- **Pacifica (Republic of California 1846; Confederation 1856; Realm 1887):** American naval storehouse at Sausalito (the storehouse is revocable); Chancellor of the Confederation (elected by the equal member delegations of the Confederal Council); Article XIV (emergency Protector) (Council two-thirds vote to appoint; the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures); Protectorship of Elias Thorne (the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures); Compact with Tokyo (ratifies each ten-year renewal); Earned county autonomy (chartered status, receivership and programme sunset) (a suspended board stays suspended up to five years, renewable by the Senate); Earned county autonomy (chartered status, receivership and programme sunset) (county powers, boundaries and grades are organic law, amendable by both chambers without local consent); Gulf compact (conditions of admission) (considers admission only if the Republic adds usable power and freedom of action without adding risk or a veto; may demand a second generation); Joint guarantee of Cuban independence (intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors); Act of Settlement of 1907 (Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty by two-thirds); Ka Lōkahi, the council of the crowns (one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes); 1927 San Francisco rescue credit to Japan (the Senate votes the rescue loan); Provisional application of the Pacific Concord (the Senate refused the treaty; provisional application is illegal in substance until consent); Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi) (two-thirds Senate consent, won in 1928); Kamchatka Company charter (Korean workers' paid contracts require Pacifica's consent (proposed arrangements)); Fuel lease for the Tokyo atomic station (the Works' duty to deliver fresh cores ends if the union is ever dissolved; all irradiated fuel still returns); The Pacifica–Caledonia Association Treaty, its admission clause and the bond refunding (admission is considered only after twenty-five consecutive years of measured outcomes; Pacifica may demand a second generation; admission must add neither risk nor a veto; both peoples must vote to unite); Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge (cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace)
- **The Philippines (dependency, then the Philippine crown):** Philippine crown offer and Calderón's organic law (the Cortes offers and a plebiscite confirms; terms signed before the vote); Ka Lōkahi, the council of the crowns (one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes)
- **Ralston College, San Francisco (1866):** Compact of the Tuolumne (Ralston College inside the wall) (leaves on a year's notice; its people stay bound to the end of the term)
- **The Sultan of Sulu and the datus:** Sulu compacts (Sultan and datus) (the Sultan alone can consent to undo them)
- **Britain:** Baku concession agreement (Anglo-Pacifican, with Azerbaijan) (the seventh-year review: invoked in the spring of 1927 for first call and a British operator, and withdrawn in June); British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty (reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED))
- **The Union (United States):** Joint guarantee of Cuban independence (intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors)
- **Wanapum (Priest Rapids):** White Bluffs compact (the Wanapum and the Columbia station) (ends the lease on a year's notice, with compensation, if the salmon counts fall below the 1919–1920 baseline three years running or the discharge limit is broken; the Works must then cut the piles' output)

## By party

### Pacifica (Republic of California 1846; Confederation 1856; Realm 1887)

- **Founding settlement of the República de California** (constitution, 1846–, §4, CANON)
- **Separate compact with Solano (Sem-Yeto)** (compact, 1846–, §4, CANON)
- **Naval standstill with Stockton** (convention, 1846–, §4, CANON) — owes: PAC → US: American vessels may provision in Californian ports for operations against Mexico; US → PAC: American vessels may not seize Californian ports
- **Cahuenga terms** (convention, 1847–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: Frémont's battalion leaves the country; Kearny's paroled survivors embark at San Diego; amnesty for Bear Flag men who are American citizens; US → PAC: the naval standstill, in writing, until Washington replies; Stockton adheres for the squadron; PAC → US: exchange of prisoners
- **Elias's promise to the Miwok guide** (pact, 1847–, §5, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MIWOK → PAC: the guide keeps the secret of the October 1847 gold find; PAC → MIWOK: Elias's promise that the Republic will protect a homeland for his people when the miners come
- **Convention of Querétaro** (convention, 1847–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MX → PAC: recognition of California; PAC → MX: assumption of about £3 million of Mexican obligations to London bondholders (see C-1847-LONDON-DEBT); PAC → MX: guarantee of Mexican citizens' property and the Church's; PAC → MX: pledge never to cede itself or its territory to any power — can end or refuse: LONDON: the bondholders' consent is sought (Hartnell carries the convention to London)
- **Assumed Mexican debt to the London bondholders** (loan, 1847–1859, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → LONDON: service of about £3 million of Mexican obligations assumed under Querétaro — secured on: customs and prospective mining revenue (later stated as gold royalties and San Francisco customs) — can end or refuse: LONDON: the bondholders' consent to the convention is sought
- **Buchanan–Vallejo Convention** (convention, 1848–1865, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: never to cede, lease or place under protection any part of its territory to a European power; PAC → US: San Francisco, Monterey and San Diego open to American warships; PAC → US: California's ports closed to the enemies of the United States in its wars; PAC → US: return of deserters from American warships; PAC → US: renunciation of the Great Basin pending a boundary commission; US → PAC: claims of 1846 closed by amnesty and a mixed commission
- **Commercial and naval articles of the Buchanan–Vallejo Convention** (convention, 1848–1860, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: most-favored-nation commerce, whalers' privileges and civil guarantees for American residents — renewals: 1860
- **American naval storehouse at Sausalito** (concession, 1848–1861, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: a revocable naval storehouse at Sausalito — renewals: 1860 — can end or refuse: PAC: the storehouse is revocable
- **British (and French) declaration of non-acquisition in California** (pact, 1848–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: UK → PAC: recognition, and a declaration that it will take no part of California under any form; FR → PAC: accession to the declaration (1849)
- **Treaty of Monterey of 1850** (treaty, 1850–, §5, CANON) — owes: US → PAC: eastern boundary: 42nd parallel, 120th meridian to 39°, southeast to the Colorado at 35°N, along the Colorado to the Gila; recognized ports and access to Yuma; PAC → US: abandons claims to the Great Basin; the US retains the inland routes and the Salt Lake country
- **Treaties with the Native nations of California (the compact of protection and pre-emption)** (compact, 1851–, §5, APPROVED, CR-033) — owes: PAC → CANATIONS: homelands reserved to each nation, never sold and leased only through the Republic, which keeps a fee; PAC → CANATIONS: annuities of goods for ceded land, paid from the Land Fund; CANATIONS → PAC: land beyond the homelands sold only to the Republic, the sole lawful buyer; PAC → CANATIONS: the spirits article (CR-076): no spirits enter a homeland except as the nation's council decides; no annuity, price, wage or debt paid in spirits; a trader who breaks it loses licence and bond and answers in the Republic's court on the nation's complaint
- **Monterey constitutional convention of 1852** (constitution, 1852–, §5, CANON)
- **Superintendency of Mines and Public Revenue** (office, 1852–, §5, CANON)
- **The venture licence (no private war abroad; ventures abroad under licence and bond)** (law, 1854–, §6, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **Treaty of Honolulu** (treaty, 1855–, §6, CANON) — owes: HI → PAC: a Californian coaling station at Honolulu; HI → PAC: customs reciprocity; PAC → HI: customs reciprocity
- **Shimoda agreement** (pact, 1855–, §7, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: Californian machinists visit Satsuma's Kagoshima works; PAC → JP: Japanese shipwrights, naval students and artillery specialists train at California's yards
- **First Minister of California** (office, 1855–, §8, CANON)
- **Articles of the Confederation of Pacifica** (compact, 1856–1887, §8, CANON) — owes: CAL → PAC: defense, the navy, customs, currency and external relations to the common institutions; HI → PAC: defense, the navy, customs, currency and external relations to the common institutions; PAC → HI: domestic self-government and residual powers; Hawaiian-language institutions; Honolulu remains the Hawaiian seat; PAC → CAL: domestic self-government and residual powers; English–Spanish guarantees — can end or refuse: CAL: ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change; HI: ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change
- **Chancellor of the Confederation** (office, 1856–1865, §8, CANON) — can end or refuse: PAC: elected by the equal member delegations of the Confederal Council
- **Article XIV (emergency Protector)** (constitution, 1856–, §8, CANON) — can end or refuse: CAL: member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal; HI: member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal (withheld in 1860); PAC: Council two-thirds vote to appoint; the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures
- **Dissolution provision of the Articles** (constitution, 1856–, §8, CANON)
- **The Confederation's note on the Declaration of Paris** (guarantee, 1856–1919, §8, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **The Reserve Bank's charter** (charter, 1857–, §8, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Treaty of Edo** (treaty, 1858–, §7, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: partnership in commerce, shipbuilding, coal, technical exchange and naval training; JP → PAC: technical advisers, training, observers, markets, harbors and resources
- **Carson Convention** (convention, 1860–1865, §8, CANON) — owes: PAC → US: withdrawal of troops; recognition of American sovereignty over Washoe; US → PAC: property guarantees and transit rights
- **Renewal of the 1848 commercial articles at Carson** (convention, 1860–1865, §8, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: the ports, the Sausalito storehouse, commerce and the rights of American residents
- **Protectorship of Elias Thorne** (office, 1861–1865, §9, CANON) — renewals: 1862, 1863, 1864 — can end or refuse: CAL: renewal requires the member legislature; HI: renewal requires the member legislature; PAC: the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures
- **Oregon's civil compact with the occupation command** (compact, c.1861–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: PAC → OR: courts and county governments continue; requisitions are paid and disputed in civilian courts; no one swears allegiance; Oregon's men, ports and revenues serve no military purpose outside Oregon; PAC → OR: from 1862 the customs collected at Astoria and Portland go into a published Oregon account that pays Oregon's courts and county officers
- **The Puget Sound counties' parallel compact** (compact, c.1861–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-059)
- **Association of Deseret: the working articles of 1862 and the Compact of Association of 1863** (compact, 1862–1867, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → DESERET: the Protector's pledge that the Union's law will not be enforced in Deseret by arms; PAC → DESERET: published terms of passage; Deseret's courts and church property left alone; Deseret's wheat bought in gold; PAC → DESERET: the garrison's posts named at the old Camp Floyd, the Bear River crossing and Fort Bridger, none within a day's ride of the city; DESERET → PAC: supply of the road and passage to the interior campaigns; Lot Smith's cavalry and the Nauvoo Legion bound to the common defence of the roads and the valley; PAC → DESERET: a confederal travellers' court for crimes on the roads, extended by the Compact to crimes of any date
- **Walla Walla convention** (convention, 1862–1862, §10, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: PAC → US: the regulars march east with the honours of war by the Mullan Road
- **Lewiston townsite lease** (lease, c.1862–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: NEZPERCE → PAC: the Lewiston townsite, leased through the Confederation
- **War bonds in coin and the agency of 1867** (loan, 1862–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-069) — owes: SANFRANCISCO → PAC: the Republic's war bonds taken in coin from the houses' reserves, brought by Ralston; PAC → SANFRANCISCO: interest on the coin bonds (amount OPEN); PAC → EASTHOUSES: from 1867, the loan the Senate declined, lent privately through Ralston's bank as the Republic's agent, which takes a share of its own
- **Confederate renunciation of Arizona and the Sunset articles** (settlement, 1863–, §10, PROPOSED) — owes: CSA → PAC: renunciation of the Confederate Arizona claim; no Confederate soldier enters New Mexico in arms; CSA → PAC: Texas gives up its old claim to New Mexico east of the river, by a resolution of its legislature at Austin; PAC → CSA: credit and the future railway connection; PAC → CSA: use of the Sunset road, but no right to march on it; Texan freight changes to New Mexican carts at El Paso
- **Matamoros supply route** (pact, 1863–, §11, APPROVED, CR-022) — owes: PAC → CSA: credit, coin and specialist goods (rifles, machine tools, fuzes, primers and mine gear) and men, around Cape Horn to neutral Matamoros and overland into Texas; no bulk
- **Fugitive agreement with Richmond** (compact, 1863–1889, §13, CANON) — owes: PAC → CSA: fugitives refused at the crossings of the Texas line, by travel-paper controls extended in 1871–1872 to the railway and the Gulf ports; Texas may police its own side
- **Box Elder compact** (compact, 1863–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → NWSHOSHONE: annuities paid from the road's own fees; Cache Valley's grazing paid for through Deseret; NWSHOSHONE → PAC: Shoshone scouts on the Snake road; the killers given up to the travellers' court by their own people, those of 1862 among them
- **Washakie's terms for passage at Fort Bridger** (pact, 1863–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060)
- **Supply compacts of the Montana camps** (compact, c.1863–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → MTCAMPS: freight at published rates, Pacifican coin, assay and licensed courts
- **Rio Grande compact** (compact, 1863–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-061) — owes: PAC → NM: elected town government; courts in Spanish and English; the territory's own institutions of 1850; the old grants confirmed, with each community's commons confirmed to the community; PAC → NM: any congregation keeps its chapel and calls its own priest; PAC → NM: Arizona county organized for Tucson and the Gila; PAC → NM: the Taos article: no one tried for 1847, on either side; the Taos and Rio Arriba companies come home as the compact's home guard, sworn to its laws and never ordered against the Union; NM → PAC: when the peace comes, debt peonage and the captive trade end, with compensation
- **The Pueblos' agreement** (pact, c.1863–, §10, APPROVED, CR-061) — owes: PAC → PUEBLOS: their grants entered in the register as homelands
- **Cubero agreement with the Navajo** (pact, 1863–, §10, APPROVED, CR-061) — owes: PAC → NAVAJO: the towns' slaving expeditions forbidden; NAVAJO → PAC: the raids stop
- **The Republic's credit to Richmond** (loan, 1863–, §11, APPROVED, CR-068) — owes: PAC → CSA: credit lent at interest for independence, spent in Europe
- **Withdrawal terms of the Fort Churchill garrison** (settlement, 1864–1864, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → US: the garrison leaves with its arms; volunteers who will not stay march east by Deseret's road, fed at Pacifica's charge
- **Washoe's civil compact** (compact, 1864–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → WASHOE: a published tax on the mines' net proceeds lighter than the one Washoe refused; PAC → WASHOE: the Carson Convention's guarantees kept
- **Navajo homeland treaty** (treaty, 1865–, §10, APPROVED, CR-015) — owes: PAC → NAVAJO: recognition of a homeland; no Long Walk; PAC → NAVAJO: the return of the Navajo held in New Mexican households: grown captives choose, a joint commission decides for the children, and Pacifica pays each household that releases one
- **Apache peace** (treaty, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → APACHE: homeland reservations, honest agents and prosecution of settler massacres; PAC → APACHE: paid scouts; PAC → APACHE: passage money for the Chiricahua from the Sunset road's customs
- **Mexican convention ending scalp bounties** (convention, c.1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: MX → PAC: an end to scalp bounties
- **McClellan armistice conventions** (convention, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: no military mission and no arms to the Confederacy while the Union's armistice with Richmond holds
- **Pacifica–Confederate Treaty of Amity and Commerce** (treaty, 1865–, §12, CANON) — owes: PAC → CSA: formal recognition
- **Enabling Law** (law, 1865–1887, §12, CANON)
- **Providentor** (office, 1865–1887, §12, CANON)
- **Hawaiian guarantees of the Enabling Law** (guarantee, 1865–, §12, APPROVED, CR-068) — owes: PAC → HI: protection of Hawaiian kingship, the Kuhina Nui, local institutions and agreed revenues, as protected provisions that cabinet legislation may not touch
- **Credit to Juárez's government on the Sonora customs** (loan, 1865–, §13, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → MX: recognition and credit secured on the Sonora customs; rifles through Paso del Norte; MX → PAC: a customs post at the boundary at the Pass that passes the rifles overland into Chihuahua
- **Spanish Fork agreement with Black Hawk's Utes** (pact, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → BHUTES: cattle and flour each year for the grazing the settlements took; homelands they will not be moved from; PAC → BHUTES: what was done that spring is forgiven on both sides; killings from that day go to the confederal court
- **Santa Rita del Cobre copper lease** (lease, 1865–, §12, APPROVED, CR-061) — owes: APACHE → PAC: the old copper mine at Santa Rita del Cobre; PAC → APACHE: a royalty to the Chihenne
- **The Hopi agreement (the villages' homeland and the Hopi article of the Navajo treaty)** (compact, 1865–, §12, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → HOPI: the mesas, fields, springs and grazing entered in the register as the villages' homeland, on a line of named places, with Moenkopi a detached part; no grant or Land Fund sale inside it (HP2, HP6); NAVAJO → HOPI: Diné families inside the line stay only by the village's leave (HP3); PAC → HOPI: passage to shrines, eagle grounds, salt and the San Francisco Peaks (HP4); no agent, school or mission placed on the mesas unless a village asks (HP5)
- **Confederate railway treaty** (treaty, 1866–, §13, CANON) — owes: CSA → PAC: a route across Texas to New Orleans for Huntington's line
- **Oregon's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: PAC → OR: the charter entrenched by the Confederal chambers and California and ratified by Hawaii's legislature: 'a charter no cabinet can touch'
- **The Oregon nations' conditional compacts** (compact, 1866–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-059)
- **Idaho's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-059)
- **Columbia's charter of accession (Washington Territory)** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-059)
- **Compacts of the treaty nations of the Sound, the coast and the Yakama** (compact, 1866–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: PAC → SOUNDNATIONS: the fishing places their treaties kept, as a right no licence can narrow
- **Protocol of certification of Oregon's returns** (convention, 1866–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-062)
- **Owyhee Paiute agreement** (pact, 1866–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060)
- **Ute homeland agreement of 1866** (pact, 1866–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → UTE: the Slope with North and Middle Park reserved as a Ute homeland that can never be sold, whose ground the Republic may lease only for the Utes
- **Washoe's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060)
- **Western Montana's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060)
- **Deseret's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → DESERET: Deseret's marriage and church-property law protected under the confederal floor; DESERET → PAC: the Nauvoo Legion placed within the Defense Law
- **New Mexico's abolition ordinance** (law, 1866–, §12, APPROVED, CR-061) — owes: PAC → NM: compensation for the peons and captives freed, forfeited by every patrón who rose in 1866
- **New Mexico's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, APPROVED, CR-061)
- **Western treaty (the Western line of the 1865 peace)** (treaty, 1867–, §12, CANON) — owes: US → PAC: cession of the Northwest, Great Basin, Arizona, Utah, Nevada, Idaho, western Montana, southwest Wyoming, Colorado's Western Slope, North and Middle Park, the San Luis Valley and New Mexico to 103°W; Oregon as Oregon's voters choose; US → PAC: evacuation of Port Townsend and Fort Union after the exchange; the surviving Puget Sound enclave evacuated; PAC → US: a $40 million gold vendor credit in six instalments from the exchange (see C-1867-VENDOR-CREDIT); PAC → US: the Union's merchants keep the Columbia and Puget Sound on published terms; its warships may come in for refuge or repair; no storehouse is granted; PAC → US: named roads open from Fort Benton to Helena, from Denver to Salt Lake and from Denver over Raton Pass to Santa Fe; the Santa Fe Trail open to the Union's freighters under published duties; PAC → US: the Colorado article: any mountain mining district may vote itself over, and Pacifica supplies and coins it either way; US → PAC: the Colorado article: transit through Denver; PAC → US: every resident of a ceded district may choose either citizenship and keeps property, courts and civil protection without distinction of colour
- **Pacific article (Guano Islands)** (treaty, 1867–, §12, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: US → PAC: renunciation of Guano Islands Act claims to Midway, Baker, Howland, Jarvis, Johnston and Kingman; quitclaim to Palmyra; PAC → US: the New York guano companies keep their workings until the deposits are exhausted and land cargoes duty-free at Hawaiian ports
- **Western cession vendor credit** (loan, 1867–1872, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: $40 million in gold in six instalments from the exchange of 1867, through 1872
- **Alaska purchase** (treaty, 1867–, §12, CANON) — owes: RU → PAC: Russia's claim to Russian America (Alaska); PAC → RU: $7.2 million in Pacifica's own gold, paid at handover; PAC → RU: the Russian subjects may leave or stay; the Orthodox Church keeps its property; the Creoles of Sitka and Kodiak become Pacifican citizens
- **Baja purchase** (treaty, 1867–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: MX → PAC: Baja (purchase); PAC → MX: the purchase price, paid over five years
- **Midway naval ground lease** (lease, 1867–, §14, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: HI → PAC: lease of Midway's naval ground (Midway part of the Hawaiian Crown lands)
- **Mutual waiver and the mixed claims commission** (settlement, 1867–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-062) — owes: US → PAC: the Union's claims from the war waived; PAC → US: Pacifica's claims from the war waived; PAC → HI: the family of the Hawaiian sailor killed in 1861 keeps its own claim before the commission
- **Charter of the Northern Commission** (charter, 1867–, §14, APPROVED, CR-070)
- **Charter of the Baja commission** (charter, 1867–1887, §13, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **The Mountain Districts' commission** (charter, 1867–1887, §12, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **Guaymas railway concession and free port** (concession, 1867–, §13, PROPOSED) — owes: MX → PAC: a Guaymas railway concession and free port
- **Guarantee of the negotiated transition at Edo** (guarantee, 1868–, §14, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → JP: fleet guarantee of the negotiated transition at Edo
- **1868 treaty with China** (treaty, 1868–, §14, PROPOSED) — owes: CN → PAC: ordinary foreign treaty rights; PAC → CN: jointly licensed family emigration (article shared by both)
- **Kodiak compact** (compact, 1868–, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: PAC → ALUTIIQ: only the nations' hunters take the sea otter
- **Seal-islands leases** (lease, c.1868–, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: SEALLESSEES → PAC: a price per skin; SEALLESSEES → UNANGAN: the Unangan sealers' terms, paid by the skin, carried in every lease — renewals: 1880, 1890, 1900, 1910, 1920, 1930
- **The Pribilof islands' compact with the Unangan** (compact, c.1868–, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: PAC → UNANGAN: their islands held by their own compact; their terms carried in every seal lease
- **The Confederation's line through British Columbia** (concession, c.1868–1897, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: BCCO → PAC: Western Union's line in British Columbia, held for the Confederation by a company of the colony
- **Sitka compact with the Tlingit** (compact, 1869–, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: PAC → TLINGIT: their village and its fisheries stay theirs; the Commission holds the Russian town's ground by a yearly rent; clan property is recognised in its courts; PAC → TLINGIT: a killing by the garrison is paid to the dead man's clan under Tlingit law; TLINGIT → PAC: disputes go to a joint hearing
- **Japanese alliance of 1870–1871** (alliance, 1870–, §14, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: full Japanese tariff autonomy and no consular courts; JP → PAC: dockyard rights at Yokosuka and a coaling lease at Hakodate; PAC → JP: foreign credit (direction as listed; not spelled out)
- **The Baja commission's compacts with the Kumeyaay, Paipai, Kiliwa and Cucapá** (compact, c.1870–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → BAJANATIONS: homelands in their sierras and the delta: the Kumeyaay in the Sierra Juárez, the Paipai at Santa Catarina, the Kiliwa in the San Pedro Mártir, the Cucapá in the delta inside Yuma (I-55, I-56)
- **Peace of 1871 (Sherman–Grant)** (settlement, 1871–, §13, CANON) — owes: CSA → US: free Mississippi navigation restored; US → PAC: quitclaim of the Pacific islands Pacifica occupied during the war
- **Pacifican guarantee of Confederate independence** (guarantee, 1871–, §13, CANON) — owes: PAC → CSA: guarantee of Confederate independence
- **Association treaties** (treaty, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: PAC → CSA: refinancing of Confederate debt; CSA → PAC: customs security for the refinanced debt and Pacifican customs co-administration; CSA → PAC: the lower Mobile Bay naval station, Galveston commercial privileges, Atlantic Auxiliary recruitment and Birmingham concessions; PAC → CSA: replacement of the surrendered Mississippi toll revenue — secured on: Confederate customs
- **The Alaskan rivers free forever to British Columbia** (treaty, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: navigation of the Stikine, the Yukon and the Porcupine through Alaska, free forever
- **Pacifica's loan, the Puget Sound rail link and free entry for British Columbia** (loan, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: a railway south to Puget Sound, a loan, and free entry for the colony's coal, timber and fish; BC → PAC: service of the loan
- **The Auk finder's compact (Gastineau Channel)** (compact, 1872–, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: PAC → AUK: a finder's share in the gold belt, agreed before the source is shown and paid by the mine
- **Mining leases in the Apache and Chiricahua countries (Globe, Clifton, Bisbee)** (lease, c.1872–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: APACHE → PAC: the Globe and Clifton districts inside the White Mountain line, and Bisbee's copper inside the Chiricahua line, on lease; PAC → APACHE: a royalty to each nation, as Santa Rita pays the Chihenne (C-1865-SANTA-RITA)
- **Alaminos local convention** (convention, 1873–, §15, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: an indemnity and the crew's release; ES → PAC: an anchorage for a Pacifican squadron to protect its trade
- **Japan's loan of 1873 (its San Francisco part)** (loan, 1873–, §14, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: JP → PAC: interest and repayment on the Republic's share of the part placed in San Francisco; JP → SANFRANCISCO: interest and repayment on the houses' share, Japan's bank being the Bank of California (CR-073 E2, the 1867 precedent)
- **The San Juan lease (Ouray's lease of the mining districts and roads)** (lease, 1873–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: UTE → PAC: the San Juan mineral belt (Silverton, Ouray, Telluride, Rico) and the Gunnison districts, with their railway and roads, on lease through the Confederation; PAC → UTE: the rent, paid to the nation; the valleys stay Ute
- **Puʻuloa Convention** (lease, 1874–1924, §17, APPROVED, CR-027) — owes: HI → PAC: lease of Pearl Harbor and its shores; no Hawaiian land alienated; PAC → HI: common defence — can end or refuse: HI: the legislature ratified it narrowly; a lease, not a cession
- **Spanish Republic's Pacifican gold loan** (loan, 1874–1876, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: service of the loan from Philippine customs and tobacco revenues; Pacifican agents sit in the customs houses — secured on: Philippine customs and tobacco revenues
- **Confederal ordinances and the Territorial Council** (law, 1874–1907, §17, APPROVED, CR-027) — owes: HI → PAC: (imposed) harbour police, the Honolulu port administration, the defence districts and, from 1876, an appointed Territorial Council that takes over the legislature's business — can end or refuse: HI: Hawaii's Supreme Court declares the ordinances void; the judgment is never enforced or reversed
- **Kāneʻohe capital lease** (lease, c.1875–, §17, CANON) — owes: HI → PAC: lease of the Kāneʻohe capital ground from the Hawaiian Crown lands
- **Philippine Redemption Loan** (loan, 1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PH → LONDON: service of the loan from the Philippine customs; PAC → LONDON: the Confederation's guarantee; PAC → PH: service limited to the gain the islands make over their last Spanish years, published every year — secured on: Philippine customs
- **Spanish law of sale (Philippines, Marianas, Carolines, Palau)** (treaty, 1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: the Philippines, Marianas, Carolines and Palau; PAC → ES: recognize no Cuban insurgents and close its ports to their filibusters — can end or refuse: ES: the sale requires a law of the Cortes
- **Philippine Organic Act (dependency of the Confederation)** (law, 1876–1887, §15, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: government by the Providentor's office through a Governor-General with a Philippine Council; the Audiencia keeps Spanish law
- **Chartered Philippine tobacco company** (charter, c.1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029)
- **Philippine land bonds (friar-estate purchases)** (loan, c.1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: the Confederation's guarantee, the same as on the Redemption Loan
- **First Korean–Pacifican treaty** (treaty, c.1878–, §14, CANON)
- **Sulu compacts (Sultan and datus)** (compact, 1878–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SULU → PAC: settlement of the south (terms not stated) — can end or refuse: SULU: the Sultan alone can consent to undo them
- **North Borneo lease to the Pacifican Borneo company** (lease, 1878–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SULU → PAC: lease of North Borneo to a Pacifican Borneo company
- **Vladivostok settlement folded into the Congress of Berlin** (settlement, 1878–, §16, PROPOSED) — owes: RU → JP: restoration of Southern Sakhalin; UK → PAC: recognition of the Philippine purchase
- **1878 Pago Pago treaty** (treaty, 1878–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SAMOA → PAC: Pago Pago (terms not stated), signed by Hawaii on the Confederation's behalf
- **The trunk-line charters on Land Fund grants** (charter, c.1878–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → TRUNKLINES: Land Fund grants, ten sections to the mile, in land and not in cash; no grant from a homeland (I-53; outline C5); TRUNKLINES → PAC: the lines built: the Raton line to Las Vegas in 1879, the thirty-fifth parallel to the Colorado in 1880–83, the Salt Lake–Snake–Columbia line in 1881–84
- **Letters of marque in the war with Russia** (law, 1878–1878, §19, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **Ryukyu mediation** (settlement, 1879–, §15, PROPOSED)
- **The Assumption Act** (law, 1879–, §19, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → OR: the member's debt assumed and its paper guaranteed, with a grant per head; likewise for each member that takes it
- **An Act for the Common Execution of the Laws (the Commission Act)** (law, 1879–1887, §19, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **The Patrimony (its lending arm at home, the Development Fund)** (charter, 1880–, §19, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Colombian transit treaty** (treaty, 1880–, §19, CANON) — owes: CO → PAC: transit and neutrality rights, first refusal on the concession and control of the Panama Railroad
- **Chilkoot compact** (compact, 1880–, §25, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: PAC → CHILKOOT: their packers carry; their trade goes on; CHILKOOT → PAC: prospectors cross their pass for a toll; CHILKOOT → PAC: from 1884, with the Commission, a wagon road to Sheep Camp and an aerial tramway over the summit
- **Western Slope mining lease on Uncompahgre Ute land** (lease, 1881–, §19, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: PAC → UTE: the lease fee for the mining ground, paid through the Republic's own office
- **The Portneuf right of way through Fort Hall** (lease, 1881–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: FORTHALL → PAC: the railway's right of way down the Portneuf, leased, not sold; PAC → FORTHALL: a rent the railway pays
- **The Militia Act** (law, 1882–, §19, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **Charter of the Light and Matter Works** (charter, 1883–, §19, PROPOSED, CR-049) — owes: ESTATES → PAC: the works, the Western Slope mines and the source trade run as one state champion with the state holding the shares; the estate pays for the Polytechnic's laboratories; ESTATES → PAC: from 1932 the Works collects and handles all fuel delivered under Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia, carrying it away at its own cost, charging nothing and paying nothing (CR-086)
- **Keelung coal concession** (concession, c.1884–, §16, PROPOSED) — owes: CN → PAC: a coal concession to a Chinese–Pacifican company; PAC → CN: railway construction and fleet-rebuilding credit
- **Chinese Customs Inspectorate under Pacifican leadership** (office, 1885–1929, §16, CANON) — owes: PAC → CN: administration of the Customs Inspectorate, with multinational staff and published accounts (PROPOSED); CN → PAC: service of Pacifican credit secured on customs — can end or refuse: CN: China's retained appointment power
- **Papal arbitration and the Leo XIII bargain** (settlement, 1885–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → PAC: Pacifica's Carolines confirmed; Pacifica's claim to Wake recorded; PAC → VATICAN: freedom for the Catholic missions in the Carolines; PAC → DE: trade and coaling rights (Germany keeps the Marshalls); PAC → VATICAN: Philippine parishes pass to Filipino secular priests; Filipino bishops named with the right to succeed; friar estates paid for partly into an endowment that stays in the islands
- **The Ahtna finder's compact (Chitina copper)** (compact, 1885–, §25, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: PAC → AHTNA: a finder's share in the copper of the Chitina
- **Philippine gold-exchange peso** (law, 1886–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: holds the peso's reserve in San Francisco
- **The four-power fur-seal convention** (convention, 1886–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: fifteen percent of the Pribilof catch; RU → BC: fifteen percent of the Commander Islands' catch, 1886–1921; PAC → BC: from 1921, with the Commander Islands, fifteen percent of their catch; JP → BC: ten percent of Robben Island's catch; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the dominion's shares, by succession under the Association Treaty
- **Port Hamilton settlement: guarantee of Korean territory** (guarantee, 1887–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with Russia and Pacifica); RU → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with China and Pacifica); PAC → KR: guarantee of Korean territory; a duty to protect Korea without owning it; UK → KR: withdrawal from Port Hamilton
- **Constitution of the Realm** (constitution, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → HI: entrenched language and tenure guarantees for Hawaii as a historic land (no veto); PAC → COUNTIES: land taxes, roads, sanitation, primary schools and ordinary policing left to county boards; PAC → CAL: California a historic land: Spanish entrenched, community property, the Mexican grants as organic law (CR-071 G6.2)
- **Regency of the Realm** (office, 1887–1907, §21, CANON)
- **Polytechnic charter (chartered estate)** (charter, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → ESTATES: the Polytechnic's examinations, audit, General Staff and telegraph — can end or refuse: CROWN: the ruler chooses the chief from three graduate-council nominees; removal only with Senate consent
- **Compact with Tokyo** (compact, 1887–1927, §24, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → PAC: backs the regency and holds its officers to the agreement; PAC → JP: guarantee against Russia and support for treaty revision with Europe; PAC → NAVAL: command of the western squadron reserved to officers of the Japanese naval party — renewals: 1897, 1907, 1917 — can end or refuse: JP: ratifies each ten-year renewal; PAC: ratifies each ten-year renewal
- **The labour article of the Constitution of the Realm** (guarantee, 1887–, §21, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → COUNTIES: no labour bound by debt, penal contract or a broker's ticket; the same wage for the same work, whoever does it; a day of rest in seven, on the day of the worker's faith; the eight-hour day on the Realm's works and in its chartered undertakings (H-16, H7.2)
- **The compact lands under the Constitution of the Realm** (guarantee, 1887–, §21, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → NATIONS: the homelands' compacts carried as the Realm's own; a homeland's line, powers and grade are not organic law and change only by compact (I-8; CR-033 A22); PAC → NATIONS: one senator for each homeland of a million acres or more, chosen by its own council; one for each league of enclaves that reaches that size (I-70 to I-72); PAC → NATIONS: citizenship for a nation's member by registration, without the English test (K-24); COUNTIES → NATIONS: a county's board, taxes and courts stop at an enclave's line unless the nation contracts for a service (I4) — can end or refuse: NATIONS: a compact changes only with the consent of the nation affected and the Realm
- **Office of Public Measures (independent estate)** (charter, 1888–, §21, CANON)
- **Franchise Law of 1888** (law, 1888–, §25, CANON)
- **Earned county autonomy (chartered status, receivership and programme sunset)** (law, c.1888–, §25, CANON) — owes: PAC → COUNTIES: chartered status after five consecutive years at the Office's four standards; measures grants follow results; COUNTIES → PAC: receivership permitted after three years below the standards or declared disorder — can end or refuse: PAC: a suspended board stays suspended up to five years, renewable by the Senate; PAC: county powers, boundaries and grades are organic law, amendable by both chambers without local consent
- **Confederate compensation bonds (final abolition)** (loan, 1888–, §25, CANON) — owes: CSA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: coupons on long compensation bonds financing final abolition; PAC → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: Pacifica's guarantee of the coupons (it pays them when Richmond misses in 1893–1895) — secured on: Confederate customs co-administered by Pacifica
- **The labour law of 1888** (law, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **The Companies Act (the Pacific Companies made a system)** (law, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **Korea's treaty of service (the king's guard)** (treaty, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → KR: a Pacific Company to drill the king's guard at Seoul in Korean words of command; KR → PAC: pay from the Korean treasury through the Companies Office
- **China's treaty of service (the Customs' preventive service)** (treaty, 1888–1929, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → CN: companies for the Customs' preventive service under the Inspectorate
- **The nations' ladder (land bought back and the chartered nation)** (law, c.1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → NATIONS: first refusal on a nation's own ceded land still in the Land Fund, at the price the Fund paid; additions to a homeland by supplementary compact; PAC → COUNTIES: the land tax lost to a homeland's addition, paid for ten years from the Land Fund's margin; PAC → NATIONS: for a chartered nation (five years at the Office's standard): its own trusteeship and the lease fee, a patrimony on the Patrimony's rules, chartered companies and home borrowing with Treasury consent, a court of first instance and police, and its language certified for the tongue vote
- **Berlin Act (Samoa)** (treaty, 1889–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; UK → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; PAC → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power
- **Midway cable-station lease from the Hawaiian Crown** (lease, 1889–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: HI → PAC: a cable-station lease on Midway, Hawaiian Crown land
- **Guest-worker programme** (law, 1890–, §25, CANON)
- **The conservancy contract of 1891 (Yellow River and Grand Canal)** (concession, 1891–, §25, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: PAC → CN: Pacifican engineers for the Yellow River and the Grand Canal; CN → PAC: the conservancy charge, among the customs' first obligations — secured on: the Maritime Customs revenue
- **Nitrate contracts at Iquique and the loan on the nitrate duty** (loan, 1891–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: CL → PAC: nitrate on long contracts at Iquique, delivered to the Realm's fields from about 1900; CL → PAC: service of the loan to the Chilean state — secured on: the nitrate duty; Chile's copper duty from the mid-1920s
- **Elected Philippine provincial juntas** (law, 1892–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: elected provincial juntas
- **Japanese landing grants for the southern wire at Chichijima and Tokyo Bay** (concession, 1892–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: JP → PAC: landings at Chichijima and on Tokyo Bay for the southern wire, under the compact of 1887
- **Hokkaido Cold-Hardy Rice Breeding Exchange** (compact, 1893–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: the Realm's stations cross cold-hardy rice with Hokkaido's and give winter nurseries in Hawaiʻi, which halves the breeding cycle; HOKKAIDO-AG → ESTATES: Hokkaido's test station and Sapporo Agricultural College share the crossing and its lines
- **Korean armistice brokered by the Regent** (treaty, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: Chinese suzerainty ended; CN → JP: a moderate indemnity and commercial rights; Japanese economic predominance in Korea
- **Guarantee of Korean independence and neutrality** (guarantee, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; JP → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; PAC → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality
- **Salt Lake's county charter (the first chartered county)** (charter, 1894–, §25, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **Rescue of the Union's gold reserve** (settlement, 1895–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: rescue of the Union's gold reserve; US → PAC: canal recognition, Asian-war neutrality and tariff concessions
- **Formosan Revenue Convention** (convention, 1895–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → PAC: a Pacifican Director-General nominated by the Regent, holding a Qing commission, controls the camphor monopoly, Keelung mines, Formosan customs and the Keelung harbour trust (pilots, lights, works and coaling rights); CN → JP: receipts pay the indemnity and Japan; CN → PAC: Pacifica's fee from receipts, before the provincial treasury — secured on: camphor monopoly, Keelung mines, Formosan customs and Keelung harbour trust
- **The Formosan revenue guard** (treaty, 1895–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → CN: a company guarding the Keelung mines and the camphor stores for the Director-General (C-1895-FORMOSA)
- **Credit for Chinese railways** (loan, 1896–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → CN: credit for a Chinese-owned Manchurian railway and the Hankow–Canton trunk
- **Whitney's commerce and arbitration treaty** (treaty, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: PAC → US: return of the Bread March prisoners
- **Indemnity facility and gold loan for Japan's gold transition** (loan, 1897–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → JP: a smaller indemnity facility and a gold loan supporting Japan's gold transition
- **Sale of the British Columbia line to British Columbia** (settlement, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: the old line through the colony, as the first half of the colony's own wire to the Yukon
- **The warnings compact (earthquakes at sea and typhoons)** (compact, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: PAC → JP: typhoon warnings: the Manila Observatory, with Guam and Chichijima, wires the storm tracks north to Japan; JP → PAC: warnings of offshore earthquakes and tide-gauge readings, wired outward to Honolulu by Ōmori Fusakichi and the Central Meteorological Observatory, by the southern wire and from 1903 by the Kuril cable
- **Protocol of Richmond (division of the Confederate debt)** (settlement, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: VA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; NC → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; GULF → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: its share of the association debt and of the compensation bonds, converted at its founding into a loan on oil royalties; CSA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: the rest: more than two-thirds of the whole, under continued customs co-administration
- **Joint guarantee of Cuban independence** (guarantee, c.1898–, §25, CANON) — owes: PAC → CU: guarantee of independence; GULF → CU: guarantee of independence; US → CU: guarantee of independence — can end or refuse: PAC: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors; GULF: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors; US: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors
- **Cuban colonial debt held by Pacifica** (loan, c.1898–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: CU → PAC: service of discounted colonial debt bought by Pacifica — secured on: Cuban customs
- **Exclusive Nicaraguan canal rights** (concession, c.1898–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: NI → PAC: exclusive canal rights
- **The Palatan peace (Tambunan a compact land)** (compact, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → NBINTERIOR: Tambunan held as a compact land under its own people; the Pacifican Borneo company held to the peace by the Companies Office on pain of its licence
- **Partition of Samoa** (settlement, 1899–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029)
- **Settlement with Britain during the Boer War** (settlement, c.1899–, §25, PROPOSED)
- **Fanning cable station and equatorial islands recognition** (treaty, c.1899–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → UK: recognizes British Fanning as the station of the Pacific Cable; UK → PAC: recognizes Pacifican sovereignty over the equatorial islands from Palmyra to Baker
- **Liaodong Gulf pledge and the Huludao first refusal** (guarantee, 1899–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → PAC: no part of the coast of the Liaodong Gulf to be leased or ceded to any power; CN → PAC: first refusal on a harbour at Huludao if one is ever built there with foreign capital
- **Manchurian railway section: Japanese running rights and wartime railway board** (concession, c.1899–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → JP: running rights on the Chinese-owned Manchurian section; CN → PAC: a Pacifican railway board in wartime
- **Gulf compact (conditions of admission)** (compact, 1900–, §25, CANON) — owes: GULF → PAC: meet the five measures and the two-part sixth test, registers, revenue, debt and policy conditions, each held twenty-five consecutive years, before admission is considered — can end or refuse: PAC: considers admission only if the Republic adds usable power and freedom of action without adding risk or a veto; may demand a second generation
- **Mutual defense pact with the Gulf Republic** (pact, 1900–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → GULF: mutual defense; GULF → PAC: mutual defense
- **Trade treaty with the Gulf Republic** (treaty, 1900–, §25, PROPOSED)
- **The Gulf's oil law: separation of the stages and the 55% ceiling** (compact, 1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: GULF → PAC: bans secret affiliates, rebates and price agreements; no refiner owns more than a minority of producing wells; trunk pipelines are common carriers at published tariffs; PAC → GULF: the trust refines no more than 55% of the Republic's crude and buys at posted prices
- **Guarantee of Chile's nitrate coast and the Strait** (guarantee, 1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → CL: guarantee against a seizure of the nitrate coast by any power from outside the Americas; PAC → CL: guarantee of the Strait as the treaty of 1881 left it; CL → PAC: coaling at Valparaíso and in the Strait
- **The storage compacts of 1900–1920** (compact, c.1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → AKIMEL: their share of the Salt; PAC → PYRAMID: their lake's flow from the Truckee; PAC → PUEBLOS: a compact of their own on the Rio Grande
- **Cable landings by the Kurils at Hakodate** (concession, 1903–, §25, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: JP → PAC: landing of the Sitka cable by Attu and the Kurils at Hakodate, on Japanese soil
- **The Pingxiang coke credit (Hanyeping)** (loan, 1904–, §26, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → PAC: service of the credit for Pingxiang's coke and its haulage
- **The western-waters treaty and its joint commission** (treaty, 1906–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: a joint commission of equals with powers to regulate as well as investigate on the Columbia, Kootenay, Okanagan, Similkameen, Skagit, Pend Oreille, the Fraser's salmon and the Alaskan rivers; no upstream-sovereignty doctrine; PAC → CLD: from Proclamation Day in 1932, the same under a water board of Pacifica and Caledonia
- **Manila–Shanghai cable landing agreement** (concession, 1906–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → CN: a sea cable of Carquinez gutta-percha from Manila, laid by the Realm's telegraph estate and the Philippine administration; CN → PAC: a landing near the mouth of the Yangtze, where China's Imperial Telegraph Administration owns and works the shore end and the Shanghai office
- **1907 renewal of the compact with Tokyo** (compact, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-022) — owes: PAC → JP: Kiyoshi as Japanese Fleet-General; a joint war council; family immigration; civic equality for Japanese Pacificans
- **Act of Settlement of 1907** (constitution, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → CROWN: establishes the Crown of Pacifica in the House of Thorne-Kamehameha; Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty; HI → CROWN: joins the Hawaiian crown to the Crown of Pacifica; the ʻAha Aliʻi proclaims Julian heir to the Hawaiian crown as Kamehameha VI; PAC → HI: dissolves the Territorial Council and restores Hawaii's legislature; Hawaiian restored as the Crown's language and in its schools; ʻAha Aliʻi keeps custody of Hawaiian crown and kuleana lands; Thalassia's ground confirmed as land of the Hawaiian throne — can end or refuse: HI: the union is put to a plebiscite of Hawaiian subjects by the restored legislature and the ʻAha Aliʻi; PAC: Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty by two-thirds
- **Julian's crowns (Sovereign of Pacifica, Kamehameha VI)** (office, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040)
- **Philippine crown offer and Calderón's organic law** (constitution, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040) — owes: PH → CROWN: the Philippine crown, offered by the Cortes and confirmed by plebiscite; CROWN → PH: a single-chamber Cortes with a permanent commission, its own budget and army; Gobernadores never posted to their home province; neither crown subordinate; Lugarteniente General governs without the old veto; PAC → PH: the Realm takes over what remains of the Redemption Loan at its 1907 balance (MODEL) — can end or refuse: PH: the Cortes offers and a plebiscite confirms; terms signed before the vote
- **Ka Lōkahi, the council of the crowns** (compact, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040) — renewals: 1917, 1927 — can end or refuse: PAC: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes; HI: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes; PH: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes
- **Mukden-Jinzhou-Huludao railway loan** (loan, 1908–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → UK: service of the senior share of the railway loan, led by the British and Chinese Corporation; CN → PAC: service of the junior share taken in San Francisco — secured on: the line's receipts
- **British and German undertakings on the Union** (pact, c.1908–1917, §27, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: UK → PAC: no state-backed money, bases, intelligence or arms to a Union attempt to overturn the North American settlement; DE → PAC: no state-backed money, bases, intelligence or arms to a Union attempt to overturn the North American settlement
- **Bonneville compact (the Cascades dam's reach)** (lease, 1908–, §25, APPROVED, CR-055) — owes: BONN → PAC: the dam's site and reach leased to the power estate's hydro companies; PAC → BONN: a royalty on every kilowatt-hour; electricity at cost for their villages; replacement fishing sites; ladders, with the fish counts published before their observers
- **The banner loan of 1908** (loan, 1908–, §26, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → PAC: service from the stipend funds the provinces no longer pay, the customs balance as second security — secured on: the provincial stipend funds; the customs balance as second security
- **Huludao Harbour Trust** (concession, 1909–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → PAC: a Qing commission for a Pacifican director and Pacifican engineers of the Huludao harbour, on the Keelung pattern; CN → PAC: the harbour dues pledged to the bondholders until the bonds are paid — secured on: Huludao harbour dues
- **Bosnian settlement and standing commission** (settlement, 1909–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: AH → RS: Serbian trade through Ottoman Salonica, with the Porte's consent; a Bosnian Diet under Habsburg sovereignty; AH → RS: inquiry and a standstill before either side uses force, by order of the standing commission; RS → AH: inquiry and a standstill before either side uses force, by order of the standing commission
- **Joachimsthal rider to the Bosnian compensation loan** (loan, 1909–1939, §27, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: PAC → AH: the loan with which Austria pays the Porte for its lost rights in Bosnia; AH → PAC: all the uranium compounds and the residues left after extraction at the Joachimsthal state works for thirty years, and first refusal on new Bohemian uranium ore; AH → PAC: the exclusive marketing of the Austrian barium-borne salt outside the monarchy and a share of its sales (CR-050)
- **Compact of the Tuolumne (Ralston College inside the wall)** (compact, 1910–1947, §27, APPROVED, CR-050-R1) — owes: PAC → RALC: its own compound, director and a budget fixed for five years at a time; equal access to sources, instruments, the installation and the internal journal; a neutral chair for contests; credit by school; endowed chairs; a champion of its choosing; RALC → PAC: its inner work under the seal, its people bound by the bond for the term — renewals: 1927 — can end or refuse: RALC: leaves on a year's notice; its people stay bound to the end of the term
- **Hetch Hetchy lease (Tuolumne Me-Wuk)** (lease, 1910–1947, §27, APPROVED, CR-050-R1) — owes: TMW → PAC: the use of Hetch Hetchy Valley for the seal's term, renewably; PAC → TMW: a fee every year; title to Poopenaut Valley and protected groves outside the fence; an autumn fortnight in the north groves; wardens' and burners' work at the Works' rates; the clinic — renewals: 1927
- **Kootzaduka'a compact (the east rim of Hetch Hetchy)** (compact, 1910–, §27, APPROVED, CR-050-R1) — owes: PAC → KMP: summer rights in the high country above the rims, a marked rim trail around the valley's east end, a share of the fee, wardens' work on the east rim
- **The Wireless Act** (law, 1911–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078)
- **Great Bear Lake: the Works' exclusive contract** (concession, 1912–1982, §27, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: CA → PAC: a Canadian-chartered company sells the Works all its ore under an exclusive contract; PAC → CA: finances the company; a free allowance of the salt for Canada's hospitals; the Works' ore-handling code; PAC → CA: from 1932, a higher royalty (rate OPEN)
- **The customs trusteeship of January 1912** (settlement, 1912–1929, §27, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The election air-time act (amending the Wireless Act)** (law, 1913–, §25, APPROVED, CR-079)
- **Fuel-oil contracts with the German navy** (pact, c.1914–1914, §28, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → DE: fuel oil for Germany's new ships, under contracts Ballin brokered
- **Entente loans (wartime advances)** (loan, 1914–, §30, CANON, CR-025) — owes: ENTENTE → PAC: repayment of the advances gathered from the Treasury, Pacifican savers and foreign subscriptions through the San Francisco syndicates; ENTENTE → US: Union investors buying through Pacifican correspondents keep their own claims and their own risk; ENTENTE → JP: repayment of Japan's lending to Russia from 1916 and to Britain and France from 1917 — secured on: from the end of 1916, the Allies' requisitioned foreign securities, many of them Pacifican bonds (the collateral loans)
- **Compacts of the later Columbia dams (Rocky Reach, Wells, McNary, Chief Joseph)** (lease, 1914–, §25, APPROVED, CR-055) — owes: COLREACH → PAC: each dam's site and reach leased; PAC → COLREACH: a royalty on every kilowatt-hour; electricity at cost for their villages; replacement fishing sites; ladders, with the fish counts published before their observers
- **Letters of marque in the war with Germany** (law, 1914–1919, §28, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **Shinkolobwe purchase option** (concession, 1915–1919, §30, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: UMHK → PAC: an exclusive option on all the deposit's ore; PAC → UMHK: financing for the mine
- **War Revenue Act** (law, 1915–, §30, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Recognition of Carranza and the rail transit to Douglas** (settlement, 1915–, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → MX: recognition jointly with the Gulf; rail transit for Hill's troops to Douglas; no arms; GULF → MX: recognition jointly with Pacifica; rail transit from Eagle Pass
- **The Sino-Japanese treaties of 25 May 1915** (treaty, 1915–, §28, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: commercial rights along the Manchurian line under China's law and police; confirmation of the Daye ore contract; JP → CN: an effective 5 per cent from the tariff revision of 1918; PAC → CN: an effective 5 per cent from the tariff revision of 1918
- **The Entente's grain on Pacifican credit** (loan, 1915–, §30, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → UK: grain bought with the Entente loans; UK → PAC: repayment with the rest of London's debt
- **The consented ninety-day expedition into Chihuahua** (pact, 1916–1916, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: MX → PAC: formal consent under the Apache convention's pursuit clause; liaison officers from the War Ministry; PAC → MX: ninety days, kept to the day; no fighting with Carranza's troops; GULF → PAC: a brigade under the defence pact of 1900
- **Siberian coalition** (alliance, 1918–, §31, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: the mass of the army in the east; CN → PAC: manpower and the Manchurian railway; PAC → SIB: command, finance, railway organization, artillery, armored trains, aircraft, river flotillas and the expeditionary corps
- **The Siberian treaty of August 1918 (Komuch, Siberia, Alash, under Pacifican guarantee)** (treaty, 1918–1921, §31, APPROVED, CR-041) — owes: KOMUCH → SIB: recognition of Siberia, independent since 4 July 1918; Komuch's suspensive veto over Alash replaced by the guarantor's arbitration; SIB → ALASH: mutual recognition restored; mixed institutions in Akmolinsk, Semipalatinsk and Turgai under joint commissars; Alash courts, the kibitka tax and local councils; a Kazakh army raised by Alash on the Siberian army's plan under common command; the land of the three oblasts frozen as it stands; ALASH → SIB: the Kazakh army under common command in wartime; two autonomies in union toward a federal power of autonomous Siberia and autonomous Alash, or an all-Russian federal power created with their participation; PAC → SIB: guarantee of Siberia's frontier; the treaty printed by the Office and deposited in Peking and Tokyo; KOMUCH → PAC: the Constituent Assembly's land law enforced without landlord reclamation, as a condition of credit — secured on: the Imperial gold reserve taken at Kazan, in a Reserve Bank escrow at Chelyabinsk
- **Emba oil concession** (concession, 1918–, §31, APPROVED, CR-041) — owes: ALASH → PAC: the Emba oil concession, signed with the Siberian treaty; kept by Pacifica through Alash's transfer to Russia in 1921
- **Manchurian railway running rights and transit** (concession, 1918–, §31, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: CN → JP: running rights and, later, guaranteed transit on the Chinese-owned line, without giving its territory to Japan; PAC → CN: the railway returns to Chinese management, improved at the coalition's expense
- **The coalition's agreement with Peking on the Chinese line (the Khingan agreement)** (pact, 1918–, §31, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The Treaty of The Hague** (treaty, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: evacuation of Belgium and northern France; no indemnity; DE → PAC: loss of its colonies; Pacifica and Japan retain Germany's Pacific islands; DE → FR: Alsace-Lorraine given up, not to France: a neutral republic in union with Switzerland (C-1919-ALSACE-LORRAINE); PAC → CN: return of Shandong apart from the Tsingtao anchorage; UK → DE: recognition of Germany's clients in Poland, the Baltic region, Ukraine and Finland; AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918 (C-1919-BOHEMIA)
- **Alsace-Lorraine: neutral republic in union with Switzerland** (guarantee, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: the Reichsland given up to a neutral republic confirmed by a vote of its people; PAC → FR: the signatories guarantee the republic's neutrality
- **The Bohemian settlement and the legionnaires' amnesty** (settlement, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918; no partition; AH → FR: an amnesty for the Czechoslovak legionnaires, guaranteed by the treaty (PROPOSED); FR → AH: withdrawal of France's 1918 recognition of the Czechoslovak National Council (PROPOSED)
- **Tsingtao anchorage lease** (lease, 1919–, §30, CANON) — owes: CN → PAC: the Tsingtao anchorage on lease; PAC → CN: return of the rest of Shandong, denying Japan the prize
- **Permanent arbitration court** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: PAC → JP: the settlement carries Japan's racial equality clause — renewals: 1932
- **1919 naval limits** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: UK → PAC: parity: the combined Pacifica–Japan fleets count at parity with Britain; DE → PAC: Germany below Britain and the Pacific partners; France and Italy below Germany; UK → PAC: disposal of older twelve-inch ships by 1922, through scrapping, training hulks or targets (Britain and Germany)
- **Julian's promise to Wrangel: Siberia's own vote** (pact, 1919–1921, §31, APPROVED, CR-041) — owes: PAC → RU: the coalition will not stand in the way when Siberia's own assembly chooses the federation; Pacifica's credit, the Office's films and the railway board's payrolls lean the way Omsk already leans; RU → PAC: a Russia that will recognise the settlement, pay its debts and owe Julian its survival; Pacifica's money reaches Wrangel only against results
- **Land-and-grain guarantee** (guarantee, 1919–, §31, PROPOSED, CR-030) — owes: PAC → SIB: the railway half: three paths in ten stay with Siberian grain, coal and passengers
- **Freeze of the San Francisco traders' Chukotka debt books** (settlement, 1919–, §31, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: PAC → CHUKOTKA: the debt books of Pacifica's San Francisco traders in Chukotka stay frozen until a commission can write them down
- **Shinkolobwe concession company** (concession, 1919–2018, §30, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: BE → PAC: the Shinkolobwe concession in a Belgian-registered company three-quarters owned by the Light and Matter Works, all output to Pacifica; PAC → BE: royalties, seats on the board and a free allowance of the barium-borne element for Belgian hospitals; reconstruction credit; from 1928–29 a higher royalty and a larger allowance (PROPOSED, C-1929-SHINKOLOBWE-REVISION); PAC → UMHK: long copper contracts
- **Joachimsthal uranium concession** (concession, 1919–1969, §30, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: BOH → PAC: the uranium side of the Joachimsthal mines, run by the Works; PAC → BOH: royalties paid to Prague; reconstruction credit — can end or refuse: BOH: renewals pass through the Bohemian Diet
- **Tyuya-Muyun (Fergana) concession** (concession, 1919–, §32, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: RU → PAC: the Tyuya-Muyun deposit in Fergana as a concession alongside Emba
- **The Previsión** (law, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Pacifica's accession to the Declaration of Paris** (convention, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **Baku concession agreement (Anglo-Pacifican, with Azerbaijan)** (concession, c.1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-046) — owes: AZ → PAC: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; PAC → UK: from 1927, a fixed allowance for the Royal Navy at Batum at market price — renewals: 1927 — can end or refuse: UK: the seventh-year review: invoked in the spring of 1927 for first call and a British operator, and withdrawn in June
- **White Bluffs compact (the Wanapum and the Columbia station)** (lease, 1920–1947, §32, APPROVED, CR-051) — owes: WANA → PAC: rights of use over the station's ground, renewably; title stays theirs; PAC → WANA: a fee every year; the village untouched; the river open with two landing places; the sacred places protected; monthly published measurements with a Wanapum observer; wardens' work at the Works' rates; the clinic — can end or refuse: WANA: ends the lease on a year's notice, with compensation, if the salmon counts fall below the 1919–1920 baseline three years running or the discharge limit is broken; the Works must then cut the piles' output
- **River accord with the treaty fishing peoples of the Columbia** (compact, 1920–, §32, APPROVED, CR-051) — owes: PAC → CRTF: fishing rights at their usual places confirmed in writing; the same measurements sent to their councils; a share of the White Bluffs fee; seats on the remedy panel, the Office in the chair
- **Rock Island compact (the dam's site and reach)** (lease, 1920–1947, §32, APPROVED, CR-051) — owes: WENC → PAC: the dam's site and the reach it raises, renewably; PAC → WENC: a royalty on every kilowatt-hour the dam sells; electricity at cost for their villages; fish ladders with published counts; a replacement fishing site of their councils' choosing; work and the clinic
- **The Realm's chartered industrial districts** (law, 1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **The regional works share of the customs surplus** (settlement, 1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **Treaty of Harbin** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: RU → SIB: Siberia, a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote, holds the country from the Ob to Lake Baikal; the Okhotsk coast west of Ola Bay and Nikolayevsk (APPROVED); Yakutia, Chukotka, the Arctic coast and the islands east of the Kara Sea (PROPOSED); SIB → RU: entry into the federal compact by the vote of the Regional Duma at Tomsk; the Siberia–Alash union of 1918 dissolved in the same act; RU → ALASH: a land of Russia keeping its courts and kibitka tax, without its army or its equal standing; Alash is not asked; RU → PAC: concessions at Grozny and Maikop against the reconstruction credit; AZ → PAC: a joint Anglo-Pacifican concession at Baku: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; RU → JP: a client sphere in Transbaikalia around Chita; RU → CN: the Amur, a sphere in Outer Mongolia and Tuva; renunciation of extraterritorial rights, concessions and the Boxer indemnity share (to Chinese schools); Mongolia back under Chinese suzerainty under its own Mongol government; SIB → CN: passage for Chinese ships down the Amur to the sea; CN → SIB: Siberian trade and settlement rights in the Chinese spheres; SIB → JP: a Siberian trial of the Nikolayevsk partisan leaders and an indemnity; JP → SIB: passage for Siberian ships through the straits of a sea now Japanese on three sides; SIB → PAC: the straits into the Kara Sea open to merchant ships; PAC → RU: gives up the old claims to Wrangel Island and the De Long islands, taking the Commander Islands instead; JP → SIB: renunciation of the pan-Mongol schemes Japan once backed
- **Treaty of Kars (the three districts)** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-047) — owes: TR → AM: Kars and Kağızman, with the fortress and the railway from Alexandropol, by the district vote of 1921; AM → TR: Olti and Ardahan, by the same vote; TR → GE: Batum stays Georgian as a free port; GE → TR: Turkish goods pass free through Batum; GE → UK: the pipeline terminal at Batum; GE → PAC: a tanker berth of Pacifica's own at Batum
- **Lena gold concessions** (concession, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → PAC: the Lena gold concessions
- **Railway west of Chita** (concession, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → PAC: the railway west of Chita, run by a Pacifican railway board since the war
- **Arctic station leases** (lease, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031)
- **Northern sea route charter** (charter, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031)
- **The Birmingham district** (charter, 1921–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **The Caribbean understanding** (settlement, 1921–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → US: Venezuela stays the Union's source; Pacifica holds only its Lago minority; US → GULF: no Union naval station on the Caribbean mainland; PAC → GULF: no Pacifican base on the Caribbean mainland; canal rights are not bases
- **Standing alliance of Pacifica, Japan, China and federal Russia** (alliance, 1922–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → RU: command of the alliance and its finance; reconstruction credit in stages, against results; PAC → SIB: a guarantee of the federal compact and of Siberia's borders, keeping a smaller claim on the railway in reserve for war (MODEL)
- **Funding of Britain's gold-dollar debt** (loan, 1923–1985, §30, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → PAC: service of its gold-dollar war debt over sixty-two years at reduced interest; the obligation keeps its gold-dollar value through sterling depreciation; PAC → UK: in 1932 cancels seventeen of the fifty-three remaining annuities in three tranches (Caledonia 2, the retrenchment 11, the signature 4; MODEL) and writes off the suspended annuity of 1931–32; thirty-six annuities stand
- **Yokohama relief and reconstruction credit** (loan, 1923–, §33, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: repayment of the reconstruction credit
- **The Huludao industrial district** (charter, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **The Pacific Halibut Treaty** (treaty, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-083)
- **Puʻuloa tenancy at the Mōʻī's pleasure** (lease, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: HI → PAC: the fleet keeps its base at Puʻuloa as the Hawaiian crown's tenant; PAC → HI: restore Puʻuloa's fishponds and pearl beds; no dredging in Kaʻahupahau's waters — can end or refuse: HI: held at the Mōʻī's pleasure; renewed at each accession
- **The Gulf Republic's Houston district** (charter, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **The Columbia storage compact** (compact, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: BC → PAC: storage on the upper Columbia (the Arrow Lakes, Kootenay Lake, later Kinbasket); PAC → BC: half of the extra downstream power, paid in power and sold back to the Realm's grid for gold dollars, with payments for flood control; no dam flooding across the line without BC's consent and compensation; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the same half under a power compact between Pacifica and Caledonia, pledged to the bond refunding
- **1927 San Francisco rescue credit to Japan** (loan, 1927–1933, §33, CANON, CR-024) — owes: PAC → JP: a $600m credit, a third from the Treasury and two-thirds from private lenders, drawn only as Japan needs it; JP → PAC: the Japanese government's guarantee; repayment by the rebuilt banks through a stabilization agency in five instalments from 1929 to 1933 — can end or refuse: PAC: the Senate votes the rescue loan
- **Provisional application of the Pacific Concord** (treaty, 1927–1928, §33, APPROVED, CR-022) — can end or refuse: PAC: the Senate refused the treaty; provisional application is illegal in substance until consent; JP: Japan's side follows Japanese law and can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent
