- **Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi)** (treaty, 1927–, §33, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: sixty-five percent of common costs (quota 65/35); Foreign Affairs, the common Navy and their Finance held in common; JP → PAC: thirty-five percent quota voted by the Diet; at least one common minister Japanese by law; Japan's prime minister countersigns appointments; PAC → JP: the western squadron reserved to the Japanese naval party until the 1932 renewal; from 1 August 1932 the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL); JP → PAC: the home army enters common command only for a Concord-declared war; CROWN → JP: annual Council of the Two Crowns at Hayama from 1928; Prince Chichibu resides at Thalassia as the Emperor's representative between Councils — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: Japan's Emperor ratifies on the Privy Council's conditional advice; its side can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent; PAC: two-thirds Senate consent, won in 1928; CROWN: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan holds no date at which it may refuse
- **Ka Lōkahi trust of the Marianas, Carolines and Palau** (settlement, 1927–, §33, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: LOKAHI → MICRO: trusteeship of the Marianas, Carolines and Palau (and, PROPOSED, Tutuila); LOKAHI → FANNING: from 1932, trusteeship of Fanning and Washington under the published charter — renewals: 1937
- **Guam harbour lease** (lease, 1927–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: LOKAHI → PAC: Guam's harbour on lease to the navy
- **Kamchatka Company charter** (charter, 1927–1947, §33, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: JP → PAC: a third of the national-policy company's shares in Pacifican hands — can end or refuse: KAM: the charter is given with the consent of the Republic of Kamchatka (proposed); KR: Korean workers' paid contracts require Seoul's consent (proposed arrangements); PAC: Korean workers' paid contracts require Pacifica's consent (proposed arrangements)
- **The 1927 settlement's oil terms** (settlement, 1927–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-053) — owes: UK → PAC: waives the sheikhdoms' nationality clause for the Pacifican company; consents to an enlarged American group in Mesopotamia; PAC → US: joins the Union's group in Mesopotamia rather than displacing it; keeps the Red Line agreement of 1928 as a shared discipline
- **The gold cover behind Japan's reserves in San Francisco** (compact, 1927–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057) — owes: PAC → JP: Japan's gold earmarked and withdrawable without notice; half the value of its dollar balances held in gold — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: neither ratio can be lowered without Japan's consent
- **The Concord's industrial frame (the Hayama annex)** (convention, 1928–1937, §33, APPROVED, CR-054) — owes: PAC → JP: supplies the Concord's aluminium, magnesium, oil products, electrical equipment, heavy machinery and the common fleet's aircraft; sells no fertiliser, textiles or ships' fittings into Japan's home market; licenses the all-metal aircraft line at Nagoya and Ōta (C-1928-JP-AIRCRAFT-LINE); JP → PAC: supplies textiles, its own nitrogen and the silk and consumer trades; builds no aluminium or heavy electrical industry against Pacifica's before the Concord's review of 1937; buys Pacifican equipment for its hydro; keeps to the single party in the European nitrogen convention — renewals: 1937
- **The Gulf's provisioning compact** (compact, 1928–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → GULF: Realm credit for the Gulf's rice, feed and drainage
- **The licensed all-metal aircraft line in Japan** (convention, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: Pacifica's published licence at the open royalty, and aluminium sheet from the Concord pool; JP → PAC: the line builds for the home army's air service and for civil lines; the common fleet's aircraft stay Pacifica's to supply
- **Tokyo Atomic Power Station Split Financing Agreement** (compact, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: TOKYO-DENTO → JP: finances and owns the site, the civil containment, the cooling works, the turbines, the alternators and the grid ties, all paid in yen; PAC → TOKYO-DENTO: the Works supplies the pile, the purified graphite, the control gear and the uranium charge; TOKYO-DENTO → PAC: the Works' outlay repaid through a lease of fuel and core charged by the kilowatt-hour for twenty years from first power (C-1931-TOKYO-FUEL-LEASE)
- **Kolyma gold concessions** (concession, 1929–, §32, CANON) — owes: SIB → PAC: concession companies working Kolyma gold with Pacifican dredges sell their gold to the Reserve Bank; the gold is Russian production
- **The Concord in the European nitrogen convention** (convention, 1930–, §33, APPROVED, CR-054) — owes: NITROCONV → PAC: London and Ludwigshafen recognise the Concord's Asian market; PAC → NITROCONV: the Concord joins as a single party
- **Hoover's war-debt moratorium** (settlement, 1931–1932, §34, CANON) — owes: PAC → ENTENTE: suspension of war-debt payments; debt is restructured
- **Second gold rescue of the Union** (loan, 1931–, §34, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: gold and debt rescue; US → PAC: acceptance of the continental settlement at Thalassia
- **Fuel lease for the Tokyo atomic station** (lease, 1931–1951, §33, APPROVED, CR-050) — owes: PAC → JP: fuel for the Tokyo station, leased from the Light and Matter Works; JP → PAC: the spent fuel, returned to Pacifica; TOKYO-DENTO → PAC: the lease of fuel and core, charged by the kilowatt-hour for twenty years from first power — can end or refuse: PAC: the Works' duty to deliver fresh cores ends if the union is ever dissolved; all irradiated fuel still returns
- **Guarantee of the dominions' crop** (guarantee, 1931–, §37, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → NZ: guarantee of the crop; PAC → NZ: from the treaty of 1932, guarantee that no hostile base stands on its soil
- **Gulf oil compact** (compact, 1932–, §34, CANON) — owes: PAC → GULF: help to support prices, without admitting the Republic; a floor under the price rather than cut output; PAC → GULF: PROPOSED (CR-084): for five years, 1932 to 1937, the oil trust's liftings of Gulf crude do not fall below a stated share of its average of 1929–31, at posted prices never below the compact's floor and inside the fifty-five percent ceiling; countersigned by the Realm with two-thirds of the Senate — renewals: 1937
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **Australia–New Zealand defense treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → ANZ: the guarantee that no hostile base stands on the dominions' soil; by reference, the Concord's protocol on the tenth parallel south and the bar on fortifying the leased ports in the Indies
- **Westminster's Recognition and Severance Act and the Bamfield cable lease** (settlement, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: UK → CLD: recognition and severance by Act of Parliament on the legislature's request and consent; in force by a commencement order (about 15 July 1932); PAC → UK: two annuities of the debt funded in 1923 cancelled from the near end of the schedule (C-1923-UK-FUNDING; the first of three tranches); PAC → IIC: guarantee of a long commercial lease of the Bamfield Pacific Cable station; CLD → UK: the Island's entrenched guarantees: Victoria the capital, the Island's provincial autonomy, Crown pensions protected, and a two-year term for keeping British nationality (the term PROPOSED)
- **The Pacifica–Caledonia Association Treaty, its admission clause and the bond refunding** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → CLD: gold-dollar reserve clearing in San Francisco; entry and residence for Caledonians in the Realm under its ordinary law; CLD → PAC: chartered industrial districts at Burrard Inlet, Prince Rupert and Trail; entry and residence for the Realm's citizens; a perpetual corridor to Alaska for rail, road and cable; hydroelectricity exported south; CLD → PAC: succession to the dominion's seal, western-waters, Columbia and halibut treaties; CLD → PAC: service of the refunded railway bonds to private San Francisco syndicates, secured on flowing revenue — secured on: for the refunding: Caledonia's half of the Columbia's downstream power, the port tolls of Burrard Inlet and Prince Rupert, new contracts for exported power, and the royalties of the Yukon and the Kootenays — can end or refuse: PAC: admission is considered only after twenty-five consecutive years of measured outcomes; Pacifica may demand a second generation; admission must add neither risk nor a veto; both peoples must vote to unite
- **British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → CROWN: ends for good the fortification of Singapore for capital ships and the heavy dry-dock work at Sembawang; a peacetime cap on its capital ships east of Ceylon (cruisers and sloops unrestricted); UK → JP: the same halt and cap, the Concord signing for both crowns; CROWN → UK: a maritime pledge of non-aggression toward Malaya and its sea lanes, covering beyond that only attack from the sea by an outside power on the Strait of Malacca and on the commerce of Malaya and the Indian Ocean; never order inside the colonies; JP → UK: the same pledge; PAC → UK: the Realm's own countersignature on the pledge (two-thirds of the Senate); UK → CROWN: retrocession of Labuan to Brunei subject to Labuan's vote, and consent to the Labuan Harbour Lease; detachment of Fanning and Washington into Ka Lōkahi's trust, the Fanning cable station leased back; quitclaim of its remaining claims in the Phoenix group; release of Tonga from the protectorate of 1900; CROWN → AU: protocol: no warship stationed and no new base built south of 10° S between Timor and 155° W (meridian PROPOSED), none sought in Portuguese Timor; no naval fortification or permanently berthed warship at the leased ports of Bintan and East Borneo; CROWN → NZ: the same protocol; JP → AU: the same protocol, which Japan signs as a crown of the Concord; UK → NZ: PROPOSED: New Zealand's contribution to the Singapore base rebated or credited against its war debt to Britain (sum OPEN); PAC → UK: eleven of the seventeen cancelled annuities of C-1923-UK-FUNDING fall on the deposit of the King's ratification of this treaty (MODEL) — can end or refuse: UK: reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED)
- **Thalassia Adjustable Gold-Parity Agreement (the yen)** (compact, 1932–, §36, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: an orderly reset of the yen's parity downward, inside a consultation band under Common Finance; JP → PAC: consultation before any change of parity; the Bank of Japan's relief lending kept under a ceiling in gold
- **Concord Atomic Power Board (charter)** (charter, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: the Board builds and runs the stations in Japan and builds the Kansai station; a published tariff formula indexed only to Tokyo's cost of generating from delivered steam coal, less an agreed discount; JP → PAC: no plant in Japan for treating irradiated fuel; every station's spent fuel returns to the Works; KANSAI-UTILITIES → PAC: the Kansai station's civil works and turbines owned in yen; the pile and the fuel leased from the Works
- **Pacifica–Hokkaido marine agronomy and kelp research compact** (compact, 1932–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: HOKKAIDO-AG → ESTATES: joint research on kombu and kelp farming with the Realm's marine stations; ESTATES → HOKKAIDO-AG: what the wartime plant at San Diego learned of harvesting kelp by machine
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate
- **The private houses' Pampas contracts** (concession, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: GRAIN-HOUSES → AR-EXPORTERS: carriage, finance and insurance of Argentina's wheat, maize and linseed to third markets; the Columbia's nitrogen and the champions' machines sold to it; PAC → GRAIN-HOUSES: the contracts are scheduled to Article II as contracts the Realm will not itself interrupt; the Realm puts no money behind them

### Kingdom and crown of Hawaii

- **Kuhina Nui (Victoria)** (office, 1855–1872, §6, CANON)
- **Treaty of Honolulu** (treaty, 1855–, §6, CANON) — owes: HI → PAC: a Californian coaling station at Honolulu; HI → PAC: customs reciprocity; PAC → HI: customs reciprocity
- **Articles of the Confederation of Pacifica** (compact, 1856–1887, §8, CANON) — owes: CAL → PAC: defense, the navy, customs, currency and external relations to the common institutions; HI → PAC: defense, the navy, customs, currency and external relations to the common institutions; PAC → HI: domestic self-government and residual powers; Hawaiian-language institutions; Honolulu remains the Hawaiian seat; PAC → CAL: domestic self-government and residual powers; English–Spanish guarantees — can end or refuse: CAL: ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change; HI: ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change
- **Article XIV (emergency Protector)** (constitution, 1856–, §8, CANON) — can end or refuse: CAL: member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal; HI: member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal (withheld in 1860); PAC: Council two-thirds vote to appoint; the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures
- **The Confederation's note on the Declaration of Paris** (guarantee, 1856–1919, §8, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **Protectorship of Elias Thorne** (office, 1861–1865, §9, CANON) — renewals: 1862, 1863, 1864 — can end or refuse: CAL: renewal requires the member legislature; HI: renewal requires the member legislature; PAC: the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures
- **Hawaiian constitution of 1864** (constitution, 1864–, §12, CANON)
- **Enabling Law** (law, 1865–1887, §12, CANON)
- **Hawaiian guarantees of the Enabling Law** (guarantee, 1865–, §12, APPROVED, CR-068) — owes: PAC → HI: protection of Hawaiian kingship, the Kuhina Nui, local institutions and agreed revenues, as protected provisions that cabinet legislation may not touch
- **Pacific article (Guano Islands)** (treaty, 1867–, §12, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: US → PAC: renunciation of Guano Islands Act claims to Midway, Baker, Howland, Jarvis, Johnston and Kingman; quitclaim to Palmyra; PAC → US: the New York guano companies keep their workings until the deposits are exhausted and land cargoes duty-free at Hawaiian ports
- **Midway naval ground lease** (lease, 1867–, §14, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: HI → PAC: lease of Midway's naval ground (Midway part of the Hawaiian Crown lands)
- **Mutual waiver and the mixed claims commission** (settlement, 1867–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-062) — owes: US → PAC: the Union's claims from the war waived; PAC → US: Pacifica's claims from the war waived; PAC → HI: the family of the Hawaiian sailor killed in 1861 keeps its own claim before the commission
- **Ruth Keʻelikōlani as Kuhina Nui** (office, 1872–1876, §17, APPROVED, CR-027)
- **Puʻuloa Convention** (lease, 1874–1924, §17, APPROVED, CR-027) — owes: HI → PAC: lease of Pearl Harbor and its shores; no Hawaiian land alienated; PAC → HI: common defence — can end or refuse: HI: the legislature ratified it narrowly; a lease, not a cession
- **Confederal ordinances and the Territorial Council** (law, 1874–1907, §17, APPROVED, CR-027) — owes: HI → PAC: (imposed) harbour police, the Honolulu port administration, the defence districts and, from 1876, an appointed Territorial Council that takes over the legislature's business — can end or refuse: HI: Hawaii's Supreme Court declares the ordinances void; the judgment is never enforced or reversed
- **Kāneʻohe capital lease** (lease, c.1875–, §17, CANON) — owes: HI → PAC: lease of the Kāneʻohe capital ground from the Hawaiian Crown lands
- **1878 Pago Pago treaty** (treaty, 1878–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SAMOA → PAC: Pago Pago (terms not stated), signed by Hawaii on the Confederation's behalf
- **Constitution of the Realm** (constitution, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → HI: entrenched language and tenure guarantees for Hawaii as a historic land (no veto); PAC → COUNTIES: land taxes, roads, sanitation, primary schools and ordinary policing left to county boards; PAC → CAL: California a historic land: Spanish entrenched, community property, the Mexican grants as organic law (CR-071 G6.2)
- **Midway cable-station lease from the Hawaiian Crown** (lease, 1889–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: HI → PAC: a cable-station lease on Midway, Hawaiian Crown land
- **Act of Settlement of 1907** (constitution, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → CROWN: establishes the Crown of Pacifica in the House of Thorne-Kamehameha; Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty; HI → CROWN: joins the Hawaiian crown to the Crown of Pacifica; the ʻAha Aliʻi proclaims Julian heir to the Hawaiian crown as Kamehameha VI; PAC → HI: dissolves the Territorial Council and restores Hawaii's legislature; Hawaiian restored as the Crown's language and in its schools; ʻAha Aliʻi keeps custody of Hawaiian crown and kuleana lands; Thalassia's ground confirmed as land of the Hawaiian throne — can end or refuse: HI: the union is put to a plebiscite of Hawaiian subjects by the restored legislature and the ʻAha Aliʻi; PAC: Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty by two-thirds
- **Julian's crowns (Sovereign of Pacifica, Kamehameha VI)** (office, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040)
- **Ka Lōkahi, the council of the crowns** (compact, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040) — renewals: 1917, 1927 — can end or refuse: PAC: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes; HI: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes; PH: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes
- **The Hawaiian harbour act (warnings)** (law, 1913–, §33, APPROVED, CR-078)
- **Puʻuloa tenancy at the Mōʻī's pleasure** (lease, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: HI → PAC: the fleet keeps its base at Puʻuloa as the Hawaiian crown's tenant; PAC → HI: restore Puʻuloa's fishponds and pearl beds; no dredging in Kaʻahupahau's waters — can end or refuse: HI: held at the Mōʻī's pleasure; renewed at each accession
- **Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi)** (treaty, 1927–, §33, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: sixty-five percent of common costs (quota 65/35); Foreign Affairs, the common Navy and their Finance held in common; JP → PAC: thirty-five percent quota voted by the Diet; at least one common minister Japanese by law; Japan's prime minister countersigns appointments; PAC → JP: the western squadron reserved to the Japanese naval party until the 1932 renewal; from 1 August 1932 the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL); JP → PAC: the home army enters common command only for a Concord-declared war; CROWN → JP: annual Council of the Two Crowns at Hayama from 1928; Prince Chichibu resides at Thalassia as the Emperor's representative between Councils — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: Japan's Emperor ratifies on the Privy Council's conditional advice; its side can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent; PAC: two-thirds Senate consent, won in 1928; CROWN: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan holds no date at which it may refuse
- **Thalassia Plan** (law, 1928–, §33, CANON) — owes: COUNTIES → HI: public land stays leasehold; county administration of leases does not transfer ownership or the ʻAha Aliʻi's custody of Hawaiian lands; CROWN → HI: on Thalassia's Hawaiian Crown ground the Mōʻī receives the premiums, which fund parks, transit and schools
- **The Hawaiian crown's leeward districts** (charter, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Lands and Waters Charter** (charter, 1932–, §36, CANON, CR-020) — owes: HI → CROWN: watershed reserves and minimum stream flows, two leeward industrial districts, restoration of mined land, leasehold for public land, the statistics bureau's annual islands index; Waimanu protected; HI → CROWN: the smelter keeps its contracts at gold parity
- **Concord–Tonga Treaty of Friendship and Maritime Security** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → TO: release from the protectorate of 1900, with Tonga's consent; HI → TO: Julian, as Mōʻī of Hawaii, is the treaty's guarantor, on the advice of the Hawaiian privy council; CROWN → TO: PROPOSED: no foreign garrison or base on Tongan soil; JP → TO: PROPOSED: the same bar

### Japan

- **Shimoda agreement** (pact, 1855–, §7, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: Californian machinists visit Satsuma's Kagoshima works; PAC → JP: Japanese shipwrights, naval students and artillery specialists train at California's yards
- **Treaty of Edo** (treaty, 1858–, §7, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: partnership in commerce, shipbuilding, coal, technical exchange and naval training; JP → PAC: technical advisers, training, observers, markets, harbors and resources
- **Guarantee of the negotiated transition at Edo** (guarantee, 1868–, §14, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → JP: fleet guarantee of the negotiated transition at Edo
- **Japanese alliance of 1870–1871** (alliance, 1870–, §14, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: full Japanese tariff autonomy and no consular courts; JP → PAC: dockyard rights at Yokosuka and a coaling lease at Hakodate; PAC → JP: foreign credit (direction as listed; not spelled out)
- **Japan's loan of 1873 (its San Francisco part)** (loan, 1873–, §14, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: JP → PAC: interest and repayment on the Republic's share of the part placed in San Francisco; JP → SANFRANCISCO: interest and repayment on the houses' share, Japan's bank being the Bank of California (CR-073 E2, the 1867 precedent)
- **Vladivostok settlement folded into the Congress of Berlin** (settlement, 1878–, §16, PROPOSED) — owes: RU → JP: restoration of Southern Sakhalin; UK → PAC: recognition of the Philippine purchase
- **Ryukyu mediation** (settlement, 1879–, §15, PROPOSED)
- **The four-power fur-seal convention** (convention, 1886–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: fifteen percent of the Pribilof catch; RU → BC: fifteen percent of the Commander Islands' catch, 1886–1921; PAC → BC: from 1921, with the Commander Islands, fifteen percent of their catch; JP → BC: ten percent of Robben Island's catch; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the dominion's shares, by succession under the Association Treaty
- **Compact with Tokyo** (compact, 1887–1927, §24, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → PAC: backs the regency and holds its officers to the agreement; PAC → JP: guarantee against Russia and support for treaty revision with Europe; PAC → NAVAL: command of the western squadron reserved to officers of the Japanese naval party — renewals: 1897, 1907, 1917 — can end or refuse: JP: ratifies each ten-year renewal; PAC: ratifies each ten-year renewal
- **Japanese landing grants for the southern wire at Chichijima and Tokyo Bay** (concession, 1892–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: JP → PAC: landings at Chichijima and on Tokyo Bay for the southern wire, under the compact of 1887
- **Hokkaido Cold-Hardy Rice Breeding Exchange** (compact, 1893–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: the Realm's stations cross cold-hardy rice with Hokkaido's and give winter nurseries in Hawaiʻi, which halves the breeding cycle; HOKKAIDO-AG → ESTATES: Hokkaido's test station and Sapporo Agricultural College share the crossing and its lines
- **Korean armistice brokered by the Regent** (treaty, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: Chinese suzerainty ended; CN → JP: a moderate indemnity and commercial rights; Japanese economic predominance in Korea
- **Guarantee of Korean independence and neutrality** (guarantee, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; JP → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; PAC → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality
- **Formosan Revenue Convention** (convention, 1895–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → PAC: a Pacifican Director-General nominated by the Regent, holding a Qing commission, controls the camphor monopoly, Keelung mines, Formosan customs and the Keelung harbour trust (pilots, lights, works and coaling rights); CN → JP: receipts pay the indemnity and Japan; CN → PAC: Pacifica's fee from receipts, before the provincial treasury — secured on: camphor monopoly, Keelung mines, Formosan customs and Keelung harbour trust
- **Indemnity facility and gold loan for Japan's gold transition** (loan, 1897–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → JP: a smaller indemnity facility and a gold loan supporting Japan's gold transition
- **The warnings compact (earthquakes at sea and typhoons)** (compact, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: PAC → JP: typhoon warnings: the Manila Observatory, with Guam and Chichijima, wires the storm tracks north to Japan; JP → PAC: warnings of offshore earthquakes and tide-gauge readings, wired outward to Honolulu by Ōmori Fusakichi and the Central Meteorological Observatory, by the southern wire and from 1903 by the Kuril cable
- **Manchurian railway section: Japanese running rights and wartime railway board** (concession, c.1899–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → JP: running rights on the Chinese-owned Manchurian section; CN → PAC: a Pacifican railway board in wartime
- **Cable landings by the Kurils at Hakodate** (concession, 1903–, §25, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: JP → PAC: landing of the Sitka cable by Attu and the Kurils at Hakodate, on Japanese soil
- **Japanese war loans raised in San Francisco** (loan, c.1904–, §26, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → SANFRANCISCO: service of the war loans Takahashi raises
- **Russo-Japanese peace (Witte's terms)** (treaty, 1905–, §26, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: RU → JP: withdrawal from Manchuria, an indemnity, recognition of Japan's southern gains and renunciation of claims in Korea
- **Japan's agreed military and transit rights in Korea** (treaty, 1905–, §26, CANON) — owes: KR → JP: agreed military and transit rights; Japanese economic predominance; coordinated foreign policy
- **1907 renewal of the compact with Tokyo** (compact, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-022) — owes: PAC → JP: Kiyoshi as Japanese Fleet-General; a joint war council; family immigration; civic equality for Japanese Pacificans
- **Entente loans (wartime advances)** (loan, 1914–, §30, CANON, CR-025) — owes: ENTENTE → PAC: repayment of the advances gathered from the Treasury, Pacifican savers and foreign subscriptions through the San Francisco syndicates; ENTENTE → US: Union investors buying through Pacifican correspondents keep their own claims and their own risk; ENTENTE → JP: repayment of Japan's lending to Russia from 1916 and to Britain and France from 1917 — secured on: from the end of 1916, the Allies' requisitioned foreign securities, many of them Pacifican bonds (the collateral loans)
- **The Sino-Japanese treaties of 25 May 1915** (treaty, 1915–, §28, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: commercial rights along the Manchurian line under China's law and police; confirmation of the Daye ore contract; JP → CN: an effective 5 per cent from the tariff revision of 1918; PAC → CN: an effective 5 per cent from the tariff revision of 1918
- **Siberian coalition** (alliance, 1918–, §31, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: the mass of the army in the east; CN → PAC: manpower and the Manchurian railway; PAC → SIB: command, finance, railway organization, artillery, armored trains, aircraft, river flotillas and the expeditionary corps
- **Manchurian railway running rights and transit** (concession, 1918–, §31, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: CN → JP: running rights and, later, guaranteed transit on the Chinese-owned line, without giving its territory to Japan; PAC → CN: the railway returns to Chinese management, improved at the coalition's expense
- **The coalition's agreement with Peking on the Chinese line (the Khingan agreement)** (pact, 1918–, §31, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The Treaty of The Hague** (treaty, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: evacuation of Belgium and northern France; no indemnity; DE → PAC: loss of its colonies; Pacifica and Japan retain Germany's Pacific islands; DE → FR: Alsace-Lorraine given up, not to France: a neutral republic in union with Switzerland (C-1919-ALSACE-LORRAINE); PAC → CN: return of Shandong apart from the Tsingtao anchorage; UK → DE: recognition of Germany's clients in Poland, the Baltic region, Ukraine and Finland; AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918 (C-1919-BOHEMIA)
- **Tsingtao anchorage lease** (lease, 1919–, §30, CANON) — owes: CN → PAC: the Tsingtao anchorage on lease; PAC → CN: return of the rest of Shandong, denying Japan the prize
- **Permanent arbitration court** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: PAC → JP: the settlement carries Japan's racial equality clause — renewals: 1932
- **1919 naval limits** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: UK → PAC: parity: the combined Pacifica–Japan fleets count at parity with Britain; DE → PAC: Germany below Britain and the Pacific partners; France and Italy below Germany; UK → PAC: disposal of older twelve-inch ships by 1922, through scrapping, training hulks or targets (Britain and Germany)
- **Treaty of Harbin** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: RU → SIB: Siberia, a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote, holds the country from the Ob to Lake Baikal; the Okhotsk coast west of Ola Bay and Nikolayevsk (APPROVED); Yakutia, Chukotka, the Arctic coast and the islands east of the Kara Sea (PROPOSED); SIB → RU: entry into the federal compact by the vote of the Regional Duma at Tomsk; the Siberia–Alash union of 1918 dissolved in the same act; RU → ALASH: a land of Russia keeping its courts and kibitka tax, without its army or its equal standing; Alash is not asked; RU → PAC: concessions at Grozny and Maikop against the reconstruction credit; AZ → PAC: a joint Anglo-Pacifican concession at Baku: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; RU → JP: a client sphere in Transbaikalia around Chita; RU → CN: the Amur, a sphere in Outer Mongolia and Tuva; renunciation of extraterritorial rights, concessions and the Boxer indemnity share (to Chinese schools); Mongolia back under Chinese suzerainty under its own Mongol government; SIB → CN: passage for Chinese ships down the Amur to the sea; CN → SIB: Siberian trade and settlement rights in the Chinese spheres; SIB → JP: a Siberian trial of the Nikolayevsk partisan leaders and an indemnity; JP → SIB: passage for Siberian ships through the straits of a sea now Japanese on three sides; SIB → PAC: the straits into the Kara Sea open to merchant ships; PAC → RU: gives up the old claims to Wrangel Island and the De Long islands, taking the Commander Islands instead; JP → SIB: renunciation of the pan-Mongol schemes Japan once backed
- **Japanese protection of the Kamchatka republic** (guarantee, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-031) — owes: JP → KAM: protection of the republic of Kamchatka and the Koryak coast as far as Ola Bay; RU → KAM: Harbin recognises the republic and its Resident
- **Transbaikalian client sphere (protectorate)** (settlement, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → TBK: a civilian Japanese Resident over a council of Buryats, Cossacks and townsmen; Japan renounces the pan-Mongol schemes; TBK → JP: Japanese settlers, the Chernovsky coal, the Nerchinsk mines and the junction of the line to Manchuria
- **Siberian coast fisheries convention** (convention, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → JP: the majority of the fishing lots on the Siberian coast for Japanese boats
- **The Mukden protocol of 1921** (settlement, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: running rights confirmed at the line's published rates, the same for every shipper and every destination and for the crop as for any freight; Siberian grain in transit to Huludao on the same rates
- **Standing alliance of Pacifica, Japan, China and federal Russia** (alliance, 1922–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → RU: command of the alliance and its finance; reconstruction credit in stages, against results; PAC → SIB: a guarantee of the federal compact and of Siberia's borders, keeping a smaller claim on the railway in reserve for war (MODEL)
- **The Shandong settlement (agreed 1919, carried out 1922)** (settlement, 1922–, §30, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: Japanese treasury-note credit repaid for the railway; a Japanese traffic manager and a joint chief accountant until the notes are redeemed; JP → CN: the railway returned
- **Yokohama relief and reconstruction credit** (loan, 1923–, §33, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: repayment of the reconstruction credit
- **1927 San Francisco rescue credit to Japan** (loan, 1927–1933, §33, CANON, CR-024) — owes: PAC → JP: a $600m credit, a third from the Treasury and two-thirds from private lenders, drawn only as Japan needs it; JP → PAC: the Japanese government's guarantee; repayment by the rebuilt banks through a stabilization agency in five instalments from 1929 to 1933 — can end or refuse: PAC: the Senate votes the rescue loan
- **Provisional application of the Pacific Concord** (treaty, 1927–1928, §33, APPROVED, CR-022) — can end or refuse: PAC: the Senate refused the treaty; provisional application is illegal in substance until consent; JP: Japan's side follows Japanese law and can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent
- **Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi)** (treaty, 1927–, §33, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: sixty-five percent of common costs (quota 65/35); Foreign Affairs, the common Navy and their Finance held in common; JP → PAC: thirty-five percent quota voted by the Diet; at least one common minister Japanese by law; Japan's prime minister countersigns appointments; PAC → JP: the western squadron reserved to the Japanese naval party until the 1932 renewal; from 1 August 1932 the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL); JP → PAC: the home army enters common command only for a Concord-declared war; CROWN → JP: annual Council of the Two Crowns at Hayama from 1928; Prince Chichibu resides at Thalassia as the Emperor's representative between Councils — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: Japan's Emperor ratifies on the Privy Council's conditional advice; its side can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent; PAC: two-thirds Senate consent, won in 1928; CROWN: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan holds no date at which it may refuse
- **Concord executive trust (first term)** (office, 1927–1932, §33, CANON) — owes: JP → CROWN: the Concord's executive entrusted to the Crown of Pacifica, because Pacifica pays the larger quota and commands the fleet — renewals: 1932
- **Kamchatka Company charter** (charter, 1927–1947, §33, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: JP → PAC: a third of the national-policy company's shares in Pacifican hands — can end or refuse: KAM: the charter is given with the consent of the Republic of Kamchatka (proposed); KR: Korean workers' paid contracts require Seoul's consent (proposed arrangements); PAC: Korean workers' paid contracts require Pacifica's consent (proposed arrangements)
- **The gold cover behind Japan's reserves in San Francisco** (compact, 1927–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057) — owes: PAC → JP: Japan's gold earmarked and withdrawable without notice; half the value of its dollar balances held in gold — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: neither ratio can be lowered without Japan's consent
- **Treaty of Nanjing** (treaty, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: CROWN → CN: the Concord guarantees China's frontiers, Manchuria included; JP → CN: the Concord guarantees China's frontiers, Manchuria included; CN → JP: railway transit and its joint management; CN → JP: the joint management sets no rate that favours a shipper or a destination (CR-081)
- **The Concord's industrial frame (the Hayama annex)** (convention, 1928–1937, §33, APPROVED, CR-054) — owes: PAC → JP: supplies the Concord's aluminium, magnesium, oil products, electrical equipment, heavy machinery and the common fleet's aircraft; sells no fertiliser, textiles or ships' fittings into Japan's home market; licenses the all-metal aircraft line at Nagoya and Ōta (C-1928-JP-AIRCRAFT-LINE); JP → PAC: supplies textiles, its own nitrogen and the silk and consumer trades; builds no aluminium or heavy electrical industry against Pacifica's before the Concord's review of 1937; buys Pacifican equipment for its hydro; keeps to the single party in the European nitrogen convention — renewals: 1937
- **The Jinan withdrawal settlement of 1928** (settlement, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: the safety of Japanese residents and property; provincial garrisons at Jinan and on the railway zone; a joint claims commission; JP → CN: withdrawal within two months
- **China's accession to the standing alliance under the Treaty of Nanjing** (alliance, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-081)
- **The licensed all-metal aircraft line in Japan** (convention, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: Pacifica's published licence at the open royalty, and aluminium sheet from the Concord pool; JP → PAC: the line builds for the home army's air service and for civil lines; the common fleet's aircraft stay Pacifica's to supply
- **Tokyo Atomic Power Station Split Financing Agreement** (compact, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: TOKYO-DENTO → JP: finances and owns the site, the civil containment, the cooling works, the turbines, the alternators and the grid ties, all paid in yen; PAC → TOKYO-DENTO: the Works supplies the pile, the purified graphite, the control gear and the uranium charge; TOKYO-DENTO → PAC: the Works' outlay repaid through a lease of fuel and core charged by the kilowatt-hour for twenty years from first power (C-1931-TOKYO-FUEL-LEASE)
- **The soybean compact** (treaty, 1929–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → CROWN: the crop travels at published rates on every Manchurian line; CN → JP: the crop travels at published rates on every Manchurian line; CROWN → CN: the Concord buys the crop by contract and never by land; JP → CN: the Concord buys the crop by contract and never by land
- **The Concord in the European nitrogen convention** (convention, 1930–, §33, APPROVED, CR-054) — owes: NITROCONV → PAC: London and Ludwigshafen recognise the Concord's Asian market; PAC → NITROCONV: the Concord joins as a single party
- **The end of the Concord's consular jurisdiction in China** (settlement, 1930–1940, §34, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The exchange of notes of 1930 on Japan's goods** (settlement, 1930–1933, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: duties on Japanese cotton goods and marine products bound for three years; CROWN → CN: acceptance of China's tariff autonomy; no schedule for Pacifican goods
- **Fuel lease for the Tokyo atomic station** (lease, 1931–1951, §33, APPROVED, CR-050) — owes: PAC → JP: fuel for the Tokyo station, leased from the Light and Matter Works; JP → PAC: the spent fuel, returned to Pacifica; TOKYO-DENTO → PAC: the lease of fuel and core, charged by the kilowatt-hour for twenty years from first power — can end or refuse: PAC: the Works' duty to deliver fresh cores ends if the union is ever dissolved; all irradiated fuel still returns
- **1932 renewal of the Concord without term** (office, 1932–, §36, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: JP → CROWN: the executive held by the Crown of Pacifica until both crowns agree otherwise at a review; Japan's overseas commands pass to common control; JP → CROWN: the naval party loses its reserved squadron as command is consolidated; CROWN → JP: the home army stays national except in a Concord war; CROWN → JP: a review every five years, the first in the summer of 1937, at which the quota, the common seats, the pool shares and the parities are open; failing agreement the terms in force continue, except that the quota moves to the share the joint audit gives; CROWN → JP: the Navy seat by treaty law, the yards' share, pool entry at the first review, the Atomic Power Board and the yen's reset in principle (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL, C-1932-HAYAMA-GRADUATION, C-1932-ATOMIC-POWER-BOARD; carried out for the yen by C-1932-ADJUSTABLE-PARITY) — renewals: 1937, 1942 — can end or refuse: CROWN: the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan may raise the alternating executive at any review and cannot bring it about by refusing
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **The Concord's external guarantee of Caledonia** (guarantee, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CROWN → CLD: external guarantee of Caledonia, kept by the common Navy; JP → CLD: the same guarantee, as the Concord's other crown; CLD → JP: entry and residence for Japanese subjects, reciprocal with Caledonians' in Japan; the floors kept for those resident on Proclamation Day who stay Japanese; no vote by the clause; CLD → CROWN: the repair contract at Esquimalt (kept with the Caledonian yard) and the Alaska corridor's naval clauses; dockyards open to the common Navy
- **The Trilateral Treaty of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CLD → CA: perpetual running rights for the Canadian Pacific across Caledonia, a bonded Canadian quay on Burrard Inlet and transit at Prince Rupert, entrenched against revocation; CA → CROWN: a yearly quota of Pacifican farm and industrial machines outside the emergency tariff (size OPEN), open to both partners' champions; CA → CROWN: the Great Bear contract given a term of fifty years from 1932 (C-1912-GREATBEAR-OFFTAKE); CROWN → CA: a higher royalty on Great Bear's ore (rate OPEN); CA → CLD: withdrawal of Macdonald's reservation against the line of 1870
- **British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → CROWN: ends for good the fortification of Singapore for capital ships and the heavy dry-dock work at Sembawang; a peacetime cap on its capital ships east of Ceylon (cruisers and sloops unrestricted); UK → JP: the same halt and cap, the Concord signing for both crowns; CROWN → UK: a maritime pledge of non-aggression toward Malaya and its sea lanes, covering beyond that only attack from the sea by an outside power on the Strait of Malacca and on the commerce of Malaya and the Indian Ocean; never order inside the colonies; JP → UK: the same pledge; PAC → UK: the Realm's own countersignature on the pledge (two-thirds of the Senate); UK → CROWN: retrocession of Labuan to Brunei subject to Labuan's vote, and consent to the Labuan Harbour Lease; detachment of Fanning and Washington into Ka Lōkahi's trust, the Fanning cable station leased back; quitclaim of its remaining claims in the Phoenix group; release of Tonga from the protectorate of 1900; CROWN → AU: protocol: no warship stationed and no new base built south of 10° S between Timor and 155° W (meridian PROPOSED), none sought in Portuguese Timor; no naval fortification or permanently berthed warship at the leased ports of Bintan and East Borneo; CROWN → NZ: the same protocol; JP → AU: the same protocol, which Japan signs as a crown of the Concord; UK → NZ: PROPOSED: New Zealand's contribution to the Singapore base rebated or credited against its war debt to Britain (sum OPEN); PAC → UK: eleven of the seventeen cancelled annuities of C-1923-UK-FUNDING fall on the deposit of the King's ratification of this treaty (MODEL) — can end or refuse: UK: reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED)
- **Labuan Harbour Lease and the Brunei auction accord** (lease, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: BN → CROWN: lease of Labuan's harbour to the Concord as a station of the common Navy on its ordinary establishment; BN → JP: the same lease: Japanese ships of the common Navy use the harbour by right, with no squadron and no commandant reserved to Japan; BN → LABUAN: municipal self-government, the option of keeping British nationality and employment in the port, as the published terms of the vote guarantee; BN → SHELL: PROPOSED: the existing lease at Seria honoured as it stands, at its old royalty; new blocks let at public auction by the Resident's administration
- **Concord–Tonga Treaty of Friendship and Maritime Security** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → TO: release from the protectorate of 1900, with Tonga's consent; HI → TO: Julian, as Mōʻī of Hawaii, is the treaty's guarantor, on the advice of the Hawaiian privy council; CROWN → TO: PROPOSED: no foreign garrison or base on Tongan soil; JP → TO: PROPOSED: the same bar
- **Batavia Compact** (compact, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: NL → JP: two fenced commercial loading ports leased for fifty years (bauxite on Bintan Island; oil in East Borneo), and open-door concessions on published royalties in unallocated East Borneo oil blocks and Bintan's bauxite (Sulawesi's nickel PROPOSED); NL → CROWN: the same leases and concessions, made with the Concord for both crowns; CROWN → NL: no annexation; respect for Dutch sovereignty outside the leased fences; no policing inside the colony; no naval fortification and no warship permanently berthed at the leased ports; JP → NL: the same; inside the fences only unarmed watchmen of the Japanese companies, with Dutch field police at the gate; NL → SHELL: Shell's fields and refineries at Tarakan, Balikpapan and in South Sumatra stay exactly as they are; NL → CROWN: PROPOSED: the Sunda, Lombok and Makassar straits and the Dutch shore of Malacca kept open to peaceful commerce, the clause scheduled to the second article of the Final Act
- **Common Navy Ministry Treaty-Law Protocol and the Joint Materials Board** (treaty, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: CROWN → JP: the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law, inside the three common ministries; Julian still names the minister; CROWN → JP: a Joint Materials Board with members of both partners, advising on the pool's allocations, with no say over shipping or grain charters and no power to bind; CROWN → JP: a fixed share of the common fleet's new building for Japanese yards, below capital ships, fixed to the second review in 1942 (35 per cent PROPOSED); capital ships open at the review of 1937; JP → CROWN: the reserved western squadron ends and command is consolidated — renewals: 1937, 1942
- **Hayama Pool-Entry Protocol** (convention, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: JP → CROWN: no primary aluminium smelting in Japan until the first review, in mid-1937; CROWN → JP: Japanese firms may hold up to 49 per cent of joint smelting and refining ventures outside Japan, 1932–1937; CROWN → JP: on the day the first review opens: admission of Japanese primary smelters and heavy-electrical works to the Concord pool at the inside price, a share of the Concord's orders, pool alumina contracts, and the 49 per cent stakes converted to Japanese majorities — renewals: 1937
- **Thalassia Adjustable Gold-Parity Agreement (the yen)** (compact, 1932–, §36, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: an orderly reset of the yen's parity downward, inside a consultation band under Common Finance; JP → PAC: consultation before any change of parity; the Bank of Japan's relief lending kept under a ceiling in gold
- **Concord Atomic Power Board (charter)** (charter, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: the Board builds and runs the stations in Japan and builds the Kansai station; a published tariff formula indexed only to Tokyo's cost of generating from delivered steam coal, less an agreed discount; JP → PAC: no plant in Japan for treating irradiated fuel; every station's spent fuel returns to the Works; KANSAI-UTILITIES → PAC: the Kansai station's civil works and turbines owned in yen; the pile and the fuel leased from the Works
- **Pacifica–Hokkaido marine agronomy and kelp research compact** (compact, 1932–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: HOKKAIDO-AG → ESTATES: joint research on kombu and kelp farming with the Realm's marine stations; ESTATES → HOKKAIDO-AG: what the wartime plant at San Diego learned of harvesting kelp by machine
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate
- **China's dual-track gold-exchange protocol and reserve compact** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CN → CROWN: customs assessments and new foreign borrowing on a gold-exchange unit linked to the Realm's dollar; CROWN → CN: the Realm's existing silver loans stay in silver; a reserve compact of its own in San Francisco on the terms the other holders have; JP → CN: signs with the Crown of Pacifica as one party, the Concord

### The Union (United States)

- **Naval standstill with Stockton** (convention, 1846–, §4, CANON) — owes: PAC → US: American vessels may provision in Californian ports for operations against Mexico; US → PAC: American vessels may not seize Californian ports
- **Cahuenga terms** (convention, 1847–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: Frémont's battalion leaves the country; Kearny's paroled survivors embark at San Diego; amnesty for Bear Flag men who are American citizens; US → PAC: the naval standstill, in writing, until Washington replies; Stockton adheres for the squadron; PAC → US: exchange of prisoners
- **Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo (alternate)** (treaty, 1848–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MX → US: cession of New Mexico, the Great Basin and the Rio Grande line; US → PAC: respect for California's frontier as Mexico has recognized it
- **Buchanan–Vallejo Convention** (convention, 1848–1865, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: never to cede, lease or place under protection any part of its territory to a European power; PAC → US: San Francisco, Monterey and San Diego open to American warships; PAC → US: California's ports closed to the enemies of the United States in its wars; PAC → US: return of deserters from American warships; PAC → US: renunciation of the Great Basin pending a boundary commission; US → PAC: claims of 1846 closed by amnesty and a mixed commission
- **Commercial and naval articles of the Buchanan–Vallejo Convention** (convention, 1848–1860, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: most-favored-nation commerce, whalers' privileges and civil guarantees for American residents — renewals: 1860
- **American naval storehouse at Sausalito** (concession, 1848–1861, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: a revocable naval storehouse at Sausalito — renewals: 1860 — can end or refuse: PAC: the storehouse is revocable
- **Treaty of Monterey of 1850** (treaty, 1850–, §5, CANON) — owes: US → PAC: eastern boundary: 42nd parallel, 120th meridian to 39°, southeast to the Colorado at 35°N, along the Colorado to the Gila; recognized ports and access to Yuma; PAC → US: abandons claims to the Great Basin; the US retains the inland routes and the Salt Lake country
- **Carson Convention** (convention, 1860–1865, §8, CANON) — owes: PAC → US: withdrawal of troops; recognition of American sovereignty over Washoe; US → PAC: property guarantees and transit rights
- **Renewal of the 1848 commercial articles at Carson** (convention, 1860–1865, §8, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → US: the ports, the Sausalito storehouse, commerce and the rights of American residents
- **The Union's Oregon Enabling Act** (law, 1861–1866, §8, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: US → OR: statehood under a new constitution without slavery or exclusion
- **Walla Walla convention** (convention, 1862–1862, §10, PROPOSED, CR-059) — owes: PAC → US: the regulars march east with the honours of war by the Mullan Road
- **Withdrawal terms of the Fort Churchill garrison** (settlement, 1864–1864, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → US: the garrison leaves with its arms; volunteers who will not stay march east by Deseret's road, fed at Pacifica's charge
- **McClellan armistice conventions** (convention, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: no military mission and no arms to the Confederacy while the Union's armistice with Richmond holds
- **Protocol of certification of Oregon's returns** (convention, 1866–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-062)
- **Western treaty (the Western line of the 1865 peace)** (treaty, 1867–, §12, CANON) — owes: US → PAC: cession of the Northwest, Great Basin, Arizona, Utah, Nevada, Idaho, western Montana, southwest Wyoming, Colorado's Western Slope, North and Middle Park, the San Luis Valley and New Mexico to 103°W; Oregon as Oregon's voters choose; US → PAC: evacuation of Port Townsend and Fort Union after the exchange; the surviving Puget Sound enclave evacuated; PAC → US: a $40 million gold vendor credit in six instalments from the exchange (see C-1867-VENDOR-CREDIT); PAC → US: the Union's merchants keep the Columbia and Puget Sound on published terms; its warships may come in for refuge or repair; no storehouse is granted; PAC → US: named roads open from Fort Benton to Helena, from Denver to Salt Lake and from Denver over Raton Pass to Santa Fe; the Santa Fe Trail open to the Union's freighters under published duties; PAC → US: the Colorado article: any mountain mining district may vote itself over, and Pacifica supplies and coins it either way; US → PAC: the Colorado article: transit through Denver; PAC → US: every resident of a ceded district may choose either citizenship and keeps property, courts and civil protection without distinction of colour
- **Pacific article (Guano Islands)** (treaty, 1867–, §12, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: US → PAC: renunciation of Guano Islands Act claims to Midway, Baker, Howland, Jarvis, Johnston and Kingman; quitclaim to Palmyra; PAC → US: the New York guano companies keep their workings until the deposits are exhausted and land cargoes duty-free at Hawaiian ports
- **Western cession vendor credit** (loan, 1867–1872, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: $40 million in gold in six instalments from the exchange of 1867, through 1872
- **Mutual waiver and the mixed claims commission** (settlement, 1867–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-062) — owes: US → PAC: the Union's claims from the war waived; PAC → US: Pacifica's claims from the war waived; PAC → HI: the family of the Hawaiian sailor killed in 1861 keeps its own claim before the commission
- **1871 border convention (Indian Territory)** (convention, 1871–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: US → CSA: transfer of the northern Cherokee and Creek lands; the Public Land Strip remains Union
- **Peace of 1871 (Sherman–Grant)** (settlement, 1871–, §13, CANON) — owes: CSA → US: free Mississippi navigation restored; US → PAC: quitclaim of the Pacific islands Pacifica occupied during the war
- **Freedom Act (Union)** (law, 1873–, §13, CANON)
- **Rescue of the Union's gold reserve** (settlement, 1895–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: rescue of the Union's gold reserve; US → PAC: canal recognition, Asian-war neutrality and tariff concessions
- **Whitney's commerce and arbitration treaty** (treaty, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: PAC → US: return of the Bread March prisoners
- **Readmission acts for Virginia (1898) and North Carolina (1899)** (law, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: VA → US: the fundamental conditions; NC → US: the fundamental conditions
- **Joint guarantee of Cuban independence** (guarantee, c.1898–, §25, CANON) — owes: PAC → CU: guarantee of independence; GULF → CU: guarantee of independence; US → CU: guarantee of independence — can end or refuse: PAC: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors; GULF: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors; US: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors
- **Federal election supervisors in North Carolina** (law, 1899–1909, §25, APPROVED, CR-048)
- **Parchman Act (Federal Grants Loyalty Act)** (law, 1911–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048)
- **Entente loans (wartime advances)** (loan, 1914–, §30, CANON, CR-025) — owes: ENTENTE → PAC: repayment of the advances gathered from the Treasury, Pacifican savers and foreign subscriptions through the San Francisco syndicates; ENTENTE → US: Union investors buying through Pacifican correspondents keep their own claims and their own risk; ENTENTE → JP: repayment of Japan's lending to Russia from 1916 and to Britain and France from 1917 — secured on: from the end of 1916, the Allies' requisitioned foreign securities, many of them Pacifican bonds (the collateral loans)
- **Huasteca's long contracts with the Union** (concession, 1916–, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: HUASTECA → US: heavy Tampico crude for the Atlantic fuel-oil market and the navy, with no political termination clause
- **The Treaty of The Hague** (treaty, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: evacuation of Belgium and northern France; no indemnity; DE → PAC: loss of its colonies; Pacifica and Japan retain Germany's Pacific islands; DE → FR: Alsace-Lorraine given up, not to France: a neutral republic in union with Switzerland (C-1919-ALSACE-LORRAINE); PAC → CN: return of Shandong apart from the Tsingtao anchorage; UK → DE: recognition of Germany's clients in Poland, the Baltic region, Ukraine and Finland; AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918 (C-1919-BOHEMIA)
- **Alsace-Lorraine: neutral republic in union with Switzerland** (guarantee, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: the Reichsland given up to a neutral republic confirmed by a vote of its people; PAC → FR: the signatories guarantee the republic's neutrality
- **The Bohemian settlement and the legionnaires' amnesty** (settlement, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918; no partition; AH → FR: an amnesty for the Czechoslovak legionnaires, guaranteed by the treaty (PROPOSED); FR → AH: withdrawal of France's 1918 recognition of the Czechoslovak National Council (PROPOSED)
- **Permanent arbitration court** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: PAC → JP: the settlement carries Japan's racial equality clause — renewals: 1932
- **1919 naval limits** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: UK → PAC: parity: the combined Pacifica–Japan fleets count at parity with Britain; DE → PAC: Germany below Britain and the Pacific partners; France and Italy below Germany; UK → PAC: disposal of older twelve-inch ships by 1922, through scrapping, training hulks or targets (Britain and Germany)
- **The Caribbean understanding** (settlement, 1921–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → US: Venezuela stays the Union's source; Pacifica holds only its Lago minority; US → GULF: no Union naval station on the Caribbean mainland; PAC → GULF: no Pacifican base on the Caribbean mainland; canal rights are not bases
- **The 1927 settlement's oil terms** (settlement, 1927–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-053) — owes: UK → PAC: waives the sheikhdoms' nationality clause for the Pacifican company; consents to an enlarged American group in Mesopotamia; PAC → US: joins the Union's group in Mesopotamia rather than displacing it; keeps the Red Line agreement of 1928 as a shared discipline
- **Second gold rescue of the Union** (loan, 1931–, §34, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: gold and debt rescue; US → PAC: acceptance of the continental settlement at Thalassia
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **German debt settlement** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: DE → US: partial recovery for Union lenders: its debts to the Union's banks are settled, the banks taking a cut in principal (PROPOSED, CR-086)
- **Pensacola lease** (lease, 1932–, §37, OPEN, CR-019)
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate

### The Confederacy

- **Confederate renunciation of Arizona and the Sunset articles** (settlement, 1863–, §10, PROPOSED) — owes: CSA → PAC: renunciation of the Confederate Arizona claim; no Confederate soldier enters New Mexico in arms; CSA → PAC: Texas gives up its old claim to New Mexico east of the river, by a resolution of its legislature at Austin; PAC → CSA: credit and the future railway connection; PAC → CSA: use of the Sunset road, but no right to march on it; Texan freight changes to New Mexican carts at El Paso
- **Matamoros supply route** (pact, 1863–, §11, APPROVED, CR-022) — owes: PAC → CSA: credit, coin and specialist goods (rifles, machine tools, fuzes, primers and mine gear) and men, around Cape Horn to neutral Matamoros and overland into Texas; no bulk
- **Fugitive agreement with Richmond** (compact, 1863–1889, §13, CANON) — owes: PAC → CSA: fugitives refused at the crossings of the Texas line, by travel-paper controls extended in 1871–1872 to the railway and the Gulf ports; Texas may police its own side
- **The Republic's credit to Richmond** (loan, 1863–, §11, APPROVED, CR-068) — owes: PAC → CSA: credit lent at interest for independence, spent in Europe
- **McClellan armistice conventions** (convention, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → US: no military mission and no arms to the Confederacy while the Union's armistice with Richmond holds
- **Pacifica–Confederate Treaty of Amity and Commerce** (treaty, 1865–, §12, CANON) — owes: PAC → CSA: formal recognition
- **Confederate railway treaty** (treaty, 1866–, §13, CANON) — owes: CSA → PAC: a route across Texas to New Orleans for Huntington's line
- **1871 border convention (Indian Territory)** (convention, 1871–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: US → CSA: transfer of the northern Cherokee and Creek lands; the Public Land Strip remains Union
- **Peace of 1871 (Sherman–Grant)** (settlement, 1871–, §13, CANON) — owes: CSA → US: free Mississippi navigation restored; US → PAC: quitclaim of the Pacific islands Pacifica occupied during the war
- **Pacifican guarantee of Confederate independence** (guarantee, 1871–, §13, CANON) — owes: PAC → CSA: guarantee of Confederate independence
- **Association treaties** (treaty, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: PAC → CSA: refinancing of Confederate debt; CSA → PAC: customs security for the refinanced debt and Pacifican customs co-administration; CSA → PAC: the lower Mobile Bay naval station, Galveston commercial privileges, Atlantic Auxiliary recruitment and Birmingham concessions; PAC → CSA: replacement of the surrendered Mississippi toll revenue — secured on: Confederate customs
- **Confederate free-womb amendment** (constitution, 1875–, §13, CANON) — can end or refuse: CSA: the constitutional route requires state consent
- **Confederate compensation bonds (final abolition)** (loan, 1888–, §25, CANON) — owes: CSA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: coupons on long compensation bonds financing final abolition; PAC → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: Pacifica's guarantee of the coupons (it pays them when Richmond misses in 1893–1895) — secured on: Confederate customs co-administered by Pacifica
- **Protocol of Richmond (division of the Confederate debt)** (settlement, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: VA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; NC → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; GULF → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: its share of the association debt and of the compensation bonds, converted at its founding into a loan on oil royalties; CSA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: the rest: more than two-thirds of the whole, under continued customs co-administration
- **The Birmingham district** (charter, 1921–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works;
