### Gulf Republic

- **Protocol of Richmond (division of the Confederate debt)** (settlement, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: VA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; NC → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; GULF → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: its share of the association debt and of the compensation bonds, converted at its founding into a loan on oil royalties; CSA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: the rest: more than two-thirds of the whole, under continued customs co-administration
- **Joint guarantee of Cuban independence** (guarantee, c.1898–, §25, CANON) — owes: PAC → CU: guarantee of independence; GULF → CU: guarantee of independence; US → CU: guarantee of independence — can end or refuse: PAC: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors; GULF: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors; US: intervention only by agreement of all three guarantors
- **Gulf compact (conditions of admission)** (compact, 1900–, §25, CANON) — owes: GULF → PAC: meet the five measures and the two-part sixth test, registers, revenue, debt and policy conditions, each held twenty-five consecutive years, before admission is considered — can end or refuse: PAC: considers admission only if the Republic adds usable power and freedom of action without adding risk or a veto; may demand a second generation
- **Mutual defense pact with the Gulf Republic** (pact, 1900–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → GULF: mutual defense; GULF → PAC: mutual defense
- **Trade treaty with the Gulf Republic** (treaty, 1900–, §25, PROPOSED)
- **Gulf Republic's conversion of its Confederate debt share** (loan, 1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: GULF → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: service of the converted loan — secured on: Spindletop oil royalties
- **The Gulf's oil law: separation of the stages and the 55% ceiling** (compact, 1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: GULF → PAC: bans secret affiliates, rebates and price agreements; no refiner owns more than a minority of producing wells; trunk pipelines are common carriers at published tariffs; PAC → GULF: the trust refines no more than 55% of the Republic's crude and buys at posted prices
- **Recognition of Carranza and the rail transit to Douglas** (settlement, 1915–, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → MX: recognition jointly with the Gulf; rail transit for Hill's troops to Douglas; no arms; GULF → MX: recognition jointly with Pacifica; rail transit from Eagle Pass
- **Sequoyah settlement** (settlement, 1916–, §25, CANON)
- **The consented ninety-day expedition into Chihuahua** (pact, 1916–1916, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: MX → PAC: formal consent under the Apache convention's pursuit clause; liaison officers from the War Ministry; PAC → MX: ninety days, kept to the day; no fighting with Carranza's troops; GULF → PAC: a brigade under the defence pact of 1900
- **The Caribbean understanding** (settlement, 1921–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → US: Venezuela stays the Union's source; Pacifica holds only its Lago minority; US → GULF: no Union naval station on the Caribbean mainland; PAC → GULF: no Pacifican base on the Caribbean mainland; canal rights are not bases
- **The Gulf Republic's Houston district** (charter, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **The Gulf's provisioning compact** (compact, 1928–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → GULF: Realm credit for the Gulf's rice, feed and drainage
- **Gulf oil compact** (compact, 1932–, §34, CANON) — owes: PAC → GULF: help to support prices, without admitting the Republic; a floor under the price rather than cut output; PAC → GULF: PROPOSED (CR-084): for five years, 1932 to 1937, the oil trust's liftings of Gulf crude do not fall below a stated share of its average of 1929–31, at posted prices never below the compact's floor and inside the fifty-five percent ceiling; countersigned by the Realm with two-thirds of the Senate — renewals: 1937
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act

- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate

### Virginia (Confederate state to 1898, then a state of the Union)

- **Protocol of Richmond (division of the Confederate debt)** (settlement, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: VA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; NC → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: their share of the association debt, paid in cash on leaving; GULF → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: its share of the association debt and of the compensation bonds, converted at its founding into a loan on oil royalties; CSA → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: the rest: more than two-thirds of the whole, under continued customs co-administration
- **Readmission acts for Virginia (1898) and North Carolina (1899)** (law, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: VA → US: the fundamental conditions; NC → US: the fundamental conditions

### Mexico

- **Convention of Querétaro** (convention, 1847–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MX → PAC: recognition of California; PAC → MX: assumption of about £3 million of Mexican obligations to London bondholders (see C-1847-LONDON-DEBT); PAC → MX: guarantee of Mexican citizens' property and the Church's; PAC → MX: pledge never to cede itself or its territory to any power — can end or refuse: LONDON: the bondholders' consent is sought (Hartnell carries the convention to London)
- **Assumed Mexican debt to the London bondholders** (loan, 1847–1859, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → LONDON: service of about £3 million of Mexican obligations assumed under Querétaro — secured on: customs and prospective mining revenue (later stated as gold royalties and San Francisco customs) — can end or refuse: LONDON: the bondholders' consent to the convention is sought
- **Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo (alternate)** (treaty, 1848–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MX → US: cession of New Mexico, the Great Basin and the Rio Grande line; US → PAC: respect for California's frontier as Mexico has recognized it
- **Treaty of Monterey of 1850** (treaty, 1850–, §5, CANON) — owes: US → PAC: eastern boundary: 42nd parallel, 120th meridian to 39°, southeast to the Colorado at 35°N, along the Colorado to the Gila; recognized ports and access to Yuma; PAC → US: abandons claims to the Great Basin; the US retains the inland routes and the Salt Lake country
- **Mexican convention ending scalp bounties** (convention, c.1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: MX → PAC: an end to scalp bounties
- **Credit to Juárez's government on the Sonora customs** (loan, 1865–, §13, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → MX: recognition and credit secured on the Sonora customs; rifles through Paso del Norte; MX → PAC: a customs post at the boundary at the Pass that passes the rifles overland into Chihuahua
- **Baja purchase** (treaty, 1867–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: MX → PAC: Baja (purchase); PAC → MX: the purchase price, paid over five years
- **Guaymas railway concession and free port** (concession, 1867–, §13, PROPOSED) — owes: MX → PAC: a Guaymas railway concession and free port
- **Recognition of Carranza and the rail transit to Douglas** (settlement, 1915–, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → MX: recognition jointly with the Gulf; rail transit for Hill's troops to Douglas; no arms; GULF → MX: recognition jointly with Pacifica; rail transit from Eagle Pass
- **The consented ninety-day expedition into Chihuahua** (pact, 1916–1916, §30, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: MX → PAC: formal consent under the Apache convention's pursuit clause; liaison officers from the War Ministry; PAC → MX: ninety days, kept to the day; no fighting with Carranza's troops; GULF → PAC: a brigade under the defence pact of 1900
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ### Britain

- **British (and French) declaration of non-acquisition in California** (pact, 1848–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: UK → PAC: recognition, and a declaration that it will take no part of California under any form; FR → PAC: accession to the declaration (1849)
- **The Alaskan rivers free forever to British Columbia** (treaty, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: navigation of the Stikine, the Yukon and the Porcupine through Alaska, free forever
- **Vladivostok settlement folded into the Congress of Berlin** (settlement, 1878–, §16, PROPOSED) — owes: RU → JP: restoration of Southern Sakhalin; UK → PAC: recognition of the Philippine purchase
- **Landing grant for the Eastern Extension company's Hong Kong–Manila cable at Bolinao** (concession, 1880–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: PH → UK: a landing at Bolinao for the Eastern Extension company's Hong Kong–Manila cable
- **The four-power fur-seal convention** (convention, 1886–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: fifteen percent of the Pribilof catch; RU → BC: fifteen percent of the Commander Islands' catch, 1886–1921; PAC → BC: from 1921, with the Commander Islands, fifteen percent of their catch; JP → BC: ten percent of Robben Island's catch; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the dominion's shares, by succession under the Association Treaty
- **Port Hamilton settlement: guarantee of Korean territory** (guarantee, 1887–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with Russia and Pacifica); RU → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with China and Pacifica); PAC → KR: guarantee of Korean territory; a duty to protect Korea without owning it; UK → KR: withdrawal from Port Hamilton
- **Berlin Act (Samoa)** (treaty, 1889–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; UK → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; PAC → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power
- **Partition of Samoa** (settlement, 1899–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029)
- **Settlement with Britain during the Boer War** (settlement, c.1899–, §25, PROPOSED)
- **Fanning cable station and equatorial islands recognition** (treaty, c.1899–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → UK: recognizes British Fanning as the station of the Pacific Cable; UK → PAC: recognizes Pacifican sovereignty over the equatorial islands from Palmyra to Baker
- **The western-waters treaty and its joint commission** (treaty, 1906–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: a joint commission of equals with powers to regulate as well as investigate on the Columbia, Kootenay, Okanagan, Similkameen, Skagit, Pend Oreille, the Fraser's salmon and the Alaskan rivers; no upstream-sovereignty doctrine; PAC → CLD: from Proclamation Day in 1932, the same under a water board of Pacifica and Caledonia
- **Mukden-Jinzhou-Huludao railway loan** (loan, 1908–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → UK: service of the senior share of the railway loan, led by the British and Chinese Corporation; CN → PAC: service of the junior share taken in San Francisco — secured on: the line's receipts
- **British and German undertakings on the Union** (pact, c.1908–1917, §27, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: UK → PAC: no state-backed money, bases, intelligence or arms to a Union attempt to overturn the North American settlement; DE → PAC: no state-backed money, bases, intelligence or arms to a Union attempt to overturn the North American settlement
- **The customs trusteeship of January 1912** (settlement, 1912–1929, §27, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **Entente loans (wartime advances)** (loan, 1914–, §30, CANON, CR-025) — owes: ENTENTE → PAC: repayment of the advances gathered from the Treasury, Pacifican savers and foreign subscriptions through the San Francisco syndicates; ENTENTE → US: Union investors buying through Pacifican correspondents keep their own claims and their own risk; ENTENTE → JP: repayment of Japan's lending to Russia from 1916 and to Britain and France from 1917 — secured on: from the end of 1916, the Allies' requisitioned foreign securities, many of them Pacifican bonds (the collateral loans)
- **The Entente's grain on Pacifican credit** (loan, 1915–, §30, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → UK: grain bought with the Entente loans; UK → PAC: repayment with the rest of London's debt
- **Siberian coalition** (alliance, 1918–, §31, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: the mass of the army in the east; CN → PAC: manpower and the Manchurian railway; PAC → SIB: command, finance, railway organization, artillery, armored trains, aircraft, river flotillas and the expeditionary corps
- **The British mission's promise of credit at Omsk** (loan, 1918–1918, §31, APPROVED, CR-046) — owes: UK → SIB: credit to whatever government sits in Omsk on 19 November, drawn on a British account
- **The Treaty of The Hague** (treaty, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: evacuation of Belgium and northern France; no indemnity; DE → PAC: loss of its colonies; Pacifica and Japan retain Germany's Pacific islands; DE → FR: Alsace-Lorraine given up, not to France: a neutral republic in union with Switzerland (C-1919-ALSACE-LORRAINE); PAC → CN: return of Shandong apart from the Tsingtao anchorage; UK → DE: recognition of Germany's clients in Poland, the Baltic region, Ukraine and Finland; AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918 (C-1919-BOHEMIA)
- **Alsace-Lorraine: neutral republic in union with Switzerland** (guarantee, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: the Reichsland given up to a neutral republic confirmed by a vote of its people; PAC → FR: the signatories guarantee the republic's neutrality
- **Permanent arbitration court** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: PAC → JP: the settlement carries Japan's racial equality clause — renewals: 1932
- **1919 naval limits** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: UK → PAC: parity: the combined Pacifica–Japan fleets count at parity with Britain; DE → PAC: Germany below Britain and the Pacific partners; France and Italy below Germany; UK → PAC: disposal of older twelve-inch ships by 1922, through scrapping, training hulks or targets (Britain and Germany)
- **Pacifica's accession to the Declaration of Paris** (convention, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **Copenhagen prisoner agreement, with gold for the Cheka's British file** (convention, 1920–1920, §31, APPROVED, CR-046) — owes: SOVNARKOM → UK: Britain's prisoners released; the Cheka's British file burned (a secret term, not kept); UK → SOVNARKOM: gold for the papers
- **Baku concession agreement (Anglo-Pacifican, with Azerbaijan)** (concession, c.1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-046) — owes: AZ → PAC: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; PAC → UK: from 1927, a fixed allowance for the Royal Navy at Batum at market price — renewals: 1927 — can end or refuse: UK: the seventh-year review: invoked in the spring of 1927 for first call and a British operator, and withdrawn in June
- **The regional works share of the customs surplus** (settlement, 1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **Treaty of Harbin** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: RU → SIB: Siberia, a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote, holds the country from the Ob to Lake Baikal; the Okhotsk coast west of Ola Bay and Nikolayevsk (APPROVED); Yakutia, Chukotka, the Arctic coast and the islands east of the Kara Sea (PROPOSED); SIB → RU: entry into the federal compact by the vote of the Regional Duma at Tomsk; the Siberia–Alash union of 1918 dissolved in the same act; RU → ALASH: a land of Russia keeping its courts and kibitka tax, without its army or its equal standing; Alash is not asked; RU → PAC: concessions at Grozny and Maikop against the reconstruction credit; AZ → PAC: a joint Anglo-Pacifican concession at Baku: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; RU → JP: a client sphere in Transbaikalia around Chita; RU → CN: the Amur, a sphere in Outer Mongolia and Tuva; renunciation of extraterritorial rights, concessions and the Boxer indemnity share (to Chinese schools); Mongolia back under Chinese suzerainty under its own Mongol government; SIB → CN: passage for Chinese ships down the Amur to the sea; CN → SIB: Siberian trade and settlement rights in the Chinese spheres; SIB → JP: a Siberian trial of the Nikolayevsk partisan leaders and an indemnity; JP → SIB: passage for Siberian ships through the straits of a sea now Japanese on three sides; SIB → PAC: the straits into the Kara Sea open to merchant ships; PAC → RU: gives up the old claims to Wrangel Island and the De Long islands, taking the Commander Islands instead; JP → SIB: renunciation of the pan-Mongol schemes Japan once backed
- **Treaty of Kars (the three districts)** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-047) — owes: TR → AM: Kars and Kağızman, with the fortress and the railway from Alexandropol, by the district vote of 1921; AM → TR: Olti and Ardahan, by the same vote; TR → GE: Batum stays Georgian as a free port; GE → TR: Turkish goods pass free through Batum; GE → UK: the pipeline terminal at Batum; GE → PAC: a tanker berth of Pacifica's own at Batum
- **Funding of Britain's gold-dollar debt** (loan, 1923–1985, §30, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → PAC: service of its gold-dollar war debt over sixty-two years at reduced interest; the obligation keeps its gold-dollar value through sterling depreciation; PAC → UK: in 1932 cancels seventeen of the fifty-three remaining annuities in three tranches (Caledonia 2, the retrenchment 11, the signature 4; MODEL) and writes off the suspended annuity of 1931–32; thirty-six annuities stand
- **The 1927 settlement's oil terms** (settlement, 1927–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-053) — owes: UK → PAC: waives the sheikhdoms' nationality clause for the Pacifican company; consents to an enlarged American group in Mesopotamia; PAC → US: joins the Union's group in Mesopotamia rather than displacing it; keeps the Red Line agreement of 1928 as a shared discipline
- **Hoover's war-debt moratorium** (settlement, 1931–1932, §34, CANON) — owes: PAC → ENTENTE: suspension of war-debt payments; debt is restructured
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **Westminster's Recognition and Severance Act and the Bamfield cable lease** (settlement, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: UK → CLD: recognition and severance by Act of Parliament on the legislature's request and consent; in force by a commencement order (about 15 July 1932); PAC → UK: two annuities of the debt funded in 1923 cancelled from the near end of the schedule (C-1923-UK-FUNDING; the first of three tranches); PAC → IIC: guarantee of a long commercial lease of the Bamfield Pacific Cable station; CLD → UK: the Island's entrenched guarantees: Victoria the capital, the Island's provincial autonomy, Crown pensions protected, and a two-year term for keeping British nationality (the term PROPOSED)
- **British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → CROWN: ends for good the fortification of Singapore for capital ships and the heavy dry-dock work at Sembawang; a peacetime cap on its capital ships east of Ceylon (cruisers and sloops unrestricted); UK → JP: the same halt and cap, the Concord signing for both crowns; CROWN → UK: a maritime pledge of non-aggression toward Malaya and its sea lanes, covering beyond that only attack from the sea by an outside power on the Strait of Malacca and on the commerce of Malaya and the Indian Ocean; never order inside the colonies; JP → UK: the same pledge; PAC → UK: the Realm's own countersignature on the pledge (two-thirds of the Senate); UK → CROWN: retrocession of Labuan to Brunei subject to Labuan's vote, and consent to the Labuan Harbour Lease; detachment of Fanning and Washington into Ka Lōkahi's trust, the Fanning cable station leased back; quitclaim of its remaining claims in the Phoenix group; release of Tonga from the protectorate of 1900; CROWN → AU: protocol: no warship stationed and no new base built south of 10° S between Timor and 155° W (meridian PROPOSED), none sought in Portuguese Timor; no naval fortification or permanently berthed warship at the leased ports of Bintan and East Borneo; CROWN → NZ: the same protocol; JP → AU: the same protocol, which Japan signs as a crown of the Concord; UK → NZ: PROPOSED: New Zealand's contribution to the Singapore base rebated or credited against its war debt to Britain (sum OPEN); PAC → UK: eleven of the seventeen cancelled annuities of C-1923-UK-FUNDING fall on the deposit of the King's ratification of this treaty (MODEL) — can end or refuse: UK: reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED)
- **Concord–Tonga Treaty of Friendship and Maritime Security** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → TO: release from the protectorate of 1900, with Tonga's consent; HI → TO: Julian, as Mōʻī of Hawaii, is the treaty's guarantor, on the advice of the Hawaiian privy council; CROWN → TO: PROPOSED: no foreign garrison or base on Tongan soil; JP → TO: PROPOSED: the same bar
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ### London bondholders and lenders

- **Convention of Querétaro** (convention, 1847–, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MX → PAC: recognition of California; PAC → MX: assumption of about £3 million of Mexican obligations to London bondholders (see C-1847-LONDON-DEBT); PAC → MX: guarantee of Mexican citizens' property and the Church's; PAC → MX: pledge never to cede itself or its territory to any power — can end or refuse: LONDON: the bondholders' consent is sought (Hartnell carries the convention to London)
- **Assumed Mexican debt to the London bondholders** (loan, 1847–1859, §4, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: PAC → LONDON: service of about £3 million of Mexican obligations assumed under Querétaro — secured on: customs and prospective mining revenue (later stated as gold royalties and San Francisco customs) — can end or refuse: LONDON: the bondholders' consent to the convention is sought
- **Philippine Redemption Loan** (loan, 1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PH → LONDON: service of the loan from the Philippine customs; PAC → LONDON: the Confederation's guarantee; PAC → PH: service limited to the gain the islands make over their last Spanish years, published every year — secured on: Philippine customs

### Spain

- **Alaminos local convention** (convention, 1873–, §15, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: an indemnity and the crew's release; ES → PAC: an anchorage for a Pacifican squadron to protect its trade
- **Spanish Republic's Pacifican gold loan** (loan, 1874–1876, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: service of the loan from Philippine customs and tobacco revenues; Pacifican agents sit in the customs houses — secured on: Philippine customs and tobacco revenues
- **Spanish law of sale (Philippines, Marianas, Carolines, Palau)** (treaty, 1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: the Philippines, Marianas, Carolines and Palau; PAC → ES: recognize no Cuban insurgents and close its ports to their filibusters — can end or refuse: ES: the sale requires a law of the Cortes

### The Philippines (dependency, then the Philippine crown)

- **Spanish Republic's Pacifican gold loan** (loan, 1874–1876, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: ES → PAC: service of the loan from Philippine customs and tobacco revenues; Pacifican agents sit in the customs houses — secured on: Philippine customs and tobacco revenues
- **Philippine Redemption Loan** (loan, 1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PH → LONDON: service of the loan from the Philippine customs; PAC → LONDON: the Confederation's guarantee; PAC → PH: service limited to the gain the islands make over their last Spanish years, published every year — secured on: Philippine customs
- **Philippine Organic Act (dependency of the Confederation)** (law, 1876–1887, §15, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: government by the Providentor's office through a Governor-General with a Philippine Council; the Audiencia keeps Spanish law
- **Chartered Philippine tobacco company** (charter, c.1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029)
- **Philippine land bonds (friar-estate purchases)** (loan, c.1876–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: the Confederation's guarantee, the same as on the Redemption Loan
- **Sulu compacts (Sultan and datus)** (compact, 1878–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SULU → PAC: settlement of the south (terms not stated) — can end or refuse: SULU: the Sultan alone can consent to undo them
- **Landing grant for the Eastern Extension company's Hong Kong–Manila cable at Bolinao** (concession, 1880–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: PH → UK: a landing at Bolinao for the Eastern Extension company's Hong Kong–Manila cable
- **Philippine Municipal Ordinance** (law, 1883–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029)
- **Papal arbitration and the Leo XIII bargain** (settlement, 1885–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → PAC: Pacifica's Carolines confirmed; Pacifica's claim to Wake recorded; PAC → VATICAN: freedom for the Catholic missions in the Carolines; PAC → DE: trade and coaling rights (Germany keeps the Marshalls); PAC → VATICAN: Philippine parishes pass to Filipino secular priests; Filipino bishops named with the right to succeed; friar estates paid for partly into an endowment that stays in the islands
- **Philippine gold-exchange peso** (law, 1886–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: holds the peso's reserve in San Francisco
- **Elected Philippine provincial juntas** (law, 1892–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: PAC → PH: elected provincial juntas
- **Manila–Shanghai cable landing agreement** (concession, 1906–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → CN: a sea cable of Carquinez gutta-percha from Manila, laid by the Realm's telegraph estate and the Philippine administration; CN → PAC: a landing near the mouth of the Yangtze, where China's Imperial Telegraph Administration owns and works the shore end and the Shanghai office
- **Philippine crown offer and Calderón's organic law** (constitution, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040) — owes: PH → CROWN: the Philippine crown, offered by the Cortes and confirmed by plebiscite; CROWN → PH: a single-chamber Cortes with a permanent commission, its own budget and army; Gobernadores never posted to their home province; neither crown subordinate; Lugarteniente General governs without the old veto; PAC → PH: the Realm takes over what remains of the Redemption Loan at its 1907 balance (MODEL) — can end or refuse: PH: the Cortes offers and a plebiscite confirms; terms signed before the vote
- **Ka Lōkahi, the council of the crowns** (compact, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040) — renewals: 1917, 1927 — can end or refuse: PAC: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes; HI: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes; PH: one vote over the succession, the agreed matters and disputes
- **The Mariveles district** (charter, 1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi)** (treaty, 1927–, §33, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: sixty-five percent of common costs (quota 65/35); Foreign Affairs, the common Navy and their Finance held in common; JP → PAC: thirty-five percent quota voted by the Diet; at least one common minister Japanese by law; Japan's prime minister countersigns appointments; PAC → JP: the western squadron reserved to the Japanese naval party until the 1932 renewal; from 1 August 1932 the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL); JP → PAC: the home army enters common command only for a Concord-declared war; CROWN → JP: annual Council of the Two Crowns at Hayama from 1928; Prince Chichibu resides at Thalassia as the Emperor's representative between Councils — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: Japan's Emperor ratifies on the Privy Council's conditional advice; its side can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent; PAC: two-thirds Senate consent, won in 1928; CROWN: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan holds no date at which it may refuse
- **Philippine Cortes's appropriation for the Mariveles dry dock** (law, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084)

### The Sultan of Sulu and the datus

- **Sulu compacts (Sultan and datus)** (compact, 1878–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SULU → PAC: settlement of the south (terms not stated) — can end or refuse: SULU: the Sultan alone can consent to undo them
- **North Borneo lease to the Pacifican Borneo company** (lease, 1878–, §15, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SULU → PAC: lease of North Borneo to a Pacifican Borneo company

### The peoples of the North Borneo interior (Mat Salleh's following at Tambunan)

- **The Palatan peace (Tambunan a compact land)** (compact, 1898–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → NBINTERIOR: Tambunan held as a compact land under its own people; the Pacifican Borneo company held to the peace by the Companies Office on pain of its licence

### China (Qing, then the Republic)

- **1868 treaty with China** (treaty, 1868–, §14, PROPOSED) — owes: CN → PAC: ordinary foreign treaty rights; PAC → CN: jointly licensed family emigration (article shared by both)
- **First Korean–Pacifican treaty** (treaty, c.1878–, §14, CANON)
- **Ryukyu mediation** (settlement, 1879–, §15, PROPOSED)
- **Keelung coal concession** (concession, c.1884–, §16, PROPOSED) — owes: CN → PAC: a coal concession to a Chinese–Pacifican company; PAC → CN: railway construction and fleet-rebuilding credit
- **Chinese Customs Inspectorate under Pacifican leadership** (office, 1885–1929, §16, CANON) — owes: PAC → CN: administration of the Customs Inspectorate, with multinational staff and published accounts (PROPOSED); CN → PAC: service of Pacifican credit secured on customs — can end or refuse: CN: China's retained appointment power
- **Port Hamilton settlement: guarantee of Korean territory** (guarantee, 1887–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with Russia and Pacifica); RU → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with China and Pacifica); PAC → KR: guarantee of Korean territory; a duty to protect Korea without owning it; UK → KR: withdrawal from Port Hamilton
- **China's treaty of service (the Customs' preventive service)** (treaty, 1888–1929, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → CN: companies for the Customs' preventive service under the Inspectorate
- **The conservancy contract of 1891 (Yellow River and Grand Canal)** (concession, 1891–, §25, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: PAC → CN: Pacifican engineers for the Yellow River and the Grand Canal; CN → PAC: the conservancy charge, among the customs' first obligations — secured on: the Maritime Customs revenue
- **Korean armistice brokered by the Regent** (treaty, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: Chinese suzerainty ended; CN → JP: a moderate indemnity and commercial rights; Japanese economic predominance in Korea
- **Guarantee of Korean independence and neutrality** (guarantee, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; JP → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; PAC → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality
- **Formosan Revenue Convention** (convention, 1895–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → PAC: a Pacifican Director-General nominated by the Regent, holding a Qing commission, controls the camphor monopoly, Keelung mines, Formosan customs and the Keelung harbour trust (pilots, lights, works and coaling rights); CN → JP: receipts pay the indemnity and Japan; CN → PAC: Pacifica's fee from receipts, before the provincial treasury — secured on: camphor monopoly, Keelung mines, Formosan customs and Keelung harbour trust
- **The Formosan revenue guard** (treaty, 1895–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → CN: a company guarding the Keelung mines and the camphor stores for the Director-General (C-1895-FORMOSA)
- **Credit for Chinese railways** (loan, 1896–, §25, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → CN: credit for a Chinese-owned Manchurian railway and the Hankow–Canton trunk
- **The Chinese railway bonds of 1896** (loan, 1896–, §25, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → SANFRANCISCO: service of the railway bonds — secured on: the customs, not the lines' receipts
- **Liaodong Gulf pledge and the Huludao first refusal** (guarantee, 1899–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → PAC: no part of the coast of the Liaodong Gulf to be leased or ceded to any power; CN → PAC: first refusal on a harbour at Huludao if one is ever built there with foreign capital
- **Manchurian railway section: Japanese running rights and wartime railway board** (concession, c.1899–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → JP: running rights on the Chinese-owned Manchurian section; CN → PAC: a Pacifican railway board in wartime
- **The Pingxiang coke credit (Hanyeping)** (loan, 1904–, §26, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → PAC: service of the credit for Pingxiang's coke and its haulage
- **Manila–Shanghai cable landing agreement** (concession, 1906–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → CN: a sea cable of Carquinez gutta-percha from Manila, laid by the Realm's telegraph estate and the Philippine administration; CN → PAC: a landing near the mouth of the Yangtze, where China's Imperial Telegraph Administration owns and works the shore end and the Shanghai office
- **Mukden-Jinzhou-Huludao railway loan** (loan, 1908–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → UK: service of the senior share of the railway loan, led by the British and Chinese Corporation; CN → PAC: service of the junior share taken in San Francisco — secured on: the line's receipts
- **The banner loan of 1908** (loan, 1908–, §26, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → PAC: service from the stipend funds the provinces no longer pay, the customs balance as second security — secured on: the provincial stipend funds; the customs balance as second security
- **Huludao Harbour Trust** (concession, 1909–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-042) — owes: CN → PAC: a Qing commission for a Pacifican director and Pacifican engineers of the Huludao harbour, on the Keelung pattern; CN → PAC: the harbour dues pledged to the bondholders until the bonds are paid — secured on: Huludao harbour dues
- **The customs trusteeship of January 1912** (settlement, 1912–1929, §27, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The Sino-Japanese treaties of 25 May 1915** (treaty, 1915–, §28, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: commercial rights along the Manchurian line under China's law and police; confirmation of the Daye ore contract; JP → CN: an effective 5 per cent from the tariff revision of 1918; PAC → CN: an effective 5 per cent from the tariff revision of 1918
- **Siberian coalition** (alliance, 1918–, §31, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: the mass of the army in the east; CN → PAC: manpower and the Manchurian railway; PAC → SIB: command, finance, railway organization, artillery, armored trains, aircraft, river flotillas and the expeditionary corps
- **Manchurian railway running rights and transit** (concession, 1918–, §31, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: CN → JP: running rights and, later, guaranteed transit on the Chinese-owned line, without giving its territory to Japan; PAC → CN: the railway returns to Chinese management, improved at the coalition's expense
- **The coalition's agreement with Peking on the Chinese line (the Khingan agreement)** (pact, 1918–, §31, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The Treaty of The Hague** (treaty, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: evacuation of Belgium and northern France; no indemnity; DE → PAC: loss of its colonies; Pacifica and Japan retain Germany's Pacific islands; DE → FR: Alsace-Lorraine given up, not to France: a neutral republic in union with Switzerland (C-1919-ALSACE-LORRAINE); PAC → CN: return of Shandong apart from the Tsingtao anchorage; UK → DE: recognition of Germany's clients in Poland, the Baltic region, Ukraine and Finland; AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918 (C-1919-BOHEMIA)
- **Tsingtao anchorage lease** (lease, 1919–, §30, CANON) — owes: CN → PAC: the Tsingtao anchorage on lease; PAC → CN: return of the rest of Shandong, denying Japan the prize
- **The regional works share of the customs surplus** (settlement, 1920–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **Treaty of Harbin** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: RU → SIB: Siberia, a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote, holds the country from the Ob to Lake Baikal; the Okhotsk coast west of Ola Bay and Nikolayevsk (APPROVED); Yakutia, Chukotka, the Arctic coast and the islands east of the Kara Sea (PROPOSED); SIB → RU: entry into the federal compact by the vote of the Regional Duma at Tomsk; the Siberia–Alash union of 1918 dissolved in the same act; RU → ALASH: a land of Russia keeping its courts and kibitka tax, without its army or its equal standing; Alash is not asked; RU → PAC: concessions at Grozny and Maikop against the reconstruction credit; AZ → PAC: a joint Anglo-Pacifican concession at Baku: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; RU → JP: a client sphere in Transbaikalia around Chita; RU → CN: the Amur, a sphere in Outer Mongolia and Tuva; renunciation of extraterritorial rights, concessions and the Boxer indemnity share (to Chinese schools); Mongolia back under Chinese suzerainty under its own Mongol government; SIB → CN: passage for Chinese ships down the Amur to the sea; CN → SIB: Siberian trade and settlement rights in the Chinese spheres; SIB → JP: a Siberian trial of the Nikolayevsk partisan leaders and an indemnity; JP → SIB: passage for Siberian ships through the straits of a sea now Japanese on three sides; SIB → PAC: the straits into the Kara Sea open to merchant ships; PAC → RU: gives up the old claims to Wrangel Island and the De Long islands, taking the Commander Islands instead; JP → SIB: renunciation of the pan-Mongol schemes Japan once backed
- **The Mukden protocol of 1921** (settlement, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: running rights confirmed at the line's published rates, the same for every shipper and every destination and for the crop as for any freight; Siberian grain in transit to Huludao on the same rates
- **Standing alliance of Pacifica, Japan, China and federal Russia** (alliance, 1922–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → RU: command of the alliance and its finance; reconstruction credit in stages, against results; PAC → SIB: a guarantee of the federal compact and of Siberia's borders, keeping a smaller claim on the railway in reserve for war (MODEL)
- **The Shandong settlement (agreed 1919, carried out 1922)** (settlement, 1922–, §30, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: Japanese treasury-note credit repaid for the railway; a Japanese traffic manager and a joint chief accountant until the notes are redeemed; JP → CN: the railway returned
- **The Huludao industrial district** (charter, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **The Huludao crop article** (charter, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → ESTATES: bonded elevators on the quay and long purchase contracts at prices posted on the Huludao exchange and published by the Customs; ESTATES → CN: the Columbia's ammonia at its published price and the Polytechnic's soybean seed at the one royalty, to any farmer and any buyer's agent
- **Treaty of Nanjing** (treaty, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: CROWN → CN: the Concord guarantees China's frontiers, Manchuria included; JP → CN: the Concord guarantees China's frontiers, Manchuria included; CN → JP: railway transit and its joint management; CN → JP: the joint management sets no rate that favours a shipper or a destination (CR-081)
- **The Jinan withdrawal settlement of 1928** (settlement, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: the safety of Japanese residents and property; provincial garrisons at Jinan and on the railway zone; a joint claims commission; JP → CN: withdrawal within two months
- **China's accession to the standing alliance under the Treaty of Nanjing** (alliance, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-081)
- **The soybean compact** (treaty, 1929–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → CROWN: the crop travels at published rates on every Manchurian line; CN → JP: the crop travels at published rates on every Manchurian line; CROWN → CN: the Concord buys the crop by contract and never by land; JP → CN: the Concord buys the crop by contract and never by land
- **Pacifican bonds for the Shanghai Settlement's power plant** (loan, 1929–, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → SANFRANCISCO: service of the bonds by the Chinese municipal company
- **The end of the Concord's consular jurisdiction in China** (settlement, 1930–1940, §34, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The exchange of notes of 1930 on Japan's goods** (settlement, 1930–1933, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: duties on Japanese cotton goods and marine products bound for three years; CROWN → CN: acceptance of China's tariff autonomy; no schedule for Pacifican goods
- **The wheat credit to the relief commission (1931)** (loan, 1931–, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CROWN → CN: fifteen million bushels of wheat and flour on credit; CN → CROWN: service from the Customs' flood-relief surtax — secured on: the Customs' flood-relief surtax
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → CROWN: ends for good the fortification of Singapore for capital ships and the heavy dry-dock work at Sembawang; a peacetime cap on its capital ships east of Ceylon (cruisers and sloops unrestricted); UK → JP: the same halt and cap, the Concord signing for both crowns; CROWN → UK: a maritime pledge of non-aggression toward Malaya and its sea lanes, covering beyond that only attack from the sea by an outside power on the Strait of Malacca and on the commerce of Malaya and the Indian Ocean; never order inside the colonies; JP → UK: the same pledge; PAC → UK: the Realm's own countersignature on the pledge (two-thirds of the Senate); UK → CROWN: retrocession of Labuan to Brunei subject to Labuan's vote, and consent to the Labuan Harbour Lease; detachment of Fanning and Washington into Ka Lōkahi's trust, the Fanning cable station leased back; quitclaim of its remaining claims in the Phoenix group; release of Tonga from the protectorate of 1900; CROWN → AU: protocol: no warship stationed and no new base built south of 10° S between Timor and 155° W (meridian PROPOSED), none sought in Portuguese Timor; no naval fortification or permanently berthed warship at the leased ports of Bintan and East Borneo; CROWN → NZ: the same protocol; JP → AU: the same protocol, which Japan signs as a crown of the Concord; UK → NZ: PROPOSED: New Zealand's contribution to the Singapore base rebated or credited against its war debt to Britain (sum OPEN); PAC → UK: eleven of the seventeen cancelled annuities of C-1923-UK-FUNDING fall on the deposit of the King's ratification of this treaty (MODEL) — can end or refuse: UK: reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED)
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate
- **China's dual-track gold-exchange protocol and reserve compact** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CN → CROWN: customs assessments and new foreign borrowing on a gold-exchange unit linked to the Realm's dollar; CROWN → CN: the Realm's existing silver loans stay in silver; a reserve compact of its own in San Francisco on the terms the other holders have; JP → CN: signs with the Crown of Pacifica as one party, the Concord

- **Korea's treaty of service (the king's guard)** (treaty, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074) — owes: PAC → KR: a Pacific Company to drill the king's guard at Seoul in Korean words of command; KR → PAC: pay from the Korean treasury through the Companies Office
- **Korean armistice brokered by the Regent** (treaty, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: Chinese suzerainty ended; CN → JP: a moderate indemnity and commercial rights; Japanese economic predominance in Korea
- **Guarantee of Korean independence and neutrality** (guarantee, c.1894–, §25, CANON) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; JP → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality; PAC → KR: guarantee of independence and neutrality

- **Japan's agreed military and transit rights in Korea** (treaty, 1905–, §26, CANON) — owes: KR → JP: agreed military and transit rights; Japanese economic predominance; coordinated foreign policy
- **Kamchatka Company charter** (charter, 1927–1947, §33, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: JP → PAC: a third of the national-policy company's shares in Pacifican hands — can end or refuse: KAM: the charter is given with the consent of the Republic of Kamchatka (proposed); KR: Korean workers' paid contracts require Seoul's consent (proposed arrangements); PAC: Korean workers' paid contracts require Pacifica's consent (proposed arrangements)

### Russia

- **Alaska purchase** (treaty, 1867–, §12, CANON) — owes: RU → PAC: Russia's claim to Russian America (Alaska); PAC → RU: $7.2 million in Pacifica's own gold, paid at handover; PAC → RU: the Russian subjects may leave or stay; the Orthodox Church keeps its property; the Creoles of Sitka and Kodiak become Pacifican citizens
- **Vladivostok settlement folded into the Congress of Berlin** (settlement, 1878–, §16, PROPOSED) — owes: RU → JP: restoration of Southern Sakhalin; UK → PAC: recognition of the Philippine purchase

- **The four-power fur-seal convention** (convention, 1886–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: fifteen percent of the Pribilof catch; RU → BC: fifteen percent of the Commander Islands' catch, 1886–1921; PAC → BC: from 1921, with the Commander Islands, fifteen percent of their catch; JP → BC: ten percent of Robben Island's catch; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the dominion's shares, by succession under the Association Treaty
- **Port Hamilton settlement: guarantee of Korean territory** (guarantee, 1887–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: CN → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with Russia and Pacifica); RU → KR: guarantee of Korean territory (with China and Pacifica); PAC → KR: guarantee of Korean territory; a duty to protect Korea without owning it; UK → KR: withdrawal from Port Hamilton
- **Russo-Japanese peace (Witte's terms)** (treaty, 1905–, §26, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: RU → JP: withdrawal from Manchuria, an indemnity, recognition of Japan's southern gains and renunciation of claims in Korea
- **Bosnian settlement and standing commission** (settlement, 1909–, §27, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: AH → RS: Serbian trade through Ottoman Salonica, with the Porte's consent; a Bosnian Diet under Habsburg sovereignty; AH → RS: inquiry and a standstill before either side uses force, by order of the standing commission; RS → AH: inquiry and a standstill before either side uses force, by order of the standing commission
- **The customs trusteeship of January 1912** (settlement, 1912–1929, §27, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **Entente loans (wartime advances)** (loan, 1914–, §30, CANON, CR-025) — owes: ENTENTE → PAC: repayment of the advances gathered from the Treasury, Pacifican savers and foreign subscriptions through the San Francisco syndicates; ENTENTE → US: Union investors buying through Pacifican correspondents keep their own claims and their own risk; ENTENTE → JP: repayment of Japan's lending to Russia from 1916 and to Britain and France from 1917 — secured on: from the end of 1916, the Allies' requisitioned foreign securities, many of them Pacifican bonds (the collateral loans)
- **Emba oil concession** (concession, 1918–, §31, APPROVED, CR-041) — owes: ALASH → PAC: the Emba oil concession, signed with the Siberian treaty; kept by Pacifica through Alash's transfer to Russia in 1921
- **Julian's promise to Wrangel: Siberia's own vote** (pact, 1919–1921, §31, APPROVED, CR-041) — owes: PAC → RU: the coalition will not stand in the way when Siberia's own assembly chooses the federation; Pacifica's credit, the Office's films and the railway board's payrolls lean the way Omsk already leans; RU → PAC: a Russia that will recognise the settlement, pay its debts and owe Julian its survival; Pacifica's money reaches Wrangel only against results
- **Tyuya-Muyun (Fergana) concession** (concession, 1919–, §32, APPROVED, CR-049) — owes: RU → PAC: the Tyuya-Muyun deposit in Fergana as a concession alongside Emba
- **Treaty of Harbin** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: RU → SIB: Siberia, a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote, holds the country from the Ob to Lake Baikal; the Okhotsk coast west of Ola Bay and Nikolayevsk (APPROVED); Yakutia, Chukotka, the Arctic coast and the islands east of the Kara Sea (PROPOSED); SIB → RU: entry into the federal compact by the vote of the Regional Duma at Tomsk; the Siberia–Alash union of 1918 dissolved in the same act; RU → ALASH: a land of Russia keeping its courts and kibitka tax, without its army or its equal standing; Alash is not asked; RU → PAC: concessions at Grozny and Maikop against the reconstruction credit; AZ → PAC: a joint Anglo-Pacifican concession at Baku: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; RU → JP: a client sphere in Transbaikalia around Chita; RU → CN: the Amur, a sphere in Outer Mongolia and Tuva; renunciation of extraterritorial rights, concessions and the Boxer indemnity share (to Chinese schools); Mongolia back under Chinese suzerainty under its own Mongol government; SIB → CN: passage for Chinese ships down the Amur to the sea; CN → SIB: Siberian trade and settlement rights in the Chinese spheres; SIB → JP: a Siberian trial of the Nikolayevsk partisan leaders and an indemnity; JP → SIB: passage for Siberian ships through the straits of a sea now Japanese on three sides; SIB → PAC: the straits into the Kara Sea open to merchant ships; PAC → RU: gives up the old claims to Wrangel Island and the De Long islands, taking the Commander Islands instead; JP → SIB: renunciation of the pan-Mongol schemes Japan once backed
- **Japanese protection of the Kamchatka republic** (guarantee, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-031) — owes: JP → KAM: protection of the republic of Kamchatka and the Koryak coast as far as Ola Bay; RU → KAM: Harbin recognises the republic and its Resident
- **Transbaikalian client sphere (protectorate)** (settlement, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → TBK: a civilian Japanese Resident over a council of Buryats, Cossacks and townsmen; Japan renounces the pan-Mongol schemes; TBK → JP: Japanese settlers, the Chernovsky coal, the Nerchinsk mines and the junction of the line to Manchuria
- **Standing alliance of Pacifica, Japan, China and federal Russia** (alliance, 1922–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → RU: command of the alliance and its finance; reconstruction credit in stages, against results; PAC → SIB: a guarantee of the federal compact and of Siberia's borders, keeping a smaller claim on the railway in reserve for war (MODEL)
- **Kolyma gold concessions** (concession, 1929–, §32, CANON) — owes: SIB → PAC: concession companies working Kolyma gold with Pacifican dredges sell their gold to the Reserve Bank; the gold is Russian production

### Siberia (independent 1918–1921; a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote of 1921)

- **Siberian coalition** (alliance, 1918–, §31, CANON) — owes: JP → PAC: the mass of the army in the east; CN → PAC: manpower and the Manchurian railway; PAC → SIB: command, finance, railway organization, artillery, armored trains, aircraft, river flotillas and the expeditionary corps
- **The Siberian treaty of August 1918 (Komuch, Siberia, Alash, under Pacifican guarantee)** (treaty, 1918–1921, §31, APPROVED, CR-041) — owes: KOMUCH → SIB: recognition of Siberia, independent since 4 July 1918; Komuch's suspensive veto over Alash replaced by the guarantor's arbitration; SIB → ALASH: mutual recognition restored; mixed institutions in Akmolinsk, Semipalatinsk and Turgai under joint commissars; Alash courts, the kibitka tax and local councils; a Kazakh army raised by Alash on the Siberian army's plan under common command; the land of the three oblasts frozen as it stands; ALASH → SIB: the Kazakh army under common command in wartime; two autonomies in union toward a federal power of autonomous Siberia and autonomous Alash, or an all-Russian federal power created with their participation; PAC → SIB: guarantee of Siberia's frontier; the treaty printed by the Office and deposited in Peking and Tokyo; KOMUCH → PAC: the Constituent Assembly's land law enforced without landlord reclamation, as a condition of credit — secured on: the Imperial gold reserve taken at Kazan, in a Reserve Bank escrow at Chelyabinsk
- **The British mission's promise of credit at Omsk** (loan, 1918–1918, §31, APPROVED, CR-046) — owes: UK → SIB: credit to whatever government sits in Omsk on 19 November, drawn on a British account
- **Land-and-grain guarantee** (guarantee, 1919–, §31, PROPOSED, CR-030) — owes: PAC → SIB: the railway half: three paths in ten stay with Siberian grain, coal and passengers
- **Treaty of Harbin** (treaty, 1921–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: RU → SIB: Siberia, a federal land of Russia from the Tomsk vote, holds the country from the Ob to Lake Baikal; the Okhotsk coast west of Ola Bay and Nikolayevsk (APPROVED); Yakutia, Chukotka, the Arctic coast and the islands east of the Kara Sea (PROPOSED); SIB → RU: entry into the federal compact by the vote of the Regional Duma at Tomsk; the Siberia–Alash union of 1918 dissolved in the same act; RU → ALASH: a land of Russia keeping its courts and kibitka tax, without its army or its equal standing; Alash is not asked; RU → PAC: concessions at Grozny and Maikop against the reconstruction credit; AZ → PAC: a joint Anglo-Pacifican concession at Baku: Pacifica operates the field and takes oil for its Black Sea and Mediterranean ships; AZ → UK: the strategic say at Baku and the Batum pipeline; RU → JP: a client sphere in Transbaikalia around Chita; RU → CN: the Amur, a sphere in Outer Mongolia and Tuva; renunciation of extraterritorial rights, concessions and the Boxer indemnity share (to Chinese schools); Mongolia back under Chinese suzerainty under its own Mongol government; SIB → CN: passage for Chinese ships down the Amur to the sea; CN → SIB: Siberian trade and settlement rights in the Chinese spheres; SIB → JP: a Siberian trial of the Nikolayevsk partisan leaders and an indemnity; JP → SIB: passage for Siberian ships through the straits of a sea now Japanese on three sides; SIB → PAC: the straits into the Kara Sea open to merchant ships; PAC → RU: gives up the old claims to Wrangel Island and the De Long islands, taking the Commander Islands instead; JP → SIB: renunciation of the pan-Mongol schemes Japan once backed
- **Siberian coast fisheries convention** (convention, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → JP: the majority of the fishing lots on the Siberian coast for Japanese boats
- **Lena gold concessions** (concession, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → PAC: the Lena gold concessions
- **Railway west of Chita** (concession, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → PAC: the railway west of Chita, run by a Pacifican railway board since the war
- **Arctic station leases** (lease, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031)
- **Northern sea route charter** (charter, 1921–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031)
- **Siberian land statute of 1922, confirmed in the federal compact** (constitution, 1922–, §32, PROPOSED, CR-031) — owes: SIB → SAKHA: autonomy: its own assembly, language, communal land and a port at Okhotsk; SIB → CHUKOTKA: government of the empty coast and Chukotka as a territory with a commissioner and a council of the districts and clans
- **Standing alliance of Pacifica, Japan, China and federal Russia** (alliance, 1922–, §32, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → RU: command of the alliance and its finance; reconstruction credit in stages, against results; PAC → SIB: a guarantee of the federal compact and of Siberia's borders, keeping a smaller claim on the railway in reserve for war (MODEL)
- **Kolyma gold concessions** (concession, 1929–, §32, CANON) — owes: SIB → PAC: concession companies working Kolyma gold with Pacifican dredges sell their gold to the Reserve Bank; the gold is Russian production

### Germany

- **Papal arbitration and the Leo XIII bargain** (settlement, 1885–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → PAC: Pacifica's Carolines confirmed; Pacifica's claim to Wake recorded; PAC → VATICAN: freedom for the Catholic missions in the Carolines; PAC → DE: trade and coaling rights (Germany keeps the Marshalls); PAC → VATICAN: Philippine parishes pass to Filipino secular priests; Filipino bishops named with the right to succeed; friar estates paid for partly into an endowment that stays in the islands
- **Berlin Act (Samoa)** (treaty, 1889–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; UK → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; PAC → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power
- **Partition of Samoa** (settlement, 1899–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029)
- **British and German undertakings on the Union** (pact, c.1908–1917, §27, PROPOSED, CR-040) — owes: UK → PAC: no state-backed money, bases, intelligence or arms to a Union attempt to overturn the North American settlement; DE → PAC: no state-backed money, bases, intelligence or arms to a Union attempt to overturn the North American settlement
- **The customs trusteeship of January 1912** (settlement, 1912–1929, §27, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **Fuel-oil contracts with the German navy** (pact, c.1914–1914, §28, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → DE: fuel oil for Germany's new ships, under contracts Ballin brokered
- **Letters of marque in the war with Germany** (law, 1914–1919, §28, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **The Treaty of The Hague** (treaty, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: evacuation of Belgium and northern France; no indemnity; DE → PAC: loss of its colonies; Pacifica and Japan retain Germany's Pacific islands; DE → FR: Alsace-Lorraine given up, not to France: a neutral republic in union with Switzerland (C-1919-ALSACE-LORRAINE); PAC → CN: return of Shandong apart from the Tsingtao anchorage; UK → DE: recognition of Germany's clients in Poland, the Baltic region, Ukraine and Finland; AH → PAC: Emperor Karl bound to his federal manifesto of October 1918 (C-1919-BOHEMIA)
- **Alsace-Lorraine: neutral republic in union with Switzerland** (guarantee, 1919–, §30, APPROVED, CR-043) — owes: DE → FR: the Reichsland given up to a neutral republic confirmed by a vote of its people; PAC → FR: the signatories guarantee the republic's neutrality
- **Permanent arbitration court** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: PAC → JP: the settlement carries Japan's racial equality clause — renewals: 1932
- **1919 naval limits** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: UK → PAC: parity: the combined Pacifica–Japan fleets count at parity with Britain; DE → PAC: Germany below Britain and the Pacific partners; France and Italy below Germany; UK → PAC: disposal of older twelve-inch ships by 1922, through scrapping, training hulks or targets (Britain and Germany)
- **Thalassia settlement (Thalassia Conference)** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: US → CSA: Senate recognition of the Confederacy; US → GULF: Senate recognition of the Gulf Republic; US → PAC: renunciation of surviving claims under the 1865 and 1871 lines; currency in an adjustable gold-dollar order; naval ratio below the common fleet and Britain; accession to the arbitration court's new charter; Soviet industrial contracts cut; PAC → US: currency stability, partial recovery through the German debt settlement, Atlantic primacy and a founding seat in the renewed court
- **German debt settlement** (settlement, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: DE → US: partial recovery for Union lenders: its debts to the Union's banks are settled, the banks taking a cut in principal (PROPOSED, CR-086)
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate

- **Permanent arbitration court** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: PAC → JP: the settlement carries Japan's racial equality clause — renewals: 1932
- **1919 naval limits** (treaty, 1919–, §30, PROPOSED, CR-019) — owes: UK → PAC: parity: the combined Pacifica–Japan fleets count at parity with Britain; DE → PAC: Germany below Britain and the Pacific partners; France and Italy below Germany; UK → PAC: disposal of older twelve-inch ships by 1922, through scrapping, training hulks or targets (Britain and Germany)
