- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate

### The Holy See

- **Papal arbitration and the Leo XIII bargain** (settlement, 1885–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → PAC: Pacifica's Carolines confirmed; Pacifica's claim to Wake recorded; PAC → VATICAN: freedom for the Catholic missions in the Carolines; PAC → DE: trade and coaling rights (Germany keeps the Marshalls); PAC → VATICAN: Philippine parishes pass to Filipino secular priests; Filipino bishops named with the right to succeed; friar estates paid for partly into an endowment that stays in the islands

### The Crown of Pacifica

- **Constitution of the Realm** (constitution, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → HI: entrenched language and tenure guarantees for Hawaii as a historic land (no veto); PAC → COUNTIES: land taxes, roads, sanitation, primary schools and ordinary policing left to county boards; PAC → CAL: California a historic land: Spanish entrenched, community property, the Mexican grants as organic law (CR-071 G6.2)
- **Regency of the Realm** (office, 1887–1907, §21, CANON)
- **Polytechnic charter (chartered estate)** (charter, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → ESTATES: the Polytechnic's examinations, audit, General Staff and telegraph — can end or refuse: CROWN: the ruler chooses the chief from three graduate-council nominees; removal only with Senate consent
- **Truce of Thalassia** (pact, 1887–, §22, CANON) — owes: INLAND → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; NAVAL → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; ESTATES → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; CROWN → INLAND: life offices for the named holders; the factions meet through the Crown
- **Inland command at Sacramento (Truce life office)** (office, 1887–, §22, CANON) — can end or refuse: CROWN: after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent
- **Japanese naval party at Pearl Harbor (Truce life office)** (office, 1887–, §22, CANON) — can end or refuse: CROWN: after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent
- **Polytechnic headship at Carquinez (Truce life office)** (office, 1887–, §22, CANON) — can end or refuse: CROWN: after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent
- **Unwritten arrangement between the Crown and the Five** (pact, 1887–, §24, APPROVED, CR-027) — owes: CROWN → ESTATES: does not touch their charters, appointments or the Development Fund; sanctions them; ESTATES → CROWN: never take sides against the Crown and never let a faction capture them
- **The press estate's charter** (charter, 1887–, §25, APPROVED, CR-079)
- **Silas's renunciation** (pact, 1906–, §26, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PRUETT → CROWN: renunciation, for Silas and his descendants, of any claim to the crown the Act will create; CROWN → PRUETT: the renewed compact reopens the mission school (PROPOSED)
- **Kiyoshi's renunciation and the Kuroda match** (pact, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-026; CR-040) — owes: KURODA → CROWN: renunciation of the crown for Kiyoshi and his descendants; CROWN → KURODA: Julian takes a wife from his house; the heir after Julian is of Kuroda blood
- **Act of Settlement of 1907** (constitution, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-040) — owes: PAC → CROWN: establishes the Crown of Pacifica in the House of Thorne-Kamehameha; Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty; HI → CROWN: joins the Hawaiian crown to the Crown of Pacifica; the ʻAha Aliʻi proclaims Julian heir to the Hawaiian crown as Kamehameha VI; PAC → HI: dissolves the Territorial Council and restores Hawaii's legislature; Hawaiian restored as the Crown's language and in its schools; ʻAha Aliʻi keeps custody of Hawaiian crown and kuleana lands; Thalassia's ground confirmed as land of the Hawaiian throne — can end or refuse: HI: the union is put to a plebiscite of Hawaiian subjects by the restored legislature and the ʻAha Aliʻi; PAC: Senate and Chamber confer the Realm's sovereignty by two-thirds
- **Julian's crowns (Sovereign of Pacifica, Kamehameha VI)** (office, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040)
- **Philippine crown offer and Calderón's organic law** (constitution, 1907–, §26, APPROVED, CR-029; CR-040) — owes: PH → CROWN: the Philippine crown, offered by the Cortes and confirmed by plebiscite; CROWN → PH: a single-chamber Cortes with a permanent commission, its own budget and army; Gobernadores never posted to their home province; neither crown subordinate; Lugarteniente General governs without the old veto; PAC → PH: the Realm takes over what remains of the Redemption Loan at its 1907 balance (MODEL) — can end or refuse: PH: the Cortes offers and a plebiscite confirms; terms signed before the vote
- **Pacific Concord (Taiheiyō Kyōyaku; Concordia del Pacífico; ka Lōkahi)** (treaty, 1927–, §33, CANON) — owes: PAC → JP: sixty-five percent of common costs (quota 65/35); Foreign Affairs, the common Navy and their Finance held in common; JP → PAC: thirty-five percent quota voted by the Diet; at least one common minister Japanese by law; Japan's prime minister countersigns appointments; PAC → JP: the western squadron reserved to the Japanese naval party until the 1932 renewal; from 1 August 1932 the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL); JP → PAC: the home army enters common command only for a Concord-declared war; CROWN → JP: annual Council of the Two Crowns at Hayama from 1928; Prince Chichibu resides at Thalassia as the Emperor's representative between Councils — renewals: 1932, 1937 — can end or refuse: JP: Japan's Emperor ratifies on the Privy Council's conditional advice; its side can lapse if Pacifica never obtains consent; PAC: two-thirds Senate consent, won in 1928; CROWN: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: from 1932 the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan holds no date at which it may refuse
- **Concord executive trust (first term)** (office, 1927–1932, §33, CANON) — owes: JP → CROWN: the Concord's executive entrusted to the Crown of Pacifica, because Pacifica pays the larger quota and commands the fleet — renewals: 1932
- **Thalassia Plan** (law, 1928–, §33, CANON) — owes: COUNTIES → HI: public land stays leasehold; county administration of leases does not transfer ownership or the ʻAha Aliʻi's custody of Hawaiian lands; CROWN → HI: on Thalassia's Hawaiian Crown ground the Mōʻī receives the premiums, which fund parks, transit and schools
- **Treaty of Nanjing** (treaty, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-039) — owes: CROWN → CN: the Concord guarantees China's frontiers, Manchuria included; JP → CN: the Concord guarantees China's frontiers, Manchuria included; CN → JP: railway transit and its joint management; CN → JP: the joint management sets no rate that favours a shipper or a destination (CR-081)
- **The Jinan withdrawal settlement of 1928** (settlement, 1928–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: the safety of Japanese residents and property; provincial garrisons at Jinan and on the railway zone; a joint claims commission; JP → CN: withdrawal within two months
- **China's accession to the standing alliance under the Treaty of Nanjing** (alliance, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-081)
- **The soybean compact** (treaty, 1929–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → CROWN: the crop travels at published rates on every Manchurian line; CN → JP: the crop travels at published rates on every Manchurian line; CROWN → CN: the Concord buys the crop by contract and never by land; JP → CN: the Concord buys the crop by contract and never by land
- **The end of the Concord's consular jurisdiction in China** (settlement, 1930–1940, §34, APPROVED, CR-081)
- **The exchange of notes of 1930 on Japan's goods** (settlement, 1930–1933, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → JP: duties on Japanese cotton goods and marine products bound for three years; CROWN → CN: acceptance of China's tariff autonomy; no schedule for Pacifican goods
- **The wheat credit to the relief commission (1931)** (loan, 1931–, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CROWN → CN: fifteen million bushels of wheat and flour on credit; CN → CROWN: service from the Customs' flood-relief surtax — secured on: the Customs' flood-relief surtax
- **1932 renewal of the Concord without term** (office, 1932–, §36, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: JP → CROWN: the executive held by the Crown of Pacifica until both crowns agree otherwise at a review; Japan's overseas commands pass to common control; JP → CROWN: the naval party loses its reserved squadron as command is consolidated; CROWN → JP: the home army stays national except in a Concord war; CROWN → JP: a review every five years, the first in the summer of 1937, at which the quota, the common seats, the pool shares and the parities are open; failing agreement the terms in force continue, except that the quota moves to the share the joint audit gives; CROWN → JP: the Navy seat by treaty law, the yards' share, pool entry at the first review, the Atomic Power Board and the yen's reset in principle (C-1932-NAVY-SEAT-PROTOCOL, C-1932-HAYAMA-GRADUATION, C-1932-ATOMIC-POWER-BOARD; carried out for the yen by C-1932-ADJUSTABLE-PARITY) — renewals: 1937, 1942 — can end or refuse: CROWN: the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; JP: the union ends only by the agreement of both crowns, ratified by both delegations; Japan may raise the alternating executive at any review and cannot bring it about by refusing
- **Lands and Waters Charter** (charter, 1932–, §36, CANON, CR-020) — owes: HI → CROWN: watershed reserves and minimum stream flows, two leeward industrial districts, restoration of mined land, leasehold for public land, the statistics bureau's annual islands index; Waimanu protected; HI → CROWN: the smelter keeps its contracts at gold parity
- **The Concord's external guarantee of Caledonia** (guarantee, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CROWN → CLD: external guarantee of Caledonia, kept by the common Navy; JP → CLD: the same guarantee, as the Concord's other crown; CLD → JP: entry and residence for Japanese subjects, reciprocal with Caledonians' in Japan; the floors kept for those resident on Proclamation Day who stay Japanese; no vote by the clause; CLD → CROWN: the repair contract at Esquimalt (kept with the Caledonian yard) and the Alaska corridor's naval clauses; dockyards open to the common Navy
- **The Trilateral Treaty of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CLD → CA: perpetual running rights for the Canadian Pacific across Caledonia, a bonded Canadian quay on Burrard Inlet and transit at Prince Rupert, entrenched against revocation; CA → CROWN: a yearly quota of Pacifican farm and industrial machines outside the emergency tariff (size OPEN), open to both partners' champions; CA → CROWN: the Great Bear contract given a term of fifty years from 1932 (C-1912-GREATBEAR-OFFTAKE); CROWN → CA: a higher royalty on Great Bear's ore (rate OPEN); CA → CLD: withdrawal of Macdonald's reservation against the line of 1870
- **British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → CROWN: ends for good the fortification of Singapore for capital ships and the heavy dry-dock work at Sembawang; a peacetime cap on its capital ships east of Ceylon (cruisers and sloops unrestricted); UK → JP: the same halt and cap, the Concord signing for both crowns; CROWN → UK: a maritime pledge of non-aggression toward Malaya and its sea lanes, covering beyond that only attack from the sea by an outside power on the Strait of Malacca and on the commerce of Malaya and the Indian Ocean; never order inside the colonies; JP → UK: the same pledge; PAC → UK: the Realm's own countersignature on the pledge (two-thirds of the Senate); UK → CROWN: retrocession of Labuan to Brunei subject to Labuan's vote, and consent to the Labuan Harbour Lease; detachment of Fanning and Washington into Ka Lōkahi's trust, the Fanning cable station leased back; quitclaim of its remaining claims in the Phoenix group; release of Tonga from the protectorate of 1900; CROWN → AU: protocol: no warship stationed and no new base built south of 10° S between Timor and 155° W (meridian PROPOSED), none sought in Portuguese Timor; no naval fortification or permanently berthed warship at the leased ports of Bintan and East Borneo; CROWN → NZ: the same protocol; JP → AU: the same protocol, which Japan signs as a crown of the Concord; UK → NZ: PROPOSED: New Zealand's contribution to the Singapore base rebated or credited against its war debt to Britain (sum OPEN); PAC → UK: eleven of the seventeen cancelled annuities of C-1923-UK-FUNDING fall on the deposit of the King's ratification of this treaty (MODEL) — can end or refuse: UK: reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED)
- **Labuan Harbour Lease and the Brunei auction accord** (lease, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: BN → CROWN: lease of Labuan's harbour to the Concord as a station of the common Navy on its ordinary establishment; BN → JP: the same lease: Japanese ships of the common Navy use the harbour by right, with no squadron and no commandant reserved to Japan; BN → LABUAN: municipal self-government, the option of keeping British nationality and employment in the port, as the published terms of the vote guarantee; BN → SHELL: PROPOSED: the existing lease at Seria honoured as it stands, at its old royalty; new blocks let at public auction by the Resident's administration
- **Concord–Tonga Treaty of Friendship and Maritime Security** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → TO: release from the protectorate of 1900, with Tonga's consent; HI → TO: Julian, as Mōʻī of Hawaii, is the treaty's guarantor, on the advice of the Hawaiian privy council; CROWN → TO: PROPOSED: no foreign garrison or base on Tongan soil; JP → TO: PROPOSED: the same bar
- **Batavia Compact** (compact, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: NL → JP: two fenced commercial loading ports leased for fifty years (bauxite on Bintan Island; oil in East Borneo), and open-door concessions on published royalties in unallocated East Borneo oil blocks and Bintan's bauxite (Sulawesi's nickel PROPOSED); NL → CROWN: the same leases and concessions, made with the Concord for both crowns; CROWN → NL: no annexation; respect for Dutch sovereignty outside the leased fences; no policing inside the colony; no naval fortification and no warship permanently berthed at the leased ports; JP → NL: the same; inside the fences only unarmed watchmen of the Japanese companies, with Dutch field police at the gate; NL → SHELL: Shell's fields and refineries at Tarakan, Balikpapan and in South Sumatra stay exactly as they are; NL → CROWN: PROPOSED: the Sunda, Lombok and Makassar straits and the Dutch shore of Malacca kept open to peaceful commerce, the clause scheduled to the second article of the Final Act
- **Common Navy Ministry Treaty-Law Protocol and the Joint Materials Board** (treaty, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: CROWN → JP: the Navy seat Japanese by treaty law, inside the three common ministries; Julian still names the minister; CROWN → JP: a Joint Materials Board with members of both partners, advising on the pool's allocations, with no say over shipping or grain charters and no power to bind; CROWN → JP: a fixed share of the common fleet's new building for Japanese yards, below capital ships, fixed to the second review in 1942 (35 per cent PROPOSED); capital ships open at the review of 1937; JP → CROWN: the reserved western squadron ends and command is consolidated — renewals: 1937, 1942
- **Hayama Pool-Entry Protocol** (convention, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: JP → CROWN: no primary aluminium smelting in Japan until the first review, in mid-1937; CROWN → JP: Japanese firms may hold up to 49 per cent of joint smelting and refining ventures outside Japan, 1932–1937; CROWN → JP: on the day the first review opens: admission of Japanese primary smelters and heavy-electrical works to the Concord pool at the inside price, a share of the Concord's orders, pool alumina contracts, and the 49 per cent stakes converted to Japanese majorities — renewals: 1937
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate
- **China's dual-track gold-exchange protocol and reserve compact** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CN → CROWN: customs assessments and new foreign borrowing on a gold-exchange unit linked to the Realm's dollar; CROWN → CN: the Realm's existing silver loans stay in silver; a reserve compact of its own in San Francisco on the terms the other holders have; JP → CN: signs with the Crown of Pacifica as one party, the Concord

### The chartered estates (Polytechnic, Office of Public Measures and others)

- **The Patrimony (its lending arm at home, the Development Fund)** (charter, 1880–, §19, APPROVED, CR-057)
- **Charter of the Light and Matter Works** (charter, 1883–, §19, PROPOSED, CR-049) — owes: ESTATES → PAC: the works, the Western Slope mines and the source trade run as one state champion with the state holding the shares; the estate pays for the Polytechnic's laboratories; ESTATES → PAC: from 1932 the Works collects and handles all fuel delivered under Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia, carrying it away at its own cost, charging nothing and paying nothing (CR-086)
- **Constitution of the Realm** (constitution, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → HI: entrenched language and tenure guarantees for Hawaii as a historic land (no veto); PAC → COUNTIES: land taxes, roads, sanitation, primary schools and ordinary policing left to county boards; PAC → CAL: California a historic land: Spanish entrenched, community property, the Mexican grants as organic law (CR-071 G6.2)
- **Polytechnic charter (chartered estate)** (charter, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → ESTATES: the Polytechnic's examinations, audit, General Staff and telegraph — can end or refuse: CROWN: the ruler chooses the chief from three graduate-council nominees; removal only with Senate consent
- **Truce of Thalassia** (pact, 1887–, §22, CANON) — owes: INLAND → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; NAVAL → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; ESTATES → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; CROWN → INLAND: life offices for the named holders; the factions meet through the Crown
- **Polytechnic headship at Carquinez (Truce life office)** (office, 1887–, §22, CANON) — can end or refuse: CROWN: after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent
- **Unwritten arrangement between the Crown and the Five** (pact, 1887–, §24, APPROVED, CR-027) — owes: CROWN → ESTATES: does not touch their charters, appointments or the Development Fund; sanctions them; ESTATES → CROWN: never take sides against the Crown and never let a faction capture them
- **The press estate's charter** (charter, 1887–, §25, APPROVED, CR-079)
- **Office of Public Measures (independent estate)** (charter, 1888–, §21, CANON)
- **Hokkaido Cold-Hardy Rice Breeding Exchange** (compact, 1893–, §25, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: the Realm's stations cross cold-hardy rice with Hokkaido's and give winter nurseries in Hawaiʻi, which halves the breeding cycle; HOKKAIDO-AG → ESTATES: Hokkaido's test station and Sapporo Agricultural College share the crossing and its lines
- **The Wireless Act** (law, 1911–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078)
- **The election air-time act (amending the Wireless Act)** (law, 1913–, §25, APPROVED, CR-079)
- **The Huludao crop article** (charter, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → ESTATES: bonded elevators on the quay and long purchase contracts at prices posted on the Huludao exchange and published by the Customs; ESTATES → CN: the Columbia's ammonia at its published price and the Polytechnic's soybean seed at the one royalty, to any farmer and any buyer's agent
- **Brazilian monazite purchases** (concession, 1926–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: BR-MONAZITE → ESTATES: monazite sand on ordinary commercial contracts; ESTATES → BR-MONAZITE: the commercial price
- **Urgeiriça: the dumps and a short concentrate contract** (concession, 1926–1929, §33, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: PT-URGEIRICA → ESTATES: the dumps of the idle mine and its concentrate on a short contract
- **Radium Hill: one purchase of ore and stock** (concession, 1926–1927, §33, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: SA-RADIUM → ESTATES: one purchase of ore and stock
- **Shinkolobwe: Belgium's revision of 1928–29** (concession, 1929–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: ESTATES → BE: a higher royalty and a larger free allowance of the salt for Belgium's hospitals; BE → ESTATES: all the output still goes to Pacifica; no share of the uranium for Belgium
- **The Patrimony board's prairie wheat contract** (concession, 1931–1941, §34, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CA-CSA → ESTATES: ten harvests, 1932 to 1941, 1.0 million tonnes a harvest inside a band of 0.8 to 1.2 million (MODEL), bought each autumn in the country elevators and delivered at Burrard Inlet; ESTATES → CA-CSA: a gold-dollar balance in San Francisco that the Agency owns, credited against each delivery ($18 a tonne, MODEL); carriage in Pacifican ships; no bid for the Agency's unsold stock of 1929–31 below the band
- **The Crown's granary and its ten-year contracts abroad** (charter, 1932–, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: ESTATES → COUNTIES: the home counties' wheat and feed bought first, at the slump's price
- **Pacifica–Hokkaido marine agronomy and kelp research compact** (compact, 1932–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: HOKKAIDO-AG → ESTATES: joint research on kombu and kelp farming with the Realm's marine stations; ESTATES → HOKKAIDO-AG: what the wartime plant at San Diego learned of harvesting kelp by machine

### The counties of the Realm

- **Constitution of the Realm** (constitution, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → HI: entrenched language and tenure guarantees for Hawaii as a historic land (no veto); PAC → COUNTIES: land taxes, roads, sanitation, primary schools and ordinary policing left to county boards; PAC → CAL: California a historic land: Spanish entrenched, community property, the Mexican grants as organic law (CR-071 G6.2)
- **The labour article of the Constitution of the Realm** (guarantee, 1887–, §21, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → COUNTIES: no labour bound by debt, penal contract or a broker's ticket; the same wage for the same work, whoever does it; a day of rest in seven, on the day of the worker's faith; the eight-hour day on the Realm's works and in its chartered undertakings (H-16, H7.2)
- **The compact lands under the Constitution of the Realm** (guarantee, 1887–, §21, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → NATIONS: the homelands' compacts carried as the Realm's own; a homeland's line, powers and grade are not organic law and change only by compact (I-8; CR-033 A22); PAC → NATIONS: one senator for each homeland of a million acres or more, chosen by its own council; one for each league of enclaves that reaches that size (I-70 to I-72); PAC → NATIONS: citizenship for a nation's member by registration, without the English test (K-24); COUNTIES → NATIONS: a county's board, taxes and courts stop at an enclave's line unless the nation contracts for a service (I4) — can end or refuse: NATIONS: a compact changes only with the consent of the nation affected and the Realm
- **Earned county autonomy (chartered status, receivership and programme sunset)** (law, c.1888–, §25, CANON) — owes: PAC → COUNTIES: chartered status after five consecutive years at the Office's four standards; measures grants follow results; COUNTIES → PAC: receivership permitted after three years below the standards or declared disorder — can end or refuse: PAC: a suspended board stays suspended up to five years, renewable by the Senate; PAC: county powers, boundaries and grades are organic law, amendable by both chambers without local consent
- **The labour law of 1888** (law, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **The Companies Act (the Pacific Companies made a system)** (law, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074)
- **The nations' ladder (land bought back and the chartered nation)** (law, c.1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → NATIONS: first refusal on a nation's own ceded land still in the Land Fund, at the price the Fund paid; additions to a homeland by supplementary compact; PAC → COUNTIES: the land tax lost to a homeland's addition, paid for ten years from the Land Fund's margin; PAC → NATIONS: for a chartered nation (five years at the Office's standard): its own trusteeship and the lease fee, a patrimony on the Patrimony's rules, chartered companies and home borrowing with Treasury consent, a court of first instance and police, and its language certified for the tongue vote
- **Salt Lake's county charter (the first chartered county)** (charter, 1894–, §25, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **Thalassia Plan** (law, 1928–, §33, CANON) — owes: COUNTIES → HI: public land stays leasehold; county administration of leases does not transfer ownership or the ʻAha Aliʻi's custody of Hawaiian lands; CROWN → HI: on Thalassia's Hawaiian Crown ground the Mōʻī receives the premiums, which fund parks, transit and schools
- **The Crown's granary and its ten-year contracts abroad** (charter, 1932–, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: ESTATES → COUNTIES: the home counties' wheat and feed bought first, at the slump's price

### State of California as a member of the Confederation (member government and legislature, from 1856)

- **Articles of the Confederation of Pacifica** (compact, 1856–1887, §8, CANON) — owes: CAL → PAC: defense, the navy, customs, currency and external relations to the common institutions; HI → PAC: defense, the navy, customs, currency and external relations to the common institutions; PAC → HI: domestic self-government and residual powers; Hawaiian-language institutions; Honolulu remains the Hawaiian seat; PAC → CAL: domestic self-government and residual powers; English–Spanish guarantees — can end or refuse: CAL: ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change; HI: ratification by the founding state required for any constitutional change
- **Article XIV (emergency Protector)** (constitution, 1856–, §8, CANON) — can end or refuse: CAL: member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal; HI: member government's formally recorded assent required for appointment; legislature required for renewal (withheld in 1860); PAC: Council two-thirds vote to appoint; the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures
- **Protectorship of Elias Thorne** (office, 1861–1865, §9, CANON) — renewals: 1862, 1863, 1864 — can end or refuse: CAL: renewal requires the member legislature; HI: renewal requires the member legislature; PAC: the Confederal Assembly can revoke emergency measures
- **Enabling Law** (law, 1865–1887, §12, CANON)
- **The Militia Act** (law, 1882–, §19, APPROVED, CR-071)
- **Constitution of the Realm** (constitution, 1887–, §21, CANON) — owes: PAC → HI: entrenched language and tenure guarantees for Hawaii as a historic land (no veto); PAC → COUNTIES: land taxes, roads, sanitation, primary schools and ordinary policing left to county boards; PAC → CAL: California a historic land: Spanish entrenched, community property, the Mexican grants as organic law (CR-071 G6.2)

### Suisun people under Sem-Yeto (Solano)

- **Separate compact with Solano (Sem-Yeto)** (compact, 1846–, §4, CANON)

### The Miwok guide's people

- **Elias's promise to the Miwok guide** (pact, 1847–, §5, PROPOSED, CR-028) — owes: MIWOK → PAC: the guide keeps the secret of the October 1847 gold find; PAC → MIWOK: Elias's promise that the Republic will protect a homeland for his people when the miners come

### Deseret (Mormon Salt Lake state)

- **Association of Deseret: the working articles of 1862 and the Compact of Association of 1863** (compact, 1862–1867, §10, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → DESERET: the Protector's pledge that the Union's law will not be enforced in Deseret by arms; PAC → DESERET: published terms of passage; Deseret's courts and church property left alone; Deseret's wheat bought in gold; PAC → DESERET: the garrison's posts named at the old Camp Floyd, the Bear River crossing and Fort Bridger, none within a day's ride of the city; DESERET → PAC: supply of the road and passage to the interior campaigns; Lot Smith's cavalry and the Nauvoo Legion bound to the common defence of the roads and the valley; PAC → DESERET: a confederal travellers' court for crimes on the roads, extended by the Compact to crimes of any date
- **Spanish Fork agreement with Black Hawk's Utes** (pact, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → BHUTES: cattle and flour each year for the grazing the settlements took; homelands they will not be moved from; PAC → BHUTES: what was done that spring is forgiven on both sides; killings from that day go to the confederal court
- **Deseret's charter of accession** (charter, 1866–1887, §12, PROPOSED, CR-060) — owes: PAC → DESERET: Deseret's marriage and church-property law protected under the confederal floor; DESERET → PAC: the Nauvoo Legion placed within the Defense Law
- **The Militia Act** (law, 1882–, §19, APPROVED, CR-071)

### The Navajo (Diné) nation

- **Cubero agreement with the Navajo** (pact, 1863–, §10, APPROVED, CR-061) — owes: PAC → NAVAJO: the towns' slaving expeditions forbidden; NAVAJO → PAC: the raids stop
- **Navajo homeland treaty** (treaty, 1865–, §10, APPROVED, CR-015) — owes: PAC → NAVAJO: recognition of a homeland; no Long Walk; PAC → NAVAJO: the return of the Navajo held in New Mexican households: grown captives choose, a joint commission decides for the children, and Pacifica pays each household that releases one
- **The Hopi agreement (the villages' homeland and the Hopi article of the Navajo treaty)** (compact, 1865–, §12, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: PAC → HOPI: the mesas, fields, springs and grazing entered in the register as the villages' homeland, on a line of named places, with Moenkopi a detached part; no grant or Land Fund sale inside it (HP2, HP6); NAVAJO → HOPI: Diné families inside the line stay only by the village's leave (HP3); PAC → HOPI: passage to shrines, eagle grounds, salt and the San Francisco Peaks (HP4); no agent, school or mission placed on the mesas unless a village asks (HP5)

### The Apache peoples

- **Apache peace** (treaty, 1865–, §12, PROPOSED) — owes: PAC → APACHE: homeland reservations, honest agents and prosecution of settler massacres; PAC → APACHE: paid scouts; PAC → APACHE: passage money for the Chiricahua from the Sunset road's customs
- **Santa Rita del Cobre copper lease** (lease, 1865–, §12, APPROVED, CR-061) — owes: APACHE → PAC: the old copper mine at Santa Rita del Cobre; PAC → APACHE: a royalty to the Chihenne
- **Mining leases in the Apache and Chiricahua countries (Globe, Clifton, Bisbee)** (lease, c.1872–, §13, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: APACHE → PAC: the Globe and Clifton districts inside the White Mountain line, and Bisbee's copper inside the Chiricahua line, on lease; PAC → APACHE: a royalty to each nation, as Santa Rita pays the Chihenne (C-1865-SANTA-RITA)

### The Native nations of California that sign the treaties of 1851–52

- **Treaties with the Native nations of California (the compact of protection and pre-emption)** (compact, 1851–, §5, APPROVED, CR-033) — owes: PAC → CANATIONS: homelands reserved to each nation, never sold and leased only through the Republic, which keeps a fee; PAC → CANATIONS: annuities of goods for ceded land, paid from the Land Fund; CANATIONS → PAC: land beyond the homelands sold only to the Republic, the sole lawful buyer; PAC → CANATIONS: the spirits article (CR-076): no spirits enter a homeland except as the nation's council decides; no annuity, price, wage or debt paid in spirits; a trader who breaks it loses licence and bond and answers in the Republic's court on the nation's complaint

### Samoa (Malietoa and the Samoan chiefs)

- **1878 Pago Pago treaty** (treaty, 1878–, §16, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: SAMOA → PAC: Pago Pago (terms not stated), signed by Hawaii on the Confederation's behalf
- **Berlin Act (Samoa)** (treaty, 1889–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029) — owes: DE → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; UK → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power; PAC → SAMOA: protection as a protecting power
- **Partition of Samoa** (settlement, 1899–, §25, APPROVED, CR-029)

### Colombia

- **Colombian transit treaty** (treaty, 1880–, §19, CANON) — owes: CO → PAC: transit and neutrality rights, first refusal on the concession and control of the Panama Railroad
- **The Caribbean understanding** (settlement, 1921–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → US: Venezuela stays the Union's source; Pacifica holds only its Lago minority; US → GULF: no Union naval station on the Caribbean mainland; PAC → GULF: no Pacifican base on the Caribbean mainland; canal rights are not bases

### The inland command and the American military aristocrats (Silas)

- **Truce of Thalassia** (pact, 1887–, §22, CANON) — owes: INLAND → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; NAVAL → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; ESTATES → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; CROWN → INLAND: life offices for the named holders; the factions meet through the Crown
- **Inland command at Sacramento (Truce life office)** (office, 1887–, §22, CANON) — can end or refuse: CROWN: after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent
- **The Companies Act (the Pacific Companies made a system)** (law, 1888–, §25, APPROVED, CR-074)

### The Japanese naval party (Kiyoshi)

- **Truce of Thalassia** (pact, 1887–, §22, CANON) — owes: INLAND → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; NAVAL → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; ESTATES → CROWN: accept Victoria's regency and her adopted son as successor; CROWN → INLAND: life offices for the named holders; the factions meet through the Crown
- **Japanese naval party at Pearl Harbor (Truce life office)** (office, 1887–, §22, CANON) — can end or refuse: CROWN: after the holder's life the Regent appoints with Senate consent
- **Compact with Tokyo** (compact, 1887–1927, §24, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → PAC: backs the regency and holds its officers to the agreement; PAC → JP: guarantee against Russia and support for treaty revision with Europe; PAC → NAVAL: command of the western squadron reserved to officers of the Japanese naval party — renewals: 1897, 1907, 1917 — can end or refuse: JP: ratifies each ten-year renewal; PAC: ratifies each ten-year renewal

- **War bonds in coin and the agency of 1867** (loan, 1862–, §10, PROPOSED, CR-069) — owes: SANFRANCISCO → PAC: the Republic's war bonds taken in coin from the houses' reserves, brought by Ralston; PAC → SANFRANCISCO: interest on the coin bonds (amount OPEN); PAC → EASTHOUSES: from 1867, the loan the Senate declined, lent privately through Ralston's bank as the Republic's agent, which takes a share of its own
- **Japan's loan of 1873 (its San Francisco part)** (loan, 1873–, §14, APPROVED, CR-071) — owes: JP → PAC: interest and repayment on the Republic's share of the part placed in San Francisco; JP → SANFRANCISCO: interest and repayment on the houses' share, Japan's bank being the Bank of California (CR-073 E2, the 1867 precedent)

- **Japanese war loans raised in San Francisco** (loan, c.1904–, §26, PROPOSED) — owes: JP → SANFRANCISCO: service of the war loans Takahashi raises
- **Pacifican bonds for the Shanghai Settlement's power plant** (loan, 1929–, §34, APPROVED, CR-081) — owes: CN → SANFRANCISCO: service of the bonds by the Chinese municipal company

- **Gulf Republic's conversion of its Confederate debt share** (loan, 1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-048) — owes: GULF → CSA-BONDHOLDERS: service of the converted loan — secured on: Spindletop oil royalties

- **Great Bear Lake: the Works' exclusive contract** (concession, 1912–1982, §27, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: CA → PAC: a Canadian-chartered company sells the Works all its ore under an exclusive contract; PAC → CA: finances the company; a free allowance of the salt for Canada's hospitals; the Works' ore-handling code; PAC → CA: from 1932, a higher royalty (rate OPEN)
- **The Trilateral Treaty of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CLD → CA: perpetual running rights for the Canadian Pacific across Caledonia, a bonded Canadian quay on Burrard Inlet and transit at Prince Rupert, entrenched against revocation; CA → CROWN: a yearly quota of Pacifican farm and industrial machines outside the emergency tariff (size OPEN), open to both partners' champions; CA → CROWN: the Great Bear contract given a term of fifty years from 1932 (C-1912-GREATBEAR-OFFTAKE); CROWN → CA: a higher royalty on Great Bear's ore (rate OPEN); CA → CLD: withdrawal of Macdonald's reservation against the line of 1870
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate

- **Guarantee of the dominions' crop** (guarantee, 1931–, §37, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → NZ: guarantee of the crop; PAC → NZ: from the treaty of 1932, guarantee that no hostile base stands on its soil
- **British Pacific Retrenchment Treaty** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: UK → CROWN: ends for good the fortification of Singapore for capital ships and the heavy dry-dock work at Sembawang; a peacetime cap on its capital ships east of Ceylon (cruisers and sloops unrestricted); UK → JP: the same halt and cap, the Concord signing for both crowns; CROWN → UK: a maritime pledge of non-aggression toward Malaya and its sea lanes, covering beyond that only attack from the sea by an outside power on the Strait of Malacca and on the commerce of Malaya and the Indian Ocean; never order inside the colonies; JP → UK: the same pledge; PAC → UK: the Realm's own countersignature on the pledge (two-thirds of the Senate); UK → CROWN: retrocession of Labuan to Brunei subject to Labuan's vote, and consent to the Labuan Harbour Lease; detachment of Fanning and Washington into Ka Lōkahi's trust, the Fanning cable station leased back; quitclaim of its remaining claims in the Phoenix group; release of Tonga from the protectorate of 1900; CROWN → AU: protocol: no warship stationed and no new base built south of 10° S between Timor and 155° W (meridian PROPOSED), none sought in Portuguese Timor; no naval fortification or permanently berthed warship at the leased ports of Bintan and East Borneo; CROWN → NZ: the same protocol; JP → AU: the same protocol, which Japan signs as a crown of the Concord; UK → NZ: PROPOSED: New Zealand's contribution to the Singapore base rebated or credited against its war debt to Britain (sum OPEN); PAC → UK: eleven of the seventeen cancelled annuities of C-1923-UK-FUNDING fall on the deposit of the King's ratification of this treaty (MODEL) — can end or refuse: UK: reverter: the halt and the cap fall away on Britain's formal notice if the Concord is dissolved, or on a finding of breach by the arbitration court at The Hague (redrafted from 'lapses' when Hayama strikes the Concord's term in June 1932; the redraft is PROPOSED)

### The Akimel O'odham of the Salt and the Gila

- **The storage compacts of 1900–1920** (compact, c.1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → AKIMEL: their share of the Salt; PAC → PYRAMID: their lake's flow from the Truckee; PAC → PUEBLOS: a compact of their own on the Rio Grande

### The Pyramid Lake Paiute

- **The storage compacts of 1900–1920** (compact, c.1900–, §25, APPROVED, CR-082) — owes: PAC → AKIMEL: their share of the Salt; PAC → PYRAMID: their lake's flow from the Truckee; PAC → PUEBLOS: a compact of their own on the Rio Grande

### Colony and Dominion of British Columbia, with the Yukon (1858–1932)

- **The Confederation's line through British Columbia** (concession, c.1868–1897, §14, APPROVED, CR-070) — owes: BCCO → PAC: Western Union's line in British Columbia, held for the Confederation by a company of the colony
- **The Alaskan rivers free forever to British Columbia** (treaty, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: navigation of the Stikine, the Yukon and the Porcupine through Alaska, free forever
- **Pacifica's loan, the Puget Sound rail link and free entry for British Columbia** (loan, 1871–, §13, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: a railway south to Puget Sound, a loan, and free entry for the colony's coal, timber and fish; BC → PAC: service of the loan
- **The four-power fur-seal convention** (convention, 1886–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: fifteen percent of the Pribilof catch; RU → BC: fifteen percent of the Commander Islands' catch, 1886–1921; PAC → BC: from 1921, with the Commander Islands, fifteen percent of their catch; JP → BC: ten percent of Robben Island's catch; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the dominion's shares, by succession under the Association Treaty
- **Sale of the British Columbia line to British Columbia** (settlement, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → BC: the old line through the colony, as the first half of the colony's own wire to the Yukon
- **The western-waters treaty and its joint commission** (treaty, 1906–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: a joint commission of equals with powers to regulate as well as investigate on the Columbia, Kootenay, Okanagan, Similkameen, Skagit, Pend Oreille, the Fraser's salmon and the Alaskan rivers; no upstream-sovereignty doctrine; PAC → CLD: from Proclamation Day in 1932, the same under a water board of Pacifica and Caledonia
- **The Pacific Halibut Treaty** (treaty, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-083)
- **The Columbia storage compact** (compact, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: BC → PAC: storage on the upper Columbia (the Arrow Lakes, Kootenay Lake, later Kinbasket); PAC → BC: half of the extra downstream power, paid in power and sold back to the Realm's grid for gold dollars, with payments for flood control; no dam flooding across the line without BC's consent and compensation; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the same half under a power compact between Pacifica and Caledonia, pledged to the bond refunding
- **Westminster's Recognition and Severance Act and the Bamfield cable lease** (settlement, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: UK → CLD: recognition and severance by Act of Parliament on the legislature's request and consent; in force by a commencement order (about 15 July 1932); PAC → UK: two annuities of the debt funded in 1923 cancelled from the near end of the schedule (C-1923-UK-FUNDING; the first of three tranches); PAC → IIC: guarantee of a long commercial lease of the Bamfield Pacific Cable station; CLD → UK: the Island's entrenched guarantees: Victoria the capital, the Island's provincial autonomy, Crown pensions protected, and a two-year term for keeping British nationality (the term PROPOSED)

### Republic of Caledonia (from 1932)

- **The four-power fur-seal convention** (convention, 1886–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: fifteen percent of the Pribilof catch; RU → BC: fifteen percent of the Commander Islands' catch, 1886–1921; PAC → BC: from 1921, with the Commander Islands, fifteen percent of their catch; JP → BC: ten percent of Robben Island's catch; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the dominion's shares, by succession under the Association Treaty
- **The western-waters treaty and its joint commission** (treaty, 1906–, §25, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: PAC → BC: a joint commission of equals with powers to regulate as well as investigate on the Columbia, Kootenay, Okanagan, Similkameen, Skagit, Pend Oreille, the Fraser's salmon and the Alaskan rivers; no upstream-sovereignty doctrine; PAC → CLD: from Proclamation Day in 1932, the same under a water board of Pacifica and Caledonia
- **The Pacific Halibut Treaty** (treaty, 1923–, §33, APPROVED, CR-083)
- **The Columbia storage compact** (compact, 1924–, §33, APPROVED, CR-058) — owes: BC → PAC: storage on the upper Columbia (the Arrow Lakes, Kootenay Lake, later Kinbasket); PAC → BC: half of the extra downstream power, paid in power and sold back to the Realm's grid for gold dollars, with payments for flood control; no dam flooding across the line without BC's consent and compensation; PAC → CLD: from 1932, the same half under a power compact between Pacifica and Caledonia, pledged to the bond refunding
- **The First Nations compacts of Caledonia and the Fraser River salmon clause** (compact, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CLD → CALEDONIA-NATIONS: no land granted and no dam, mine or railway built on a nation's unceded country without its compact; annual rents and power royalties; published fishery remedies; no dam on the Fraser's main stem; CLD → CALEDONIA-NATIONS: at Burrard: the Kitsilano and Capilano reserve lands restored, rent on the harbour works from Proclamation Day, a seat on the harbour board; CALEDONIA-NATIONS → CLD: leases, never sales, of the ground the compacts name — can end or refuse: CALEDONIA-NATIONS: a nation may refuse to lease or sign, and the standstill rule then holds on its ground; only the nations of the river can reopen the Fraser clause
- **Westminster's Recognition and Severance Act and the Bamfield cable lease** (settlement, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: UK → CLD: recognition and severance by Act of Parliament on the legislature's request and consent; in force by a commencement order (about 15 July 1932); PAC → UK: two annuities of the debt funded in 1923 cancelled from the near end of the schedule (C-1923-UK-FUNDING; the first of three tranches); PAC → IIC: guarantee of a long commercial lease of the Bamfield Pacific Cable station; CLD → UK: the Island's entrenched guarantees: Victoria the capital, the Island's provincial autonomy, Crown pensions protected, and a two-year term for keeping British nationality (the term PROPOSED)
- **The Pacifica–Caledonia Association Treaty, its admission clause and the bond refunding** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: PAC → CLD: gold-dollar reserve clearing in San Francisco; entry and residence for Caledonians in the Realm under its ordinary law; CLD → PAC: chartered industrial districts at Burrard Inlet, Prince Rupert and Trail; entry and residence for the Realm's citizens; a perpetual corridor to Alaska for rail, road and cable; hydroelectricity exported south; CLD → PAC: succession to the dominion's seal, western-waters, Columbia and halibut treaties; CLD → PAC: service of the refunded railway bonds to private San Francisco syndicates, secured on flowing revenue — secured on: for the refunding: Caledonia's half of the Columbia's downstream power, the port tolls of Burrard Inlet and Prince Rupert, new contracts for exported power, and the royalties of the Yukon and the Kootenays — can end or refuse: PAC: admission is considered only after twenty-five consecutive years of measured outcomes; Pacifica may demand a second generation; admission must add neither risk nor a veto; both peoples must vote to unite
- **The Concord's external guarantee of Caledonia** (guarantee, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CROWN → CLD: external guarantee of Caledonia, kept by the common Navy; JP → CLD: the same guarantee, as the Concord's other crown; CLD → JP: entry and residence for Japanese subjects, reciprocal with Caledonians' in Japan; the floors kept for those resident on Proclamation Day who stay Japanese; no vote by the clause; CLD → CROWN: the repair contract at Esquimalt (kept with the Caledonian yard) and the Alaska corridor's naval clauses; dockyards open to the common Navy
- **The Trilateral Treaty of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-083) — owes: CLD → CA: perpetual running rights for the Canadian Pacific across Caledonia, a bonded Canadian quay on Burrard Inlet and transit at Prince Rupert, entrenched against revocation; CA → CROWN: a yearly quota of Pacifican farm and industrial machines outside the emergency tariff (size OPEN), open to both partners' champions; CA → CROWN: the Great Bear contract given a term of fifty years from 1932 (C-1912-GREATBEAR-OFFTAKE); CROWN → CA: a higher royalty on Great Bear's ore (rate OPEN); CA → CLD: withdrawal of Macdonald's reservation against the line of 1870
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act
- **Article I of the Final Act of Thalassia: the renunciation and the Realm's pledge** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: UK → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; its one separating plant dismantled before the court's observer (PROPOSED); US → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; DE → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; it keeps the right to build piles for power; CA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; ANZ → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works; CN → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CLD → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); MX → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); GULF → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); CSA → CROWN: no separating plant, no test, all irradiated fuel delivered to the Works (signature PROPOSED); PAC → UK: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → US: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → DE: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → ANZ: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CN: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CLD: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → MX: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → GULF: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; PAC → CSA: the weapon is never used against a signatory that keeps the article; JP → PAC: the Emperor's countersignature of the pledge as an act of the Concord; PAC → CROWN: the Works carries the fuel away at its own cost, charges nothing and pays nothing; the Realm discloses nothing and is not inspected (PROPOSED) — can end or refuse: PAC: cannot declare the pledge lapsed; the pledge ends toward a signatory only when the Hague Arbitration Court has found it in breach, and it holds in war as in peace
- **Article II of the Final Act of Thalassia: the food order** (treaty, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: CROWN → UK: in peace: no embargo; the scheduled contracts honoured; neither food, its means nor credit for them made conditional on a concession; the means and the scheduled passage kept open at published prices; CROWN → US: the same four undertakings; CROWN → DE: the same four undertakings; CROWN → FR: the same four undertakings, given although France reserves on Article I; CROWN → CN: the same four undertakings; the wheat credit of 1931 and the soybean compact scheduled; CROWN → CA: the same four undertakings; the prairie wheat contract scheduled; CROWN → ANZ: the same four undertakings; New Zealand's crop guarantee of 1931 scheduled; CROWN → CLD: the same four undertakings; CROWN → MX: the same four undertakings; CROWN → GULF: the same four undertakings; the provisioning compact of 1928 scheduled; CROWN → CSA: the same four undertakings; JP → CROWN: signs as the Concord's other partner and as a guarantor, being fed by its own crop; PAC → CROWN: the Realm's own guarantee of the article, voted by two-thirds of the Senate

### Kingdom of the Netherlands and the Netherlands East Indies

- **Batavia Compact** (compact, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: NL → JP: two fenced commercial loading ports leased for fifty years (bauxite on Bintan Island; oil in East Borneo), and open-door concessions on published royalties in unallocated East Borneo oil blocks and Bintan's bauxite (Sulawesi's nickel PROPOSED); NL → CROWN: the same leases and concessions, made with the Concord for both crowns; CROWN → NL: no annexation; respect for Dutch sovereignty outside the leased fences; no policing inside the colony; no naval fortification and no warship permanently berthed at the leased ports; JP → NL: the same; inside the fences only unarmed watchmen of the Japanese companies, with Dutch field police at the gate; NL → SHELL: Shell's fields and refineries at Tarakan, Balikpapan and in South Sumatra stay exactly as they are; NL → CROWN: PROPOSED: the Sunda, Lombok and Makassar straits and the Dutch shore of Malacca kept open to peaceful commerce, the clause scheduled to the second article of the Final Act
- **Final Act of Thalassia** (treaty, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: FR → CROWN: its signature under the Act and Article II, with a reservation on Article I; NL → CROWN: its signature under the Act's schedule for its straits only; MX → CROWN: its signature under both articles, with a reservation keeping the Guaymas charter out of the Act

- **Labuan Harbour Lease and the Brunei auction accord** (lease, 1932–, §37, APPROVED, CR-084) — owes: BN → CROWN: lease of Labuan's harbour to the Concord as a station of the common Navy on its ordinary establishment; BN → JP: the same lease: Japanese ships of the common Navy use the harbour by right, with no squadron and no commandant reserved to Japan; BN → LABUAN: municipal self-government, the option of keeping British nationality and employment in the port, as the published terms of the vote guarantee; BN → SHELL: PROPOSED: the existing lease at Seria honoured as it stands, at its old royalty; new blocks let at public auction by the Resident's administration

### Tokyo Electric Light Company (Tokyo Dentō) and its syndicate of Japanese utility bonds

- **Tokyo Atomic Power Station Split Financing Agreement** (compact, 1928–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-085) — owes: TOKYO-DENTO → JP: finances and owns the site, the civil containment, the cooling works, the turbines, the alternators and the grid ties, all paid in yen; PAC → TOKYO-DENTO: the Works supplies the pile, the purified graphite, the control gear and the uranium charge; TOKYO-DENTO → PAC: the Works' outlay repaid through a lease of fuel and core charged by the kilowatt-hour for twenty years from first power (C-1931-TOKYO-FUEL-LEASE)
- **Fuel lease for the Tokyo atomic station** (lease, 1931–1951, §33, APPROVED, CR-050) — owes: PAC → JP: fuel for the Tokyo station, leased from the Light and Matter Works; JP → PAC: the spent fuel, returned to Pacifica; TOKYO-DENTO → PAC: the lease of fuel and core, charged by the kilowatt-hour for twenty years from first power — can end or refuse: PAC: the Works' duty to deliver fresh cores ends if the union is ever dissolved; all irradiated fuel still returns
- **Concord Atomic Power Board (charter)** (charter, 1932–, §33, APPROVED, CR-085) — owes: PAC → JP: the Board builds and runs the stations in Japan and builds the Kansai station; a published tariff formula indexed only to Tokyo's cost of generating from delivered steam coal, less an agreed discount; JP → PAC: no plant in Japan for treating irradiated fuel; every station's spent fuel returns to the Works; KANSAI-UTILITIES → PAC: the Kansai station's civil works and turbines owned in yen; the pile and the fuel leased from the Works

- **The warnings compact (earthquakes at sea and typhoons)** (compact, 1897–, §25, APPROVED, CR-078) — owes: PAC → JP: typhoon warnings: the Manila Observatory, with Guam and Chichijima, wires the storm tracks north to Japan; JP → PAC: warnings of offshore earthquakes and tide-gauge readings, wired outward to Honolulu by Ōmori Fusakichi and the Central Meteorological Observatory, by the southern wire and from 1903 by the Kuril cable

### Pacifica's private grain houses (merchants, carriers and lenders on the Pampas)

- **The private houses' Pampas contracts** (concession, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: GRAIN-HOUSES → AR-EXPORTERS: carriage, finance and insurance of Argentina's wheat, maize and linseed to third markets; the Columbia's nitrogen and the champions' machines sold to it; PAC → GRAIN-HOUSES: the contracts are scheduled to Article II as contracts the Realm will not itself interrupt; the Realm puts no money behind them

### The grain exporters of Buenos Aires and the Pampas

- **The private houses' Pampas contracts** (concession, 1932–, §37, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: GRAIN-HOUSES → AR-EXPORTERS: carriage, finance and insurance of Argentina's wheat, maize and linseed to third markets; the Columbia's nitrogen and the champions' machines sold to it; PAC → GRAIN-HOUSES: the contracts are scheduled to Article II as contracts the Realm will not itself interrupt; the Realm puts no money behind them

### The monazite sellers of Bahia and Espírito Santo

- **Brazilian monazite purchases** (concession, 1926–, §33, PROPOSED, CR-086) — owes: BR-MONAZITE → ESTATES: monazite sand on ordinary commercial contracts; ESTATES → BR-MONAZITE: the commercial price

### The owners of the Urgeiriça mine before August 1929

- **Urgeiriça: the dumps and a short concentrate contract** (concession, 1926–1929, §33, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: PT-URGEIRICA → ESTATES: the dumps of the idle mine and its concentrate on a short contract

### The Radium Hill mining companies of South Australia

- **Radium Hill: one purchase of ore and stock** (concession, 1926–1927, §33, APPROVED, CR-086) — owes: SA-RADIUM → ESTATES: one purchase of ore and stock
